## I. The Crime / 第一章 罪案


===Original===
The telephone bell rang on the table of Superintendent Sinclair at Scotland Yard. He was a busy man, and had given orders that he was not to be disturbed except on matters important.

Putting down a paper he had been reading, he picked up the receiver. A woman’s voice spoke.

“Is that Scotland Yard?”

“Yes, yes,” he said impatiently, “Superintendent Sinclair speaking, what is it?”

“Listen carefully,” said the voice. “The Home Secretary has been murdered at his own house, it would be as well if you would come at once. Have you got that? Just repeat.”

===Chinese===
苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）辛克莱警司（Superintendent Sinclair）桌上的电话铃响了。他是个忙人，早已下过命令：除非要事，不得打扰。

他放下正在读的文件，拿起听筒。一个女人的声音开口了。

“是苏格兰场吗？”

“是的，是的，”他不耐烦地说，“我是辛克莱警司，什么事？”

“请仔细听好，”那声音说。“内政大臣（Home Secretary）在自己家里被人谋杀了，你最好马上过来。听明白了吗？请复述一遍。”


===Original===
Even Sinclair, the coolest head in the service, was staggered for a moment. There was not a trace of hurry or emotion in the voice. It might have been inviting him to tea. Before he could collect himself, the voice began again.

“I will repeat,” and the same impassive message came through with the concluding words, “Have you got that?”

Sinclair pulled himself together.

“Who is speaking?” he said. He heard a laugh and then the voice﻿—

“Oh, no one in particular, just the murderer,” and then silence.

===Chinese===
就连辛克莱这个警界最冷静的人，也有片刻发懵。那声音里没有一丝急促或激动的痕迹，仿佛是在请他去喝茶似的。他还没回过神来，那声音又响了起来。

“我再说一遍，”同样无动于衷的通报传了过来，末了又是那句，“听明白了吗？”

辛克莱定了定神。

“你是谁？”他说。他听到一声轻笑，接着那声音说——

“哦，不是什么特别的人，就是凶手罢了。”随后是一片寂静。


===Original===
He rang his bell, and his assistant, or “familiar” as he was termed, Lewis, entered.

“Someone is playing a joke of sorts on us. Just find out who called up,” he said abruptly, and went on reading. The thing was so absurd, but something was wrong, and someone would have to answer for this. In a minute Lewis returned.

“They don’t seem to know downstairs, sir, there is a new operator at the exchange, and it seems that someone said she was a personal friend of yours, and must speak at once to you.”

“Oh, of course, the same old game. I suppose they think it’s funny,” and he turned savagely to his work.

“By the way, Lewis, just find out where the Home Secretary is,” he added.

===Chinese===
他按了铃，他的助手——用行话说是他的“亲随”——刘易斯（Lewis）走了进来。

“有人在跟我们开什么玩笑。去查一下刚才是谁打来的。”他没头没脑地说完，接着看他的文件。这事荒唐透顶，可总觉得不对劲，总得有人对此负责。不一会儿刘易斯回来了。

“楼下的人好像说不清楚，长官，电话交换台新来了个接线员，好像是有人说她自己是您的私人朋友，必须立刻跟您通话。”

“哦，当然了，还是那套老把戏。他们大概觉得这样很好玩。”他气冲冲地转回去做他的事。

“对了，刘易斯，去查一下内政大臣现在在哪儿。”他又补了一句。


===Original===
About ten minutes had passed, when a knock came at the door, and a clerk ushered in Mr. Collins.

Sylvester Collins was not a Sherlock Holmes or anything like it, but after a successful career at the Bar, at a time when all his many friends had expected him to “take silk,” he had suddenly thrown up his whole career, and started as an Inquiry Agent and Amateur Detective, though he hated the expression, and always claimed that he was merely trying to use his experience at the Bar in a practical way.

However, he had been phenomenally successful, perhaps through luck, perhaps through a keen, trained brain and good common sense.

If his friends wanted to upset him, they would call him Sherlock Holmes, which was like a red rag to a bull to him.

He worked excellently with the official force, and had been “briefed” by them on many occasions, with the happiest results to all except the criminals who had been run to earth.

===Chinese===
大约十分钟过去了，有人敲门，一名文书把科林斯先生（Mr. Collins）引了进来。

西尔维斯特·科林斯（Sylvester Collins）并非歇洛克·福尔摩斯，也根本不是那一类人物，但他原本在律师界前程似锦，正当众多朋友都指望他“受任御用大律师”之时，他却突然抛弃了整个事业，改行做起了调查代理人兼业余侦探——尽管他厌恶这个称呼，总是声称自己不过是想把律师界的经验用于实务而已。

然而，他的成就惊人，也许是运气好，也许是因为一副敏锐、训练有素的头脑和良好的常识。

朋友们若想惹恼他，就管他叫歇洛克·福尔摩斯，这称呼对他就像斗牛士手里的红布。

他与官方警力合作得极为出色，多次受官方“委托”办案，结果皆大欢喜——只有被查获归案的罪犯除外。


===Original===
A clean-cut face with a large nose, and a firm mouth, were his chief characteristics. Soft brown eyes, and curly hair almost black, gave his face a curiously paradoxical expression.

When not engaged professionally, he was a keen sportsman, and enjoyed life to the full.

He was entirely devoid of “side” or “swank.”

Sinclair was a very different type. He was more like the Scotland Yard officer of real life than of fiction. After successful work in India, he had applied for and obtained his post. He had just a detective’s training and education. He made no pretensions to be other than a trained official with no particular brilliance, and he was glad to have the help of his friend, who had brains and not his experience.

Collins always came to Sinclair without ceremony.

===Chinese===
轮廓分明的脸、大鼻子和坚毅的嘴，是他的主要特征。柔和的棕色眼睛和近黑的鬈发，又给他的脸平添一种奇特的、自相矛盾的神情。

不办案的时候，他是个热衷运动的人，尽情享受生活。

他丝毫没有“架子”或“炫耀”之类的习气。

辛克莱则完全是另一路人。他更像现实生活中而非小说里的苏格兰场警官。在印度干出一番成绩之后，他申请并获得了如今的职位。他受过正规的侦探训练与教育，从不自命为别的什么，只当自己是名并无过人之才的、受过训练的官员；他很乐意有朋友相助——朋友有头脑，却没有他这样的经验。

科林斯来找辛克莱向来不讲客套。


===Original===
He entered smoking a cigarette, and placed his hat and stick on the table.

“Well,” he said. “What’s the trouble now?”

Sinclair looked up in some surprise.

“What do you mean?”

“You sent for me?”

“I’m sure I didn’t,” said the other.

===Chinese===
他叼着烟走进来，把帽子和手杖放在桌上。

“怎么样，”他说，“这回又是什么麻烦？”

辛克莱有些诧异地抬起头。

“你这话什么意思？”

“不是你叫我来的吗？”

“我肯定没有，”对方说。


===Original===
“But someone from here called me up on the phone about﻿—” he looked at his watch﻿—“about ten minutes ago, and said you wanted to speak to me.”

“Someone from here. Who was it?”

“I am sure I don’t know. It sounded like a woman.”

“What did she say?” said the Superintendent turning in his chair.

“Nothing more than that. Simply asked if I were speaking, and said ‘Superintendent Sinclair wants to speak to you at once if you can come,’ and rang off.”

“Well, I’m damned,” said Sinclair.

===Chinese===
“可是这里有人打电话给我，大概在——”他看了看表——“大概十分钟前，说你有话要对我讲。”

“这儿的人。是谁？”

“我确实不知道。听声音像是个女人。”

“她说了什么？”警司转过椅子问道。

“没有别的了。只是问接电话的是不是我，然后说‘辛克莱警司想马上同你通话，你要是能来就请过来’，接着就挂了。”

“嘿，活见鬼了。”辛克莱说。


===Original===
“You may be for all I know, but I was just off to tennis,” and he glanced at his flannels. “I suppose someone has been playing the fool. I’ll get off.”

“Stop. If they have, they have been trying to fool me, too,” and he told of the message he had received.

Collins listened with interest.

“What have you done?” he said.

“I asked Lewis to find out where the Home Secretary was. I expect he has found him now. The thing is absurd.”

===Chinese===
“你见鬼不见鬼我管不着，反正我正要去打网球。”他瞥了一眼自己的法兰绒运动裤。“我看是有人在胡闹。我走了。”

“站住。要是那样，他们也想耍我呢。”说着，他把接到的那通电话通报了一遍。

科林斯饶有兴味地听着。

“你采取了什么措施？”他问。

“我让刘易斯去查内政大臣在哪里。估计这会儿他已经查到了。这事太荒唐了。”


===Original===
Lewis came in.

“Well?” said Sinclair.

“The Home Secretary is not in the House or at the Home Office. They do not know where he is.”

“Call up his house,” said Sinclair, irritated.

“Better not,” said Collins. “If there’s nothing in it we don’t want to look fools, and if there has really been murder done the less known the better. I’ll tell you what﻿—I have my car outside. Let’s run up to his house in Leveson Square. You can make some excuse. You often want to see him.”

The Superintendent made a face. “I’m not big enough to go calling on the Home Secretary.”

===Chinese===
刘易斯进来了。

“怎么样？”辛克莱说。

“内政大臣既不在议会，也不在内政部（Home Office）。他们不知道他在哪儿。”

“打他家电话。”辛克莱烦躁地说。

“最好别打，”科林斯说，“如果纯属子虚乌有，咱们可不想出洋相；倘若真出了人命，知道的人越少越好。这么办——我的车就在外面，咱们跑一趟他在利文森广场（Leveson Square）的宅子。你随便找个借口，你本来就常要见他的。”

警司做了个苦脸。“我这级别还不够上门拜访内政大臣的。”


===Original===
“Never mind, fake up something. I’ll come with you.”

“All right, I’ll bring two plain clothes officers in case there is anything in it. We often have to keep a special watch there, so that’ll be quite in order.”

Collins laughed. “Thank goodness I am not official. What a lot of red tape you people have.”

“Why, what would you have done, then?”

“Charged up and asked him if he were dead by any chance.”

“Come along.”

===Chinese===
“别管那些，胡诌个理由就是。我陪你去。”

“好吧，我带上两名便衣，以防真有其事。那边我们常要特别布控，这样行事完全名正言顺。”

科林斯笑了。“幸亏我不是官方的人。你们这些人真是繁文缛节一大堆。”

“那你换作是我，会怎么办？”

“直闯进去，问他是不是碰巧已经死了。”

“走吧。”


===Original===
Lewis had been listening to them.

“Come along, Lewis, and bring Smith,” said Sinclair.

To his surprise Lewis was as white as chalk, and his hand trembled.

“If you don’t mind, sir,” he said, “I would much rather not come. I don’t feel very well.” Collins gazed keenly at him for a moment.

“How long have you felt ill?” he said.

“Only just a few minutes ago, sir, I think it’s the heat.”

===Chinese===
刘易斯一直在旁边听着。

“一起走吧，刘易斯，把史密斯（Smith）也带上。”辛克莱说。

令他吃惊的是，刘易斯脸色白得像粉笔，手也在发抖。

“您要是不介意，长官，”他说，“我实在不想去。我有点不舒服。”科林斯锐利地注视了他片刻。

“你不舒服有多久了？”他问。

“就刚才几分钟的事，长官，我想是天太热的缘故。”


===Original===
“Let’s get someone else, then, only hurry along, I want to get to my tennis,” said Collins impatiently.

Sinclair was about to grumble, but a look from Collins made him silent. “Go and get two men then at once. Tell them to meet us at the door.”

“What the devil was the matter with Lewis?” said Sinclair in the car. “He is my right-hand man.”

“Dunno,” said Collins who was driving, “wait till we know what has happened.”

Every incident that transpired from that moment was so stamped on the memory of the two men that there was no mistake about the facts.

===Chinese===
“那就另找个人吧，只是要快，我还赶着去打网球呢。”科林斯不耐烦地说。

辛克莱正要抱怨，科林斯一个眼色便让他住了口。“那就立刻去调两个人来，叫他们在门口等我们。”

“刘易斯到底怎么了？”辛克莱在车里问。“他可是我的左膀右臂。”

“不知道，”开车的科林斯说，“等弄清楚出了什么事再说。”

从那一刻起发生的每一件事，都深深刻在了两人的记忆里，事实因此绝无差错。


===Original===
On arriving at the door of the Home Secretary’s house, Sinclair stationed Smith at the front entrance, with orders not to show himself, but to watch.

The second man was disposed at the back, where was a high wall, but no actual entrance. The basement opened into an area in front.

The two men ascended the front steps and Sinclair rang the bell. An aged housekeeper, Mrs. Simmons, opened the door.

“Is the Home Secretary in?” said Sinclair.

“Yes, sir, he’s in his study,” she looked at the men doubtfully, “he did not wish to be disturbed.”

“When did you see him last?” said Collins, looking at her keenly.

===Chinese===
到了内政大臣宅邸门前，辛克莱派史密斯守住正门，命令他不要露面，只管盯住动静。

第二个人被安排在宅子后面，那里有一堵高墙，却没有真正的出入口。地下室朝前面的采光井开敞。

两人登上门前台阶，辛克莱按了门铃。开门的是位上了年纪的女管家西蒙斯太太（Mrs. Simmons）。

“内政大臣在家吗？”辛克莱问。

“在的，长官，他在书房里。”她狐疑地打量着两人，“他吩咐过不要打扰。”

“你最后一次见到他是什么时候？”科林斯目光锐利地看着她问。


===Original===
“Why, about half an hour back, sir,” said she in surprise.

“What fools we have been,” said Sinclair, “we’d better go.”

“Not at all. Now we are here we will see him. We can ask him about Blake who is to be hanged next Thursday. There’s a big petition you know for a reprieve.”

“Very good, but it’s a fool’s errand.” He turned to the housekeeper.

“Would you kindly take my card to Sir James, and ask him if he could spare me a minute?”

When the woman had gone, Sinclair said, “I shall get hell for this. He will ask me what it has got to do with me, and why I did not use the ordinary channels.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，约莫半个钟头前吧，长官。”她吃惊地说。

“咱们真是犯傻了，”辛克莱说，“还是走吧。”

“决不。既然来了，就见见他。可以问问布莱克（Blake）的事，他下星期四就要被处绞刑了，你知道的，现在有一份要求缓刑的大请愿书。”

“很好，可这趟纯属白跑。”他转向女管家。

“劳驾把我的名片递给詹姆斯爵士（Sir James），问问他能否赏我一分钟？”

女人走后，辛克莱说：“这回我可要挨骂了。他会问我这事与我何干，为何不走正常渠道。”


===Original===
“Leave it to me,” said the other with his easy confidence. He generally got his own way in most things.

After a brief interval Mrs. Simmons returned.

“I have knocked twice,” she said, “but there is no answer. I expect he is asleep. I hardly like to disturb him unless it is a very important matter.”

The two men exchanged glances.

“I am afraid it is,” said Collins. “We had better see. This is Superintendent Sinclair from Scotland Yard.”

At the name the old woman turned pale.

===Chinese===
“交给我吧。”对方带着一贯的从容自信说。他在大多数事情上总能如愿。

不多时，西蒙斯太太回来了。

“我敲了两回门，”她说，“可没人应。我猜他是睡着了。若不是要紧事，我实在不想惊动他。”

两人交换了一下眼色。

“恐怕正是要紧事，”科林斯说，“我们最好进去看看。这位是苏格兰场的辛克莱警司。”

听到这个名头，老太太的脸刷地白了。

===Original===
“Scotland Yard?” she stammered. “I hope nothing is wrong?”

“Why should anything be wrong,” said Collins. “The Home Secretary often sees officials from the Yard, doesn’t he?”

“Yes,” said she; “but there have been some queer things today here.”

“What things?” said Collins.

“Oh, come along, don’t start asking questions now,” said Sinclair. The two men entered the hall.

The housekeeper disappeared down the stairs, but the others did not notice her departure at the moment.

===Chinese===
“苏格兰场？”她结结巴巴地说，“但愿没出什么事吧？”

“能出什么事呢，”科林斯说，“内政大臣常接见苏格兰场的官员，不是吗？”

“是倒是，”她说，“可今天这宅子里出了些蹊跷事。”

“什么事？”科林斯问。

“哦，行了，这会儿先别问东问西。”辛克莱说。两人走进门厅。

女管家下楼去了，但两人当时并没有留意她离开。


===Original===
They made for the library door where the housekeeper had knocked. Sinclair tried the handle. The door was locked. He knocked loudly, but there was no response.

“We shall have to break the door down,” said he.

“Oh, that’s very clumsy,” said Collins, “and makes such a noise.” Stooping down he examined the lock.

“That’s an easy matter, the key is in the lock.”

He produced a fine pair of pliers, and deftly gripping the end of the key, turned it without difficulty.

===Chinese===
他们走向女管家敲过门的书房。辛克莱拧了拧把手，门锁着。他重重地敲门，里面毫无回应。

“看来只能破门了。”他说。

“哎呀，那也太笨了，”科林斯说，“而且闹出的动静太大。”他俯身查看门锁。

“好办得很，钥匙就插在锁眼里。”

他掏出一把细钳子，灵巧地夹住钥匙柄，毫不费力地一转。


===Original===
“You would make a good burglar,” laughed the superintendent. Collins opened the door and glanced round.

The room was in semidarkness, and after the glare outside it was hard to see anything for a moment. By the empty grate was a large armchair, and seated in this was the familiar figure of the Home Secretary, Sir James Watson. He was huddled up in his chair, and his head was at a curious angle to his body.

Sinclair was about to advance into the room.

“Stop,” said the other. “For Heaven’s sake don’t go inside and leave footmarks. Whatever is the matter, this requires a doctor. I will wait here, you telephone for a doctor.”

He glanced round the room.

“There doesn’t appear to be one here. Ask the housekeeper.”

===Chinese===
“你倒能当个出色的窃贼。”警司笑道。科林斯推开门，环顾四周。

房间里半明半暗，从外面的强光进来，一时间什么也看不清。空壁炉旁摆着一把大扶手椅，椅上坐着内政大臣詹姆斯·沃森爵士（Sir James Watson）那熟悉的身影。他蜷缩在椅子里，脑袋以一个古怪的角度歪向身子。

辛克莱正要往房里走。

“站住，”对方说，“千万进不得，会留下脚印的。不管出了什么事，都得请医生来。我在这儿等着，你去打电话叫医生。”

他扫视了一圈房间。

“这里好像没有电话。去问女管家。”


===Original===
Sinclair went to the head of the stairs and called.

There was some delay, and he called again angrily.

A muffled voice answered him.

“Where’s the telephone, quick?” he shouted.

A sound was heard on the stairs, and Mrs. Simmons came up. She was crying.

===Chinese===
辛克莱走到楼梯口喊人。

拖了一会儿没人应，他又气冲冲地喊了一声。

一个闷声闷气的声音应了他。

“电话在哪儿？快！”他吼道。

楼梯上响起脚步声，西蒙斯太太上来了。她正哭着。


===Original===
“Stop that,” said Sinclair roughly. “Where is the telephone?”

“There isn’t one in the house, sir,” she said. “Sir James had it taken away. He was always being rung up.”

Collins was getting impatient. “Send one of your men for a doctor, then, the old woman is no good. There are plenty of them round here. Hurry, man, it may be life or death.”

Sinclair dashed down the steps, and called the man on duty. He returned breathless.

Collins had dragged two large mats to the door of the library, and was carefully spreading one on the floor. The two men entered, and placed the second mat beyond the first.

===Chinese===
“别哭了，”辛克莱没好气地说，“电话在哪儿？”

“宅子里没有电话，长官，”她说，“詹姆斯爵士叫人给拆了。总有没完没了的电话找他。”

科林斯渐渐不耐烦了。“那就派你的一名手下去请医生，这老太太是指望不上的。这一带医生多得很。快去，伙计，这可是人命关天的事。”

辛克莱飞奔下台阶，唤来值守的警员，又气喘吁吁地跑了回来。

科林斯已把两块大地垫拖到书房门口，正细心地把一块铺在地板上。两人走进去，把第二块垫子接在第一块之后铺开。


===Original===
“On your knees,” he said in a whisper.

They approached the figure in the chair.

One glance was sufficient. Even in the semidarkness they could see an ugly mark on the side of the head from which a very thin trickle of blood was coming.

“A bullet hole,” said Sinclair, who was versed in these matters. “He’s been shot.”

“Hum,” said Collins, “wait for the doctor. Meanwhile I will have some light.” With the utmost precautions he moved his rugs to the window, and pulled up the blinds.

The room was beautifully furnished, for Sir James was a man of taste and had the means to gratify it.

===Chinese===
“跪着走。”他低声道。

两人朝椅中的人影挪近。

只消一眼就够了。即便在半暗中，他们也能看见头侧一处可怕的伤痕，一缕细细的血正从那里渗出来。

“弹孔，”精于此道的辛克莱说，“他是中枪了。”

“唔，”科林斯说，“等医生来了再说。先得有点光。”他小心翼翼地挪着垫子走到窗前，拉起百叶帘。

房间布置得精美雅致，詹姆斯爵士品位不俗，也有财力满足这份品位。


===Original===
The walls were covered with books to a height of seven feet.

Above that one or two choice pictures were hung.

The fireplace was a fine piece of carved oak.

As far as they could see, the room was empty.

The windows were hasped, and there was no other entrance.

The library had originally been two rooms, and ran the full depth of the house. It had been adapted by Sir James, and was his favourite room.

===Chinese===
墙上齐七英尺高都排满了书。

再往上挂着一两幅精心挑选的画。

壁炉是一件精美的雕橡木作品。

就他们所见，房间里再无别人。

窗户都搭着搭扣扣死，再没有别的入口。

这间书房原本是两个房间，纵贯整座宅子的进深。詹姆斯爵士把它改成了如今的样子，是他最心爱的房间。


===Original===
A fussy little doctor arrived, and was brought into the room with the same precautions.

Sinclair introduced himself and his companion.

The doctor made a very careful examination, while the others waited.

“Dead,” he said. “I should think about half an hour, possibly more. It is difficult to tell exactly.” He looked up.

“Is it a case of murder or suicide?”

“At present we know no more than you do,” said Sinclair. “We had only just come, and sent for you at once.”

===Chinese===
一位大惊小怪的小个子医生到了，也被以同样的防范措施引进房内。

辛克莱介绍了自己和同伴。

医生仔细检查，其余人等在一旁。

“死了，”他说，“我估计约莫半小时，兴许更久。确切时间很难断定。”他抬起头。

“这是谋杀还是自杀？”

“眼下我们知道的并不比你多，”辛克莱说，“我们也是刚到，立刻就请你来了。”


===Original===
“Quite right, quite right,” said the little doctor pompously.

“Meanwhile you will, of course, keep this entirely to yourself,” said Collins.

“But isn’t this?” he glanced at the stricken man. “Surely this is the Home Secretary.”

“Exactly,” said Collins dryly. “That is why it is necessary for you to remain silent until you are asked to speak. Superintendent Sinclair represents Scotland Yard. You understand?”

The doctor bowed. He saw himself playing a prominent part in a great drama, which would bring him notoriety and clients.

“The body had better be moved for me to make a more exhaustive examination,” said he.

===Chinese===
“很好，很好。”小个子医生拿腔作调地说。

“在此期间，此事当然要对外人绝口不提。”科林斯说。

“可这岂不是——”他瞥了一眼那遭了毒手的人，“这可不就是内政大臣嘛。”

“正是，”科林斯冷冷地说，“所以你必须保持沉默，直到有人要你开口。辛克莱警司代表苏格兰场。你明白吗？”

医生鞠了一躬。他仿佛看见自己在一出大戏里扮演显要角色，这会给他带来名声和主顾。

“最好把遗体挪个地方，让我做更彻底的检查。”他说。


===Original===
“Would you please wait outside till we have made our observations if you don’t mind, as the fewer in here the better, but I think you had better remain in the house, if you can manage it.”

“Certainly,” said the doctor, “I am at your service.”

“Then perhaps you would tell the housekeeper to stay where she is,” said Sinclair.

“Now for your men,” said Collins, when the doctor had gone out.

“We must tell them to see that no one leaves the house.” They went first to the front door and called Smith.

===Chinese===
“请你在外面稍候，等我们勘查完毕——如果不介意的话——这屋里人越少越好。不过我想，你要是能安排，最好还是留在宅子里。”

“当然，”医生说，“听凭吩咐。”

“那就麻烦你告诉女管家，让她待在原地别动。”辛克莱说。

“现在轮到你那两个人了。”医生出去后，科林斯说。

“得吩咐他们盯住，任何人不得离开宅子。”他们先到正门唤来史密斯。


===Original===
Collins was careful to keep the open library door in sight all the time.

After giving him his orders, they had to get in touch with the man at the back. There was a small garden, bounded by a high wall, and beyond that a lane. Seated on the wall was the figure of the other man, keeping a good lookout. Collins went to a back window and called him softly.

“Seen anything?” he asked.

“No, sir, nothing doing here,” said the man cheerfully.

It was refreshing in the midst of what looked like a grim tragedy to find a cheery soul who seemed to be enjoying himself.

He returned to Sinclair.

===Chinese===
科林斯始终小心地让敞开的书房门保持在视线之内。

向他交代完毕，他们还得同后面那人取得联系。宅后有个小花园，围着一道高墙，墙外是一条小巷。另一名警员正骑坐在墙头，警惕地瞭望。科林斯走到后窗，轻声唤他。

“看见什么了吗？”他问。

“没有，长官，这边没动静。”那人乐呵呵地说。

在这场眼看是惨烈的悲剧之中，竟有这么个乐天派，似乎还自得其乐，倒令人精神一振。

他回到辛克莱身边。


===Original===
“Now for the room.”

The two had been used to work together, and Sinclair knew exactly when to leave matters to Collins and when to take charge himself.

As was usual in these cases, Collins thought aloud, and the other checked his statements.

He approached the dead man, moving still on the rugs.

“Clean bullet wound﻿—no burning﻿—fired from a distance﻿—probably while he slept﻿—entered right temple﻿—bullet lodged in the brain﻿—all straight forward﻿—both hands limp, and peaceful expression﻿—ergo unexpected attack and no resistance﻿—now, let’s see﻿—eyes shut﻿—confirms first impression. Anything else about the body?”

Sinclair looked at it critically.

===Chinese===
“现在来看房间。”

两人共事已久，辛克莱清楚地知道什么时候该放手交给科林斯，什么时候该亲自主持。

照这类案子的惯例，科林斯边看边说出推断，同伴则替他核对。

他仍踩着垫子，走近死者。

“干净的弹孔——没有灼痕——隔了一段距离开的枪——多半是在他睡着时——射入右太阳穴——子弹留在脑中——一切直截了当——双手松垂，神情安详——可见是猝然袭击，未曾抵抗——再看看——双眼合着——印证了第一印象。遗体还有别的异常吗？”

辛克莱挑剔地打量着遗体。


===Original===
“No;” he said, “but from the way he lies the shot must have come from the doorway, or somewhere near that.”

“We are coming to that in a minute,” said the other.

“Now let’s have a look round. Observation only, no speculation. Table, with two glasses.” He took one up and then the other.

“Just whisky and soda. There’s the decanter and there’s the syphon.”

“Nothing very mysterious about that. But who was the visitor?﻿ ﻿… Cigar ash, I cannot tell five hundred kinds of ash,” said he with a smile, “still, they both smoked.”

===Chinese===
“没有，”他说，“不过从他躺卧的姿势看，那一枪必定是从门口方向打来的，或是那边附近某处。”

“这个我们马上就说到。”对方说。

“现在四下看看。只观察，不臆测。桌子上有两只杯子。”他先后拿起两只端详。

“只是威士忌和苏打水。酒瓶在那儿，苏打水壶也在。”

“这没什么神秘的。可访客是谁呢？……雪茄灰——我可分不清五百种烟灰。”他含笑说，“不过，他们俩都抽过烟。”


===Original===
“Now for the floor﻿—help me with the rugs. Right﻿—hullo.”

As they moved the second rug they disclosed a revolver lying on the floor. Collins picked it up.

“Service revolver﻿—Webley﻿—now obsolete﻿—” He broke the revolver carefully.

“Five full and one empty﻿—seems obvious﻿—too obvious.” He was always disappointed if a problem proved quite easy of solution.

“Well, we must wait for the bullet﻿—I hope it doesn’t fit﻿—”

Sinclair laughed. “I don’t believe you care in the least whether the murderer is punished or not, as long as you have something interesting to solve.”

===Chinese===
“现在看地板——帮我挪垫子。好了——咦。”

挪开第二块垫子时，底下露出一只掉在地板上的左轮手枪。科林斯把它拾了起来。

“军用左轮手枪——韦伯利（Webley）牌——如今已经退役——”他小心地掰开弹巢。

“五发满弹，一发空壳——看来一目了然——太一目了然了。”一个问题若轻易可解，他总会感到失望。

“唉，只能等取出子弹了——但愿它对不上号——”

辛克莱笑了。“我看只要有个有趣的谜可解，凶手受不受惩罚，你根本就不放在心上。”

===Original===
“Oh, I must say I like something abstruse; but never mind.

“Now for footprints. On this soft pile carpet they ought to show, thanks to our precautions.”

He went down on his knees, and examined the carpet carefully.

The other took a chair and watched.

After a long and keen search all over the room, he rose to his feet.

===Chinese===
“哦，我承认我喜欢费解的难题。不过先不管这个。

“现在看脚印。多亏我们防范周密，这软绒地毯上应该留得下来。”

他跪下去，仔细查看地毯。

辛克莱拉过一把椅子坐着看。

在全屋细细搜寻良久之后，他站起身来。


===Original===
“There are three sets of marks,” he said.

“Here are one lot walking up and down and crossing frequently. Number two was sitting down here, it is quite a different type of boot, or rather shoe, I think, and here are the marks which I rather fancy are my own when I stepped to lay the rug.”

He removed his shoe and placed it on the mark.

“That’s right,” he said. “It shows how careful one has to be. If you and I and the doctor had all walked over the carpet we should have obliterated the others.﻿ ﻿…

“Now which of these is the dead man’s?”

===Chinese===
“有三串脚印。”他说。

“这一串来回走动，还频繁交叠。第二个人是坐在这儿的，鞋型完全不同——我看与其说是靴子不如说是浅口鞋——这几枚脚印，我猜多半是我自己铺垫子时踩下的。”

他脱下自己的鞋，比在脚印上。

“没错，”他说，“可见人得何等小心。要是你我再加上医生都从地毯上走过去，其余的脚印就全毁了。……

“现在看看，哪一串是死者的？”


===Original===
Sinclair was hardened in criminal matters, and without compunction removed one of the dead man’s boots.

“Exactly,” said Collins, fitting it to a mark on the floor. “That’s that. He was the one who walked about the room. How does that fit in with the idea of him being shot when asleep?”

“No theories yet,” said Sinclair.

“Right you are. That’s the floor. Now the windows. Firmly fastened. Anything curious there?”

“Nothing that I see except we have to find how the murderer escaped.”

“A hot day in summer, and all the windows close fastened. Well, perhaps he did not want the shot to be heard.”

===Chinese===
辛克莱对刑案早已处之泰然，毫不迟疑地脱下死者一只靴子。

“正是，”科林斯把它比向地板上一枚脚印。“这就清楚了。在屋里走来走去的正是他。这跟他熟睡中枪的说法怎么对得上呢？”

“先别下结论。”辛克莱说。

“说得对。地板看完了。再看窗户。关得严严实实。有什么蹊跷吗？”

“我看没有，只是我们还得查明凶手是怎么逃走的。”

“大热的天，所有窗户却扣得死死的。唔，也许他是不想让人听见枪声。”


===Original===
“Are you suggesting suicide?”

“Why not? Oh, I see, you are thinking of the telephone message. Still, we must not eliminate the possibility at present.”

“Door locked on the inside, and no trace of the second person.”

“Of course, the walls and floor will have to be examined,” said Sinclair.

“Of course, and the ceiling and chimney. Well, that’s all here, and we had better get the doctor and remove the body.”

“Wrap those glasses and decanter and syphon carefully up for finger prints,” said Collins.

===Chinese===
“你是在暗示自杀？”

“为什么不能是？哦，我明白了，你是想着那通电话。尽管如此，眼下我们还不能排除这种可能。”

“门从里面锁着，第二个人却无影无踪。”

“当然，墙壁和地板还得查验。”辛克莱说。

“当然，还有天花板和烟囱。好，这里就到此为止，最好请医生进来，把遗体搬走。”

“把那些杯子、酒瓶和苏打水壶仔细包好，留验指纹。”科林斯说。


===Original===
Sinclair turned scarlet.

“I am most awfully sorry. I ought to have known better, but this thing upset me rather. While you were grovelling on the floor I helped myself to a little whisky﻿—it was really unpardonable.”

“I saw you,” said Collins coolly. “It would cost you your place if it were known, but I shan’t tell any tales.”

“Thanks,” said Sinclair simply. It was a little weakness he had.

They took the body carefully into the dining-room, and left it with the doctor.

“Now for the housekeeper,” said Sinclair.

===Chinese===
辛克莱的脸涨得通红。

“实在对不起之至。我本不该犯这种错的，只是这事把我搅得心神不宁。你在地上趴着的时候，我给自己倒了点威士忌——实在不可原谅。”

“我看见了。”科林斯淡淡地说，“这事要是传出去，你这饭碗就保不住了，不过我不会说出去的。”

“多谢。”辛克莱老老实实地说。这是他的一个小毛病。

他们小心地把遗体抬进餐厅，交给医生看守。

“现在找女管家问话。”辛克莱说。


===Original===
“Mrs. Simmons, will you come here, please?”

The woman came in very distressed, and seated herself, at a word from Collins.

“Now, Mrs. Simmons,” he said in kind tones, “Can you throw any light on this affair? Please calm yourself and tell us all you know.”

After a prolonged examination, the following facts were elicited, which are better put together.

The Home Secretary was a widower. He had one daughter, Mabel, who lived with him. She had gone down to their country place in Devonshire, from which he had come the day before, and he intended to return the next day. The servants had gone, leaving Mrs. Simmons to look after Sir James.

===Chinese===
“西蒙斯太太，请你过来一下好吗？”

女管家悲悲戚戚地走进来，科林斯一句吩咐，她便坐下了。

“好了，西蒙斯太太，”他和颜悦色地说，“你能对这桩事情提供些线索吗？请镇定下来，把你知道的都告诉我们。”

经过长时间的盘问，问出了下面这些情况，这里一并归拢叙述。

内政大臣是个鳏夫，有一个女儿梅布尔（Mabel），同他住在一起。她已经去了他们位于德文郡（Devonshire）的乡下宅邸——他前一天刚从那边回来，本打算次日再回去。仆人们都放假走了，只留西蒙斯太太照料詹姆斯爵士。


===Original===
He had been at his Office all the morning, returning for lunch. He was a solitary man and shunned company.

At about three o’clock a ring had come at the door, and she had gone up to answer it. When she got to the top of the stairs, she saw that Sir James had already opened the door. A man came in and went straight to the library. She could not see anything of him, as Sir James was between them. She thought nothing of it, as it was probably an official from the Home Office. Sir James locked the door, and the two were together for about half an hour. She heard nothing, as the kitchen was not under the library.

Then there was a ring from the library. She was quite certain of that. She went up after a moment, as she had been writing a letter. When she got to the hall, Sir James was showing the visitor out, and she did not see him. Sir James stood on the steps and watched him go. As she was not wanted she went down again.

A little later Sir James came out from the library, and went across the road with a letter, which he put into the pillar-box. She waited for him, as she wished to ask about arrangements for the evening.

===Chinese===
他整个上午都在部里办公，午饭时回来。他是个落落寡合的人，不爱交际。

三点钟左右，门铃响了，她上楼应门。走到楼梯顶时，她看见詹姆斯爵士已经开了门。一个男子进来，径直进了书房。由于爵士挡在中间，那人什么样她一点也没瞧见。她也没在意，心想多半是内政部的官员。詹姆斯爵士锁上门，两人在里面待了约莫半个钟头。她什么也没听见，因为厨房并不在书房正下方。

接着书房里响起铃。这一点她十分肯定。她当时正在写信，隔了片刻才上去。走到门厅时，詹姆斯爵士正送客出门，她还是没看见来客。爵士站在台阶上，目送那人离去。用不着她，她便又下楼去了。

过了片刻，詹姆斯爵士从书房出来，拿着一封信过了马路，投进了邮筒。她等他回来，想问问晚上的安排。


===Original===
When he came back he told her he should be out to dinner, and that he was on no account to be disturbed.

He had then gone in and locked the door. She was certain of that﻿—she had heard the key turn. After that she had been busy in the dining-room, and was quite certain that no one else had called. She had heard nothing until the two men had come. She had heard no sound of a shot.

She would certainly have heard one from where she was.

“Why was she so agitated when the door was opened?”

She was given to presentiments, and was feeling afraid of something after the strange man had called.

At the conclusion Collins asked her about the household. Were there any relations who came?

===Chinese===
他回来后告诉她，他晚饭要在外面吃，而且无论如何不许打扰他。

随后他便进了书房，锁上了门。这一点她确定无疑——她听见钥匙转动的声音。之后她一直在餐厅里忙活，确信再没有旁人来过。直到那两位进门，她什么也没听见，也没听见枪声。

以她所处的地方，枪声她是断乎听得见的。

“那开门时她为何那样惊惶？”

她这人容易心生预感，那陌生男子来访之后，总觉得要出什么事。

最后，科林斯问起家里的情况。有亲戚来往吗？


===Original===
“No, sir,” she said, “though Sir James used to have regular house-parties at his country place. He lived very quietly in London.”

“Has he ever shown any signs of being afraid of an attack, or anything of that sort?”

“Well, sir, there have been times when he seemed uneasy. He has asked whether there was anybody hanging round the house, and he always kept a loaded revolver in his room.”

“Oh, did he? And where is it now?”

The housekeeper led them into the library, now guarded by a plainclothes man. She opened a drawer in the writing table. Within lay a small silver-plated revolver, fully loaded.

“You say that Sir James had one child, a daughter?” said Collins.

===Chinese===
“没有，长官，”她说，“不过詹姆斯爵士以前常在乡下宅邸定期举办庄园聚会。在伦敦他过得非常清静。”

“他可曾显露过担心遇袭之类的迹象？”

“呃，长官，有几次他确实显得心神不宁。他问过宅子周围有没有人转悠，他房里还总放着一把上了膛的左轮手枪。”

“哦，是吗？现在枪在哪儿？”

女管家领他们进了书房，此时已有一名便衣看守。她打开写字台的一个抽屉，里面躺着一把镀银小左轮手枪，弹巢满弹。

“你说詹姆斯爵士有一个孩子，是女儿？”科林斯问。


===Original===
Mrs. Simmons hesitated. “Well, sir, I have been with the family twenty years. There was another, a son, but he was a wrong ’un, and went abroad many years ago, and, as far as I know, the family have heard nothing since.”

“But hasn’t Sir James kept in touch with him?”

“Of course, I do not know all that has happened, but I do know that Sir James used to make an allowance to him; but the time came when the firm of lawyers said they had lost all trace of him, and the money was stopped.”

“Where was he at that time﻿—I mean in what country?”

“I don’t properly know, but it was in one of those South American States.”

“And that is all you can tell us,” said Collins, fixing the housekeeper with a sharp look.

===Chinese===
西蒙斯太太迟疑了一下。“呃，长官，我在这家做了二十年了。本来还有一个，是位少爷，可他是个败类，多年前就去了国外，据我所知，家里人从此再没有他的音信。”

“可詹姆斯爵士没同他保持联系吗？”

“当然，事情的全貌我并不清楚，但我知道詹姆斯爵士从前按月给他津贴；后来律师事务所说他已下落全无，钱也就停了。”

“那时他在什么地方——我是说在哪个国家？”

“我不很清楚，只知道是南美那一带的国家。”

“你能说的就这些了。”科林斯目光锐利地盯住女管家。


===Original===
“Yes, sir, as far as I can remember, but of course I am all of a fluster. Something more may occur to me; but, oh, sir, what shall I do, I cannot stay in this dreadful house?”

“There is no need for you to do so, is there, Sinclair?” said Collins.

The other tugged at his moustache. “I don’t know. Where are you going?”

“I want to go to my sister’s house at Forest Gate, if I may, I am so upset with all this.”

Collins drew Sinclair aside.

“Let her go,” he said, “and have her watched. It may be useful.”

===Chinese===
“是的，长官，就我记起来的而言是这些了，可我这心里乱作一团。说不定还会想起些什么。可是，唉，长官，我可怎么办哪，这凶宅我是一刻也待不下去了。”

“你不必待在这儿，对吧，辛克莱？”科林斯说。

对方捻着胡髭。“我不知道啊。你要去哪儿？”

“要是允许的话，我想去福雷斯特盖特（Forest Gate）我妹妹家，这一切实在叫我受不住。”

科林斯把辛克莱拉到一边。

“放她走，”他说，“派人盯住她。兴许有用。”


===Original===
“Very good,” said he. To Mrs. Simmons, he said, “You can go, but you must give us your address, you will be wanted as a witness at the inquest. Don’t talk about the affair at all. Do you understand?”

“Thank you, sir, I will go and pack,” said she gratefully.

Collins watched her go.

“What do you make of her?” he said.

“She seemed quite straightforward; I think she’s told us the truth.”

Collins gave a laugh. “Yes,” he said. “The truth, but not the whole truth. She’s a clever old woman.”

===Chinese===
“很好。”他说。接着对西蒙斯太太说：“你可以走了，但必须留下地址，审讯时需要你作证。这事一个字也不许外传。明白吗？”

“谢谢您，长官，我这就去收拾行李。”她感激地说。

科林斯望着她离去。

“你看她这个人怎么样？”他问。

“她看上去相当直率，我看她说的是实话。”

科林斯笑了笑。“是啊，”他说，“是实话，但不是全部实话。这个老太太精明得很。”


===Original===
“What do you mean?”

“When a simple soul tells the tale, and tries to conceal something, she gives herself away. She will not look straight at you. When you are dealing with the cunning type, she will look at you with a particularly open face and innocent look. All the time she was telling her narratives she was confused and upset, as was natural. But when I asked her if she had anything else to say her manner altered, and she became collected and looked me straight in the face.”

“Oh, you imagine these things. I didn’t see any difference.”

“Very good,” said Collins, “we shall see.”

“Now for the next move,” said Sinclair, who always got irritated when his colleague assumed this superior manner. “I must go to the Yard and make a full report. We cannot keep this thing secret. It will make a great stir. Will you come with me?”

===Chinese===
“这话怎讲？”

“心地单纯的人讲述事情又想隐瞒点什么时，就会露马脚——她不敢正眼看你。而对付狡猾的那类人，她反倒会一脸特别坦荡、特别无辜地看着你。她讲述经过时始终慌乱失措，这很自然。可当我问她还有什么要补充时，她的神态变了，变得镇定自若，直直地望着我的脸。”

“哦，这都是你的臆想。我可没看出有什么两样。”

“很好，”科林斯说，“走着瞧吧。”

“下一步怎么办？”辛克莱说——每逢同事摆出这副高人一等的架势，他总要来气。“我必须回场里写份完整报告。这事瞒不住的，必将掀起轩然大波。你跟我一起去吗？”

===Original===
“I will run you down in my car, and then must get off at once.”

“Where to?” said the other in surprise.

“Someone must break the news to the girl. It’s a rotten job, but it’s of the greatest importance. I am off to Devonshire, and hope I shall arrive in time.”

“In time?”

“Before the news reaches there.”

“You’ve got something at the back of your mind, I can see that. It’s not just to spare the girl’s feelings.”

===Chinese===
“我开车送你过去，然后就得马上动身。”

“去哪儿？”对方诧异地问。

“总得有人把噩耗告诉那姑娘。这是份苦差事，却又至关重要。我这就动身去德文郡，但愿赶得及。”

“赶得及？”

“抢在消息传到之前。”

“你心里打着别的算盘，我看得出来。绝不仅仅是不忍伤那姑娘的心。”


===Original===
Collins smiled. “I would like to make the acquaintance of the family,” he said.

“But there is only one in the family,” said Sinclair surprised.

“Perhaps,” said Collins.

They made their way into the Square, where dusk was falling.

Several persons were looking up at the house and pointing.

“What the devil is the meaning of that?” said Sinclair, as Collins was starting the car.

===Chinese===
科林斯微微一笑。“我想结识一下这一家人。”他说。

“可这家里只有一个人啊。”辛克莱吃惊地说。

“也许吧。”科林斯说。

他们走向广场，暮色正降临。

有几个人正仰望那座宅子，指指点点。

“这到底是怎么回事？”科林斯发动车子时，辛克莱问道。


===Original===
“Ask me another, jump in,” and they went off.

As they turned into Bond Street, where the lights were on, they saw a newspaper boy shouting, and running down the street. In front of him was a news-bill, on which was printed:

“Home Secretary Murdered at his House.

				Full Details.”

“Well, I’m damned,” said the Superintendent.

===Chinese===
“我哪知道，上车吧。”两人疾驰而去。

转进邦德街（Bond Street）时，街上已灯火通明，只见一个报童喊着叫着沿街奔跑，胸前挂着一块新闻招贴，上面印着：

“内政大臣在家中遇害。

全部详情。”

“嘿，真是活见鬼。”警司说。


===Original===
Collins stopped the car, and bought a paper.

On the News page, across two columns, was a flaring account of the murder.

“What in Hell’s name is the meaning of this?” said Sinclair.

“Let’s go to the Yard,” said Collins, putting in the clutch.

Mr. Boyce was a flabby man of fifty. He had had an unsuccessful career at the Bar which would have ruined a man without means; but his father was a distinguished Judge of the High Court, and had considerable influence. After trying to get his son a job as Stipendiary and a County Court Judge, he at last jobbed him into the position of Commissioner in Scotland Yard, where he subsisted on the brains of his subordinates. He listened with an assumption of wisdom to the account of the affair given by Sinclair. Collins had come with him after the incident of the newspaper. He had a profound contempt for Boyce, which the other resented though he dared not show his resentment.

===Chinese===
科林斯停下车，买了一份报。

新闻版上横跨两栏，登着这篇耸人听闻的凶案报道。

“这到底是搞的什么名堂？”辛克莱说。

“去场里再说。”科林斯踩下离合器。

博伊斯先生（Mr. Boyce）是个五十岁上下、软塌塌的人。他在律师界混得不得意，这份事业若换作没有家底的人早被拖垮了；好在他父亲是高等法院声名显赫的法官，颇有势力。老爷子先想给儿子谋个领薪治安法官或郡法院法官的差事，最后终于把他安插进苏格兰场当上了总监，靠着下属的头脑混日子。他摆出一副胸有成竹的样子，听辛克莱讲述事情经过。报纸事件之后，科林斯同他一道来了。科林斯对博伊斯深怀轻蔑，博伊斯怀恨在心，却不敢表露。


===Original===
While Sinclair was reporting, Collins had got busy with a timetable, and then turned to the telephone.

The others waited while he called up.

After several conversations, he laid the receiver down, and turned to the other two.

“The Editor of the *Evening Rag*,” said he. “I asked him where he got the news of the murder from, and he says via the Central News. He says he was careful to ascertain whether it was authentic before he sent it to press. What do you think he says?”

“Can’t guess,” said Sinclair shortly.

“He says it came in in the form of a report from Scotland Yard, on official paper, signed by Superintendent Sinclair.”

===Chinese===
辛克莱汇报的时候，科林斯忙着翻列车时刻表，随后又转向电话。

他打电话时，其余两人等着。

说了几通电话之后，他放下听筒，转向另外两人。

“《晚间小报》（*Evening Rag*）的主编，”他说，“我问他这凶案消息是从哪儿来的，他说是经中央新闻社（Central News）转的。他说发排之前还特意核实过真伪。你们猜他怎么说？”

“猜不着。”辛克莱没好气地说。

“他说消息是以苏格兰场通报的形式发来的，用的是公函信纸，署名辛克莱警司。”


===Original===
Sinclair turned purple.

Boyce looked at him with large, fishy eyes.

“Really, really,” he said, “this is most unorthodox.”

“You don’t suppose I sent it in, do you, sir,” spluttered Sinclair.

Collins intervened.

“I can answer for that,” said he; “Sinclair has been with me the whole time. No; there is another explanation for this.”

===Chinese===
辛克莱的脸涨成了紫色。

博伊斯瞪着一双呆滞的大鱼眼瞧着他。

“真是的，真是的，”他说，“这太不合规矩了。”

“长官，您总不会以为是我发出去的吧。”辛克莱气急败坏地说。

科林斯出面打圆场。

“这点我可以担保，”他说，“辛克莱一直同我在一起。不，此事另有解释。”


===Original===
“What is that?”

“Why the same person who called us on the phone, and probably the murderer. It is curious how vain these people are. He may have stepped too far. It’s just possible he’s given us a valuable clue. One cannot send letters with impunity. There’s the post mark, and the time.”

“The document must be obtained,” said Boyce.

“I have already asked the Central News to send it here for inspection. It is coming now by hand.”

While they waited, Collins turned to Sinclair.

“About that telephone call, you say it was a woman’s voice?”

===Chinese===
“什么解释？”

“就是给我们打电话的那个人，十有八九就是凶手。说来奇怪，这类人多么爱慕虚荣。他这一步或许走过头了，倒可能送给我们一条宝贵的线索。寄信是不可能不留痕迹的——有邮戳，还有时间。”

“必须把那份文件弄到手。”博伊斯说。

“我已请中央新闻社把它送来查验，这会儿正由专人送来。”

等待的当口，科林斯转向辛克莱。

“说说那通电话，你说那是个女人的声音？”


===Original===
“Well, of course, I thought so at the time; but it may have been a man’s disguised.”

“Or something else?” said Collins.

“What do you mean?” said Boyce, almost startled.

“Well, it might have been a boy’s voice.”

“Oh, surely not.”

“We cannot eliminate the possibility, and then again it might have been a man’s voice not disguised.”

===Chinese===
“唔，当时我自然这么觉得，但也可能是男人装出来的。”

“或者别的可能？”科林斯说。

“你是什么意思？”博伊斯问，几乎吃了一惊。

“唔，那也可能是个男孩的声音。”

“哦，绝不会吧。”

“我们不能排除这种可能，而且，那也可能是一个没有伪装的男人声音。”


===Original===
“How could that be?”

“There are some men with treble voices who sing falsetto like a boy. We cannot take anything for granted.”

Boyce gave a sniff. He did not like this sort of speculation.

“I must get back and change, and then get some food, and catch the night train,” said Collins. “I have plenty of time, so we can go into the position if you care to. You had better have some grub with me, and if you care to join us,” he said to Boyce, “I shall be delighted.”

“Thank you very much, I will with pleasure,” said the other. It was just what he wanted. He could listen to the others and then retail the information as his own. It was the way in which he worked his department.

===Chinese===
“那怎么可能？”

“有些男人天生一副高音嗓子，能像男孩那样唱假声。任何事我们都不能想当然。”

博伊斯嗤了一声。他不喜欢这类推想。

“我得回去换衣服，吃点东西，赶夜班火车，”科林斯说，“时间还宽裕，二位若愿意，可以把局势捋一遍。你们最好同我随便吃点——”他对博伊斯说，“您若肯赏光，我很高兴。”

“多谢好意，我乐意奉陪。”对方说。这正中他下怀——他可以先听两人谈论，再把消息当自己的转述出去。他掌管部门靠的就是这套办法。


===Original===
A messenger boy was ushered in by a clerk, and handed a document to Sinclair, who signed the receipt and the lad departed.

In haste he opened the envelope, and pulled out another which had been opened. It was addressed to the Central News Agency, and was a Government envelope. Inside was a sheet of paper with the official stamp of Scotland Yard.

The note was short and in type.

“Sir,” it said, “I am authorized to inform you that the Home Secretary, Sir James Watson, was murdered this afternoon at his house in Leveson Square between three and four o’clock, by an unknown assailant. He was shot through the head, and death was instantaneous.

“ ‘The cause of the crime is at present unknown, and no trace of the assailant can be found. Scotland Yard have the matter in hand, and a reward will shortly be offered for information leading to the apprehension of the murderer.’ ”

===Chinese===
文书领进来一名信差小童，把一份文件交给辛克莱。辛克莱签了回执，小童便走了。

他急忙拆开信封，抽出另一个已拆开的信封。那信封是寄给中央新闻社的，用的是政府公函信封。里面是一张盖着苏格兰场官印的信纸。

便函很短，是打字打的。

“先生，”函上写道，“兹奉命通知：内政大臣詹姆斯·沃森爵士今日下午三时至四时之间，在利文森广场的宅邸中遭不明凶手杀害。子弹贯穿头部，当场毙命。

“‘案发动因目前不明，凶手亦无线索可寻。苏格兰场已着手查办，并将悬赏缉拿凶犯，凡提供线索有助捕获凶手者皆有重赏。’”


===Original===
It was signed “Arthur Sinclair, Superintendent, C.I.D.” and had the official stamp on it.

Sinclair laid the paper down with a look of bewilderment.

“We must keep this carefully,” said Boyce sententiously. “It is a document of the utmost importance.”

“This is of greater importance,” said Collins quietly.

The other two looked at him in surprise. He was holding the envelope.

===Chinese===
落款是“亚瑟·辛克莱，刑事侦查部（C.I.D.）警司”，并盖着官印。

辛克莱一脸茫然地把信纸放下。

“这份文件必须妥善保存。”博伊斯煞有介事地说，“它是至为重要的物证。”

“这个更重要。”科林斯平静地说。

另外两人惊异地看着他。他手里正拿着那个信封。


===Original===
Slowly he laid it on the table and pointed.

“This has escaped the notice of the Central News people. Probably because they have an assistant to open envelopes who simply throws them into the wastepaper basket. I particularly asked them to get the envelope, which they have done.”

“But what is the point?”

Collins placed his finger on the postmark.

“Two forty-five,” said he. “This was sent off before the murder took place.”

The three men looked at each other in silence.

===Chinese===
他缓缓把信封放在桌上，指了指。

“这一点逃过了中央新闻社那班人的眼睛。大概因为拆信的助手随手就把信封扔进了废纸篓。我特意嘱咐他们把信封也找来，他们照办了。”

“可这有什么蹊跷？”

科林斯把手指按在邮戳上。

“两点四十五分，”他说，“这信是在凶案发生之前寄出的。”

三人面面相觑，默然无语。

## II. Speculations / 第二章 推测


===Original===
Collins, Sinclair and Boyce had just discussed an excellent glass of port after a frugal but well-cooked meal at Collins’ flat.

The room was tastefully but not luxuriously furnished, and was stamped with the individuality of the occupier.

Over the mantelpiece was an oar, a relic of the time when Collins had stroked his college boat to victory in the *Mays*.

Four selected pictures were on the walls, but the eye was caught by *Napoleon, the Last Phase*, which seemed to dominate the room, with its tortured sadness.

Collins rose, rang the bell for coffee, and handed round cigars.

===Chinese===
科林斯、辛克莱和博伊斯在科林斯的公寓里用过一顿简单却烹制得法的饭菜之后，刚刚“研讨”完一杯上好的波特酒。

这房间布置得雅致而不奢华，处处带着主人的个性印记。

壁炉架上方横着一支船桨，是科林斯当年担任领桨手、率学院艇在“五月赛”（*Mays*）中夺魁的纪念。

墙上挂着四幅精心挑选的画，但最引人注目的是那幅《拿破仑，最后阶段》（*Napoleon, the Last Phase*），画中饱含痛苦的悲怆，仿佛君临整个房间。

科林斯起身，按铃叫咖啡，又把雪茄分给众人。


===Original===
During the meal all reference to the tragedy of the day had been dropped by tacit consent. Now each of the men drew up his chair to the fire, and prepared to discuss the affair﻿—in spite of the heat of the day the nights were cold.

Collins suggested, with an irony which was lost on the others, that Boyce should give them the light of his wisdom on the problem.

This he hastily declined, and Sinclair asked Collins to open the ball. Here he was in his element.

“I will go through the facts, and Sinclair can check me.” The others nodded assent. The cigars were good and the chairs comfortable. They were in the right mood for listening.

“First then,” said Collins, “there are four possibilities. It may have been suicide, but the doctor does not think that is possible. There was no blackening round the wound, and it would not be likely that a man could shoot himself through the head and throw the revolver away from him.”

===Chinese===
席间，三人默契地绝口不提当天的惨剧。此刻，各人把椅子挪近壁炉，准备讨论这桩案子——尽管白天炎热，夜里却很冷。

科林斯提议，请博伊斯就这个难题赐教高见——这反话两人都没听出来。

博伊斯慌忙推辞，辛克莱便请科林斯开个头。这正是他如鱼得水的场合。

“我来把事实过一遍，辛克莱可以帮我核对。”其余两人点头同意。雪茄上好，座椅舒适，正是听讲的好心境。

“那么，首先，”科林斯说，“有四种可能。可能是自杀，但医生认为不可能。伤口周围没有灼黑，而且一个人朝自己头部开枪后还能把左轮手枪扔出去，这种可能性微乎其微。”


===Original===
“But what about the telephone messages and the newspaper article?” said Boyce.

“We are coming to that, but they may have nothing to do with the crime.”

The other two exchanged glances.

“Then we come to accident. That is a possibility. A man may have tried blackmail or to extract some secret, and fired by accident. Here again the position of the body and the whole arrangement of the room are against such an idea.”

===Chinese===
“那电话通报和报纸文章作何解释？”博伊斯说。

“那个我们马上要谈到，不过它们可能与罪案本身无关。”

另外两人交换了一下眼色。

“接下来是意外。这是一种可能。也许有人试图敲诈或逼问某个秘密，失手开了枪。但尸体的位置和整个房间的布置同样否定了这种想法。”


===Original===
“Then there is only murder?” said Boyce.

“Not quite,” said the other. “There is first the work of a madman.”

“But that is nevertheless murder,” said Sinclair.

“Yes; but we then have to proceed on an entirely different basis. If this is the work of a lunatic, it explains the telephone messages and the newspaper article. It is just the sort of thing that a madman with an inordinate vanity would do. And we need not look for motive. If that is so, our task will be simplified.”

The others agreed.

===Chinese===
“那就只剩谋杀了？”博伊斯说。

“未必，”对方说，“首先还有疯子的作为这一种。”

“可那也仍旧是谋杀啊。”辛克莱说。

“是的，但那样我们就得在一个全然不同的基础上进行推演。如果这是疯子所为，电话通报和报纸文章就都得到了解释。这正是一个虚荣心极度膨胀的疯子会干的事，而且我们不必寻找动机。若果真如此，我们的任务反倒简单了。”

其余两人表示同意。


===Original===
“Now we come to the fourth. A cold-blooded and deliberate murder, of which each detail was planned beforehand so accurately that the criminal had the effrontery to inform the Press before it was done.”

“That is the most probable,” said Sinclair.

“I agree, but we must not lose sight of the others.”

“Now for our facts. At sometime before 2:30 when the box was cleared, a letter was posted at Westminster Bridge Post Office, the contents of which you know. At 3:00 o’clock a man calls on the Home Secretary who is either expected or well known to Sir James.”

===Chinese===
“现在说第四种。一场冷血而蓄谋已久的谋杀，每个细节都事先计划得分毫不差，以致罪犯胆敢在动手之前就通知报界。”

“这种可能性最大。”辛克莱说。

“我同意，但其余几种也不能忽视。”

“现在说我们掌握的事实。2:30 信箱清空之前的某个时刻，有人在威斯敏斯特桥邮局投寄了一封信，内容你们都知道了。3:00 整，一名男子来访，这位内政大臣要么正在等他，要么此人深为詹姆斯爵士所熟识。”


===Original===
“How do you know that?” said Boyce.

“Surely,” said Collins; “he opened the door himself, and takes him straight to his library.”

“This man may have been the actual murderer or not. We are here going on the statements of the housekeeper, which may turn out to be a tissue of lies; but I do not think so, she is not a good enough actress for that. This man stays for half an hour, and is let out by the Home Secretary. After that Sir James writes a letter and posts it himself. He returns and goes to his room complaining of feeling sleepy.”

“Did he?” said Sinclair, “I did not hear that.”

===Chinese===
“你怎么知道？”博伊斯说。

“这不明摆着嘛，”科林斯说，“爵士亲自开的门，还把他径直领进书房。”

“这人可能就是凶手，也可能不是。这方面我们依据的是女管家的供述，它也可能是一派谎言连篇；但我不这么看，她的演技还没好到那个地步。此人待了半个钟头，由内政大臣亲自送出门。此后詹姆斯爵士写了一封信，亲自去投寄。他回来后进了书房，说觉得自己发困。”

“是吗？”辛克莱说，“这我可没听说过。”


===Original===
“Certainly,” said Collins, “Mrs. Simmons said so, if she is reliable. Very good, he locks himself in, and asks not to be disturbed. Here he remains, as far as we know, till the murder takes place. We find the door locked and the windows fastened, with no apparent means of escape. There is no one in the room.”

“By Jove, he was a cool hand,” said Sinclair. “All the time he was talking with Sir James the letter was on its way to the Central News, and might have arrived. He must have calculated things pretty well.”

“Undoubtedly, and he probably knew that there was no telephone in the house.”

Collins got up and handed round the cigar box. When he resumed his seat he continued, and his face was grave.

===Chinese===
“千真万确，”科林斯说，“西蒙斯太太说的——如果她可靠的话。很好，他把自己锁在里面，还吩咐不许打扰。据我们所知，他就一直待在里面，直到凶案发生。我们发现门锁着，窗户扣死，没有明显的脱逃途径，房间里也没有别人。”

“天哪，好一个冷静的家伙。”辛克莱说。“他同詹姆斯爵士谈话的全部时间里，那封信正在寄往中央新闻社的途中，说不定已经送到了。他的算计必定相当精准。”

“毫无疑问，而且他多半知道宅子里没有电话。”

科林斯起身，把雪茄盒递了一圈。重新落座后，他接着说，神色凝重起来。


===Original===
“A Home Secretary is very open to attack. He may have refused to pardon a criminal, and the man when he comes out from penal servitude or imprisonment will seek revenge. He is always getting threatening letters. Then there are murderers whom he reprieves, and the relatives of the murdered man may seek revenge. Again, there are political fanatics. You remember the Phoenix Park murders.”

“Of course,” said Boyce, “the whole staff will be put on tomorrow to investigate this side of the question.” Collins nodded.

“Then there is a personal revenge. His life appears to be a blameless and honourable one, but one never knows; there are skeletons in the best of cupboards.”

“There was a ne’er-do-well son,” said Sinclair.

===Chinese===
“内政大臣是最容易招致袭击的人。他可能拒绝赦免某个罪犯，那人在服完苦役或监禁出狱之后就会寻仇。他总收到恐吓信。还有被他缓刑的杀人犯，以及被杀者的亲属，都可能寻仇。再有就是政治狂徒。你们记得凤凰公园暗杀案吧。”

“当然，”博伊斯说，“明天全体人手都会投入这个方向的调查。”科林斯点了点头。

“还有私仇。他的一生似乎无可指摘、体面清白，但谁也说不准——再体面的柜子里也藏着骷髅。”

“不是还有个不成器的儿子嘛。”辛克莱说。


===Original===
“Chut, don’t let’s come to any personalities until we have more data; we shall be following wandering fires.”

Sinclair was not to be silenced.

“What do you make of the behaviour of Lewis when I asked him to come with me?” he said.

“I don’t pretend to make anything of it. It may have the most natural of explanations.”

“I have never known him to behave like that before,” he persisted. Collins ignored him.

===Chinese===
“嘘，数据不足之前先别涉及具体的人，否则咱们就是追着鬼火跑了。”

辛克莱却不肯罢休。

“我让他跟我一起去的时候，刘易斯那副样子，你怎么看？”他说。

“我不假装能看出什么。那也许有再自然不过的解释。”

“我从没见过他那样。”他还想坚持。科林斯不加理会。


===Original===
“We have five things to bear in mind, or shall I put it we have five questions to answer:

“Who sent the letter to the Central News, and for what object?

“Who called us up on the telephone, and why?

“Who was the man who called at three?

“How did the murderer get in, and how did he escape?

“What was the motive of the murderer?”

===Chinese===
“有五件事须牢记在心，或者这么说吧，有五个问题要回答：

“谁把信寄给了中央新闻社，目的何在？

“谁打电话找到了我们，为什么？

“三点钟来访的男子是谁？

“凶手怎么进去的，又是怎么逃走的？

“凶手的动机是什么？”


===Original===
“You’ve left out the most important of all,” said Boyce, “who was the murderer?”

“Yes, of course, there’s always that,” said Collins with an indulgent smile.

“You’ve put it very well,” said Sinclair, who was in a genial mood after Collins’ excellent fare.

“We must find out all we can about the letter and envelope.”

===Chinese===
“你把最重要的一个漏了，”博伊斯说，“凶手是谁？”

“是啊，当然，这一问总是少不了的。”科林斯带着宽容的微笑说。

“你归纳得非常好。”辛克莱说——享用了科林斯的美食之后，他心情正好。

“关于信和信封，我们必须尽量查个水落石出。”


===Original===
“It was a most extraordinarily good forgery, that signature,” said Boyce, “I would have sworn in any court it was yours.”

Sinclair’s face flushed. “Are you suggesting anything, sir,” he said.

“Of course not, my dear fellow, only it was, wasn’t it?”

“Then you must try and find out about the telephone calls,” intervened Collins.

===Chinese===
“那个签名伪造得实在高明，”博伊斯说，“随便到哪个法庭我都能发誓那是你签的。”

辛克莱的脸涨红了。“长官，您这是暗示什么吗？”他说。

“当然不是，亲爱的伙计，只是那签名确实像，不是吗？”

“那你们得设法查一查那几通电话。”科林斯插话道。


===Original===
“I shall have Mrs. Simmons up to the Yard and take down a full statement,” said Boyce, anxious to show he had a grasp of the situation.

“All right, I must put a few things together and get off. We will each carry on with our own line of research.”

And he went into the next room.

“Has he got something up his sleeve?” said Boyce. “It’s not like him to go off when there’s an interesting problem to solve. It can’t be just to break the news, he knows nothing of these people.”

“He’s pretty deep, and it may be he saw something that I didn’t,” said Sinclair, modestly. “He seemed to think Mrs. Simmons was holding something back.”

===Chinese===
“我要把西蒙斯太太传到场里，录一份完整的口供。”博伊斯说，急于显示自己掌控着局势。

“好吧，我得收拾几样东西动身了。咱们各自沿自己的线索查下去。”

说罢他走进隔壁房间。

“他是不是藏了什么牌？”博伊斯说。“有难题可解的时候中途离开，可不像他的作风。总不会只是为了去报丧——这些人他一个也不认识。”

“他城府很深，也许是瞧出了什么我没瞧出的东西。”辛克莱谦逊地说。“他好像认为西蒙斯太太有所隐瞒。”


===Original===
Collins came back with a small suitcase.

“I will let you know when I am coming back,” he said, “and we will meet and compare notes. This will make a most almighty stir, and if the Prime Minister thinks it is the work of an ex-convict or anything of that sort, you will get it hot.”

Boyce made a wry face.

“I am afraid so,” he said.

===Chinese===
科林斯提着一只小提箱回来了。

“我什么时候回来会通知你们的，”他说，“到时候咱们碰头交换笔记。这事将掀起一场惊天风波，要是首相认定这是某个出狱囚犯之流的作为，你们可有得好受了。”

博伊斯做了个苦脸。

“恐怕是这样。”他说。

## III. At the Vale / 第三章 在谷园


===Original===
The Vale was situated in one of the lovely valleys of North Devon on the borders of Somerset. In the distance could be seen the Mendip Hills. Here the summer stayed on when autumn had taken hold of less favoured spots.

It was a fine old house, half-timbered, nestling in the valley, almost hidden in trees and covered with ivy. The gardens had been the special joy of Sir James Watson. It was here he led his simple home life away from the factions of Westminster and the labours of his thankless office.

He was a cold, haughty, reserved man, with few friends. His one joy in a rather lonely life was his daughter Mabel. Like so many widowers with an only daughter, he was somewhat selfish, and could never believe that she had grown up. He had watched with anxiety the attentions which had been paid to her by the many who had appeared as possible suitors.

She, unconscious of it all, had led a secluded life among her flowers; she hated the times she had to spend in the gloomy house in Town, and had no liking for London or its gaieties.

===Chinese===
谷园（The Vale）坐落在北德文郡（North Devon）与萨默塞特郡（Somerset）交界处一道秀美的山谷里。远处望得见门迪普丘陵（Mendip Hills）。当秋意已笼罩那些福分稍薄的地方时，夏天仍在这片山谷里流连。

这是一座精美的老宅，半木结构，偎在山谷之中，几乎全然掩映在树丛里，墙上爬满常春藤。花园一直是詹姆斯·沃森爵士特别的心爱之处。正是在这里，他远离威斯敏斯特的派系倾轧，远离那份吃力不讨好的差事的操劳，过着简朴的居家生活。

他是个冷淡、高傲、矜持的人，朋友寥寥。在他颇为孤寂的一生里，唯一的乐事便是女儿梅布尔。同许多只有一个女儿的鳏夫一样，他有些自私，始终不肯相信女儿已经长大成人。多少人登门献殷勤，一副有望求亲的架势，他看在眼里，忧在心头。

而她对这一切浑然不觉，只在花丛间过着与世隔绝的日子；她厌恶不得不在城里的阴沉宅邸中度过的时光，也不喜欢伦敦和它的灯红酒绿。


===Original===
Her mother had died when she was a baby, and no cloud of sorrow except one had crossed her path.

That one had been when her brother, quite a boy, had been sent down from Oxford, and her father had sternly pointed to the door, and told him never to come back till he had redeemed his character.

He had provided ample funds for the young man to make a fresh start, and had recommended him to the care of an old friend in Monte Video. He had refused to tell his daughter where the brother had gone, lest they should write to each other.

Mabel had been only a child at the time, but she never forgot her brother. As she sat in the garden after breakfast no shadow crossed her mind. The letters and papers had not arrived, as they were out of the beaten track.

===Chinese===
她母亲在她襁褓中便去世了，此后只有一件事在她的生命里投下过愁云。

那便是她的哥哥——当时还完全是个少年——被牛津大学勒令退学的那一回。父亲严厉地指着大门，叫他不挽回名声就永远别再回来。

他为这个年轻人备下了充足的钱款供他东山再起，又把他托付给蒙得维的亚（Monte Video）的一位老朋友照应。他不肯告诉女儿哥哥去了哪里，唯恐兄妹俩书信往来。

那时梅布尔还只是个孩子，可她从没忘记过哥哥。此刻她早饭之后坐在花园里，心头没掠过一丝阴影。信件和报纸还没有送到，因为这里偏处一隅，不在寻常邮路上。


===Original===
John, the butler, approached her from the house with a salver, on which he bore a visiting card.

“A gentleman wishes to see you, Miss Mabel,” he said, and handed the card. She took it and read,

Mr. Sylvester Collins,

				14, Severn Street,

				London, W.

===Chinese===
管家约翰（John）从宅子里朝她走来，手里托着一只托盘，上面盛着一张名片。

“有位先生想见您，梅布尔小姐。”他说着递上名片。她接过念道：

西尔维斯特·科林斯先生

塞文街14号

伦敦西区


===Original===
“What does he want?” said she.

“I do not know, Miss, but he asked if you could see him on an important matter. He has come down by the night train from London.”

“Oh, I’ll come and see him,” and she got up and went in.

Collins had slept on the way down, and had breakfasted on the train. He felt quite fresh after a motor ride from Wilton-on-Sea, but he had a strong distaste for his task.

===Chinese===
“他想干什么？”她说。

“我不知道，小姐，不过他问您能不能为一件要紧事见他一面。他是搭伦敦来的夜班火车下来的。”

“哦，那我去见他。”说着她起身进了屋。

科林斯在来的路上睡了一觉，早饭也是在火车上吃的。从滨海威尔顿（Wilton-on-Sea）坐汽车走完最后一段路，他觉得精神颇为清爽，只是对这桩差事满心厌恶。


===Original===
He walked up and down the fine old drawing-room, through the open windows of which came the scent of roses.

The girl entered, and he was struck with her simple beauty, without any of the adornments of the modern girl, and in her dainty morning frock of cretonne.

He knew that in a few moments her present happiness would be turned to bitter sorrow. She advanced towards him at once, and took his hand in a friendly way.

“You are a friend of my father’s, I suppose,” she said.

===Chinese===
他在那间精美的老客厅里来回踱步，敞开的窗外飘进阵阵玫瑰花香。

姑娘走了进来，她那不加修饰的天然之美令他心头一震——没有时下女郎的半点妆点，只穿一件雅致的印花布晨裙。

他知道，不消片刻，她此刻的安宁幸福就要化作锥心的悲痛。她径直向他走来，亲热地握住他的手。

“我想，您是我父亲的朋友吧。”她说。


===Original===
“Miss Watson,” he said gravely. “It is no good beating about the bush. I have some bad news for you. You must try and be brave.”

“My father,” she said, with quick instinct.

He nodded.

“Don’t tell me anything has happened to him. He only left me the day before yesterday. Is he ill?”

In her excitement she had not let go of his hand, and her fingers retained their hold.

“You must try and bear up, a terrible thing has happened. The worst that can happen.”

===Chinese===
“沃森小姐，”他郑重地说，“这事拐弯抹角没有用。我有个坏消息要告诉您。您得尽力坚强些。”

“是我父亲。”她凭着敏锐的直觉脱口说道。

他点了点头。

“别告诉我他出了什么事。前天他才离开我。他病了吗？”

激动之下，她一直没有松开他的手，手指还紧紧攥着不放。

“您一定要撑住。出了一件可怕的事，最坏的那种事。”


===Original===
“You mean,” she said, her face turning pale, “that he is dead.”

“Yes,” he said.

The shock of such an announcement does not, as a rule, have the instant effect that is supposed to take place. The mind cannot at once grasp the facts. It is like a shell wound. For a moment the wounded man gazes in surprise at a stump where his arm was a moment before. It takes some seconds before realization or pain is felt.

So it was with Mabel. It was as though someone were telling her a tale of some remote happening which did not concern her.

“How was it?” she whispered.

He had expected tears, possibly a fainting fit. This calm surprised him for the moment.

===Chinese===
“您的意思是，”她说，脸色渐渐发白，“他死了。”

“是的。”他说。

这样的噩耗，照例并不会像人们想象的那样立刻见效。头脑无法一下子领会事实。这好比炮弹创伤：伤者会一时惊愕地瞪着那截片刻前还长着手臂的残桩。要过好几秒钟，领悟和疼痛才会袭来。

梅布尔正是如此。那情形仿佛有人在给她讲一件远在天边、与她毫不相干的事。

“是怎么出的事？”她低声问。

他原本料想会有眼泪，兴许还有一场昏厥。这份平静一时叫他诧异。


===Original===
“Shall I tell you?” he said.

“Please.”

“He was found shot in his library yesterday.”

“Do you mean murdered?” she said, dismissing the thought of suicide unconsciously.

“I am afraid so,” he replied. The sweat stood on his forehead. What a fool he had been to undertake this task!

“And you have come to tell me about it? How kind of you,” she said, as in a dream. He saw this could not last, and with quick instinct rang the bell.

===Chinese===
“要我说给您听吗？”他问。

“请说吧。”

“昨天，他被发现死在书房里，是中枪死的。”

“您是说，是被人谋杀的？”她说，不知不觉间已把自杀的念头撇到了一边。

“恐怕是这样。”他答道。汗珠冒上了他的额头。自己竟揽下这趟差事，真是蠢透了！

“您特意跑来告诉我这件事？您人真好。”她像梦游似的说。他看出这种状态撑不了多久，当机立断按了铃。


===Original===
The door opened, and an old servant with a sweet face came in. She had been Mabel’s nurse, and had remained with her as a sort of companion and friend. At the sight of her something seemed to snap in the girl’s head, and she ran to her.

“Oh, Nanna,” she cried. “My father has been murdered.”

The woman looked indignantly at Collins, as though he had struck her darling, and took her in her arms, where the tears came at last.

Collins withdrew to the farther side of the room, and looked at the garden. When he turned, the room was empty.

Irresolute, he strolled into the old garden. What a catastrophe had he brought by his news! Better, perhaps, if he had wired.

===Chinese===
门开了，进来一位面容慈祥的老仆妇。她从前是梅布尔的乳母，后来一直留在她身边，算半个女伴，也是朋友。姑娘一见到她，脑子里仿佛有什么东西咔地绷断了，朝她扑了过去。

“哦，娜娜（Nanna）！”她哭喊道，“我父亲被人谋杀了。”

老仆妇愤愤地瞪了科林斯一眼，仿佛是他动手打了自己的心肝宝贝，一面把姑娘搂进怀里。泪水这才夺眶而出。

科林斯退到房间另一头，望着花园。待他回过头来，屋里已经空了。

他心神不定地踱进那座老花园。他的消息招来了怎样一场灾祸！当初若是拍个电报，或许反倒好些。


===Original===
Still, he must go through with it. He could not study the feelings of the poor girl when larger issues were at stake.

Presently he saw the butler coming towards him.

The old man was bent, and he had been crying.

“My mistress is too upset to see you, sir,” he said; “but I was to ask you to make yourself at home. And would you like some refreshment?”

“Thank you,” he replied. “I have had breakfast. I do not need anything. You have heard the sad news.”

===Chinese===
不过，事已至此，他必须把差事办到底。更大的利害关系悬在眼前，他顾不上体恤这可怜姑娘的心情了。

不多时，他看见老管家朝自己走来。

老人佝偻着身子，方才哭过一场。

“我们小姐悲痛过度，不能来见您了，先生，”他说，“不过她吩咐我请您只当在自己家里一样，别客气。您要不要用些茶点？”

“谢谢你，”他答道，“我用过早饭了，什么也不需要。你已经听说那不幸的消息了。”


===Original===
“Yes, sir, the papers have come, and the post. They all know now,” and he broke down.

“Come, come, man,” said Collins almost roughly. “It’s all right for women to cry.”

“I had known him for twenty-five years, sir,” said the old man simply, “and I wish it had been me instead of him. Do you think they will catch the murderer?”

“Surely,” said Collins. “But perhaps I had better go.”

“Oh, no, sir, Miss Mabel wants to see you when she is a bit better. She particularly asked me to say so.”

“Well, then, if so, perhaps you could give me a minute? I would like to ask a question or two.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生，报纸送来了，邮件也到了。如今大家都知道了。”说着他悲不自胜，又哭了出来。

“得了，得了，老伙计，”科林斯几乎粗暴地说，“哭哭啼啼是女人家的事。”

“我在他身边待了二十五年哪，先生，”老人朴直地说，“我恨不得死的是我，不是他。您说，他们抓得到凶手吗？”

“必定抓得到。”科林斯说，“不过，我或许还是走的好。”

“哦，别走，先生。梅布尔小姐等缓过来一些，是要见您的。她特意嘱咐我这么说。”

“那么，既然如此，你可否给我一分钟？我想问一两个问题。”


===Original===
The old butler bowed and waited.

“You were here when Sir James’ son went away?”

“Master Ronald? Oh, sir, I am sure he is nothing to do with this terrible murder.”

“Whoever said he was? But he is now heir to his father’s baronetcy even if he has been cut out of his father’s will, and we must try and find him.”

“You’ll excuse me, sir,” said the old man. “But are you a friend of the family?”

“I hope so,” said Collins cheerily, and not to commit himself.

===Chinese===
老管家躬了躬身，垂手等着。

“詹姆斯爵士的儿子离家的时候，你在府上吗？”

“罗纳德（Ronald）少爷？哦，先生，我敢担保，这桩可怕的谋杀案跟他毫无干系。”

“谁说他有干系了？不过，如今他已是父亲从男爵爵位的继承人，哪怕父亲的遗嘱里除去了他的名字——我们必须设法找到他。”

“您别见怪，先生，”老人说，“可您是这家的朋友么？”

“但愿是吧。”科林斯乐呵呵地说，不肯把话说死。


===Original===
“What exactly did young Ronald do? Anything very dreadful?” he asked.

The butler drew himself up with dignity.

“I think, sir, you had better ask someone else,” he said.

“That’s very stupid of you,” said Collins, with a smile which took the sting from his words. “You only make me believe it was something very dreadful, and I don’t expect it was at all.”

The butler was not sharp, he fell at once.

“Oh, no, sir, not at all, it was only when he was at Oxford. He went off to the ‘Derby’ without leave, and lost a lot of money there. It was what he had for the term, and when he was sent down he had to tell Sir James. He had been rather wild before, and that’s what happened,” he concluded lamely.

===Chinese===
“小罗纳德究竟干了什么？很见不得人的事么？”他问。

管家挺直了身子，摆出一副体面的架子。

“我看，先生，您还是去问别人吧。”他说。

“你这一手可不高明，”科林斯说，脸上带笑，把话里的刺都拔尽了，“你这样只会叫我相信那是件了不得的坏事，我可完全不觉得有那么严重。”

管家脑子不灵光，立刻就上了当。

“哦，不，先生，压根儿不是，那还是他在牛津念书时候的事。他未经准假就跑去看‘德比’赛马会（the Derby），在那儿输掉了一大笔钱，一学期的用度全赔了进去。被勒令退学之后，他只好向詹姆斯爵士和盘托出。他从前就颇为荒唐，事情的经过就是这样。”他末了讷讷地说。


===Original===
“Hum,” muttered Collins. “I see, and nothing has been heard from him for some time?”

“No, sir, he seems to have quite disappeared. I don’t think he is dead, or we should have heard. Still, if you were to advertise for him he might come back. He ought to be here to look after Miss Mabel till she is married.”

Collins looked up sharply.

“Is she engaged, then?” he said.

“Well, not exactly, but how my tongue does go. I must get back to the house.” He moved to go.

“One moment,” said Collins quietly. “You can trust me; who is the fortunate gentleman who is﻿—well﻿—nearly, eh?”

===Chinese===
“唔，”科林斯低声道，“我明白了。这阵子一直没有他的音信？”

“没有，先生，他像是彻底没了踪影。我想他没死，不然总该有消息传来。不过，您要是登报寻他，他说不定就回来了。他也该在这儿照应梅布尔小姐，直到她出阁。”

科林斯倏地抬起头来。

“这么说，她订了亲？”他问。

“呃，倒也说不准。瞧我这舌头，就是管不住。我真得回宅子去了。”说着他抬脚要走。

“且慢，”科林斯不动声色地说，“尽管信我；那位有福气的绅士是谁——呃——就是差不多要成的那么一位，嗯？”


===Original===
The butler looked at him doubtfully. Had he offered a bribe he would have refused information, but Collins was too old a hand for that.

“Well, seeing as poor Sir James is gone, I don’t think it matters. It is Mr. Eric.”

“Mr. Eric what?”

“I thought you would know, being a friend of the family. Mr. Eric Sanders, Sir James’ private secretary,” and he looked at Collins with suspicion.

He saw the look. “Oh, that’s it,” said he. “Of course, I ought to have guessed, and how does his suit prosper?”

“I beg your pardon, sir?” said the other.

===Chinese===
管家将信将疑地望着他。倘若科林斯掏钱行贿，他反倒会守口如瓶，可科林斯是何等老练的角色，才不使那等手段。

“唉，可怜的詹姆斯爵士既然去了，我想说说也不要紧了。是埃里克（Eric）先生。”

“埃里克先生，姓什么？”

“我还当您知道呢，您不是这家的朋友嘛。是埃里克·桑德斯（Eric Sanders）先生，詹姆斯爵士的私人秘书。”说着，他狐疑地打量了科林斯一眼。

他察觉了那副神色。“哦，原来如此，”他说，“我本该猜到的。他求亲求得如何？”

“您说什么，先生？”对方问。


===Original===
“I mean are they engaged, or just likely to become so?”

“Sir James wouldn’t hear of it, and last time Mr. Eric was here they had words over it, for I heard them, but I must really be going.”

“All right, John, I will wait here till Miss Mabel wants to see me. You might bring me any papers you have.” The butler bowed and made his way to the house.

“So that’s it, is it?” he said to himself. “There are at least two candidates for honours. We are getting on.” The papers told him nothing. Sinclair had been to work, and apart from a bald statement of the facts, and obituary notices, there was nothing striking. Of course, there were leading articles on the perils of foreign anarchists and on the saintly character of the deceased, but this was old stock-in-trade, kept ready for any assassination of a notable person which might occur, and adapted to circumstances.

“As long as this country continues to harbour, etc.,” said one Daily. He tossed them aside, one by one. Sir James, of course, had a good selection of papers sent to his house, and they arrived whether he was there or not.

===Chinese===
“我是说，他们是已经订了亲，还是眼看就要订亲？”

“詹姆斯爵士死活不松口。上一回埃里克先生来的时候，两人还为这事起了口角，我都听见了。不过我真得走了。”

“好，约翰，我就在这儿等梅布尔小姐要见我。手头有什么报纸，都给我拿来吧。”管家躬身领命，往宅子去了。

“原来如此，是吧？”他自言自语道，“这份荣耀至少有两位候选人。咱们有进展了。”报纸没告诉他任何东西。辛克莱已经做过一番工作，除了干巴巴的事实通报和几则讣告之外，并无醒目的内容。当然，社论是少不了的，一谈外国无政府主义者的祸患，二颂死者的圣徒品格——不过这全是现成的老套货色，但凡有要人遇刺便可随时取用，因时因势稍加改削而已。

“只要这个国家继续窝藏，云云。”某家日报写道。他把报纸一份份丢到一旁。当然，詹姆斯爵士府上订着齐全的各色报纸，无论他在家不在家，照送不误。


===Original===
He sat long in deep thought, smoking continuously. Presently he put his hand into his pocket, and drew out his pocket book. He looked round with his habitual caution, and then took out a visiting card. On one side was the name of Mr. Eric Sanders, and the address of a well-known London Club, and on the other was written in pencil﻿—

“For God’s sake, see me. I will not detain you.”

“Sinclair, my friend, you would have liked to get this﻿—pushed under the door. Mrs. Simmons, you were not telling the whole truth. I think this requires further investigation.”

===Chinese===
他久久坐着出神，烟一支接一支地抽。过了一会儿，他把手伸进口袋，掏出记事本来。他带着惯常的谨慎四下里张了张，然后抽出一张名片。名片一面是埃里克·桑德斯先生的名字，和一家伦敦知名俱乐部的地址；另一面用铅笔写着——

“看在上帝份上，见见我吧。我不会耽搁您的。”

“辛克莱，我的朋友，你一定很想拿到这个——从门底下塞进来的。西蒙斯太太，你没把实话全说出来。依我看，这事还需要深究下去。”


===Original===
He rose from his seat and strolled through the old garden with its gorgeous, herbaceous beds of late summer, where delphiniums and hollyhocks and the bright blue of borage made a dream of colour.

It was all very fair, and quiet after the dust and sweat of London. He returned to the house filled with a vague disquiet. Entering the hall, he was met by a maid.

“Miss Mabel would like to see you in her own room,” she said, and on his nodding assent she conducted him to a sweet sitting room, fragrant with flowers and furnished with the taste of a girl who had the means to gratify her every wish.

She was seated on a sofa, white faced, and dressed all in black.

===Chinese===
他从座位上起身，信步穿过老花园。晚夏的花境正开得绚烂，飞燕草、蜀葵，还有琉璃苣的亮蓝，织成一片色彩的幻梦。

这一切秀美宜人，比起伦敦的尘土与汗热，安静得多了。他回到宅子里，心头笼着一层莫名的忐忑。刚进门厅，一名女仆迎了上来。

“梅布尔小姐想在她自己的房间里见您。”她说。他点头应允，女仆便领他来到一间雅致的起居室，满室花香，陈设处处见出一位有财力随心所欲的姑娘的品位。

她坐在一张沙发上，面无血色，一身黑衣。


===Original===
She had conquered her emotion. Her old nurse stood by her like a sentry on duty.

“Mr. Collins,” she said: “I am puzzled to know why you undertook this long journey to break this sad news to me. Were you a friend of my father’s? I am very grateful,” she continued hastily, as though fearing she was too frigid in her manner.

“Really, to tell the truth, I don’t know myself why I came,” he answered. “When this terrible event happened, your old housekeeper was quite unnerved, and there seemed no one to undertake the job. It did not seem right that you should see it first in the papers, or get a telegram.”

“I am much obliged to you. You must not think me ungrateful, but of course I am rather upset at present. I have read what the newspapers have to say. Perhaps you can tell me more?” and she motioned him to sit.

===Chinese===
她已把情绪制服了。老乳母侍立在旁，像一名值岗的哨兵。

“科林斯先生，”她说，“我实在纳闷，您为什么要跑这么远的路，来向我传达这个噩耗。您是我父亲的朋友吗？我很感激。”她赶忙又补上一句，像是怕自己的态度显得太冷淡了。

“说实话，连我自己也不知道为什么来。”他答道，“这可怕的事情发生时，你们的老管家整个人都吓垮了，又似乎没有人能担当这件事。总不能让您最先从报纸上看到，或是接到一封电报了事。”

“太谢谢您了。您千万别当我不知好歹，只是我此刻心绪实在不宁。报纸上说的话我都看过了。也许您能告诉我更多？”说着，她示意他坐下。


===Original===
“I won’t go into details, Miss Watson,” he said. “The accounts in the papers are accurate as far as they go. I can, however, tell you this. Your father did not suffer at all. His look was most peaceful, and it appears that he was shot while asleep.”

A look of pain crossed her face, but she mastered her emotion.

“I am thankful for even that,” she said. “Have the police any idea at all who can have done this cruel thing. I do not believe my father had any enemies, he was such a good and upright man that no one could have a grudge against him.”

“At present all is dark,” he replied, “but of course you must remember that as Home Secretary your father was brought in contact with the worst criminals in the country, and one of them may have been trying to avenge a fancied wrong. Then, again, it may have been the work of a lunatic. That is more than probable.”

“In a way I hope it was,” she said. “One could feel that it was the sort of accident that might happen to anyone. It is so dreadful to think that someone has deliberately murdered him.”

===Chinese===
“细节我就不说了，沃森小姐，”他说，“报纸的报道就其所载而言是准确的。不过有一点我可以告诉您：令尊没有受什么苦。他的面容极为安详，看来是在熟睡中中枪的。”

一丝痛楚掠过她的脸庞，但她把情绪压住了。

“单凭这一点，我就已经感恩了。”她说，“警方到底有没有一点头绪，知道是谁下了这样的毒手？我不信父亲有什么仇家。他是那样善良、正直的一个人，谁也不至于记恨他。”

“眼下一切还是一团漆黑。”他答道，“不过您当然得记住，令尊身为内政大臣，打过交道的都是全国最凶恶的罪犯，其中某一个，也许就是在为一桩凭空想象的冤仇报复。再者，这也可能是疯子的手笔。这种可能性反倒更大。”

“从某方面说，我倒希望是那样。”她说，“那样还能觉得，这是谁都可能碰上的一种意外。一想到有人处心积虑地谋害了他，就实在太可怕了。”


===Original===
She stumbled over the ill-omened word, and nearly broke down. The watchful nurse came near and laid a hand on her head. A look of gratitude shone for a moment in her eyes, and she reached up and took the hand in hers.

It was a pathetic picture.

“You will forgive me asking,” she continued, “but I do not quite see what you were doing there, Mr. Collins, you are not in the Police Force?”

“I am a barrister by profession,” he replied, “and had gone there with Superintendent Sinclair, who is an old friend of mine. Now, can I do anything for you before I go? You will forgive a stranger saying so, but you seem so entirely alone. Oh, I know you have the most loyal and faithful servants,” he added hastily, “but you don’t seem to have a friend to help you. Haven’t you some relation I can wire for?”

===Chinese===
说到那个不祥的字眼，她磕绊了一下，几乎支撑不住。警觉的乳母走上前来，把一只手放在她头上。她眼中霎时闪过一抹感激，抬手把那只手握在自己手里。

这是一幅令人心酸的景象。

“请您原谅我多问，”她接着说，“可我实在不大明白，您当时在那里做什么呢，科林斯先生？您并不是警察里的人吧？”

“我的本职是个大律师，”他答道，“那天是同辛克莱警司一起去的，他是我的老朋友。那么，在我走之前，还能为您效点什么劳吗？恕我一个外人直言——您看上去实在太孤单了。哦，我知道您有最忠心可靠的仆人，”他赶忙补了一句，“可您身边似乎没有一个能帮衬的朋友。有没有哪位亲眷，我可以替您拍电报请来？”


===Original===
“I have no near relative. We have led a very secluded life. You see we are so much in town. My father had many acquaintances, but no real friends. Those who did not know him thought him very reserved. He was not really so, you know.”

“You were an only child?” he said carelessly.

“Mr. Collins, I am going to tell you. It will all come out now. I had a brother, ten years older than I. He quarrelled with my father. It was nothing very dreadful, but father thought he was doing no good and getting into bad company, so he sent him off to South America. For some years now we have lost sight of him. It was a great grief to father. He had hoped that Ronald would have come back and settled down here.”

“Well, we must find him now, as he will be the new Baronet, and there will be advertisements everywhere for him. I suppose there is no reason why he should not come back?”

“None whatever,” she said proudly. “What he did was only a boy’s escapade when at Oxford, there was nothing criminal.”

===Chinese===
“我没有近亲。我们一直过着深居简出的日子。您知道，我们在城里待的时间太多了。父亲相识满天下，却没有一个真正的朋友。不了解他的人都觉得他非常矜持。其实他并不是那样的人，您知道的。”

“您是独生的吧？”他闲闲地问。

“科林斯先生，我这就告诉您吧。反正这事如今也瞒不住了。我有过一个哥哥，比我大十岁。他同父亲吵翻了。倒不是什么天大的坏事，只是父亲觉得他不务正业，又交上了坏朋友，便把他打发去了南美。这几年，我们完全失去了他的音讯。这是父亲一桩很大的伤心事。他一直盼着罗纳德能回来，在这里成家立业。”

“那么，现在我们必须找到他，因为他将是新的从男爵，四面八方都会登出寻人启事来。我想，他没有理由不回来吧？”

“毫无理由。”她自豪地说，“他干的那点事，不过是少年人在牛津时的一回荒唐，毫无犯罪可言。”


===Original===
“Well, I expect there will be little difficulty in finding him now,” he said hopefully; “but it will take some time. Meanwhile, isn’t there anyone who could help?”

The colour rose to her pale face.

“I think you ought to wire for Mr. Sanders,” she said, “he was my father’s private secretary, and knows more about his affairs than anyone else.”

Collins gave her one keen look. “Certainly,” he said. “He is obviously the man to come. Where shall I find him?”

“At the Home Office,” she said. “He is certain to be there, but I expect he has been round to Leveson Square this morning.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，我想如今要找到他不会有多大困难。”他满怀希望地说，“只是要费些时日。眼下这段时间，可有人能来帮衬您么？”

血色涌上了她苍白的脸。

“我想您应当拍电报请桑德斯先生来，”她说，“他是我父亲的私人秘书，比谁都清楚父亲的事务。”

科林斯锐利地看了她一眼。“当然，”他说，“显然该来的就是他。我到哪里能找到他？”

“在内政部。”她说，“他一定在那儿，不过我想，今天早上他准已去过勒维森广场了。”


===Original===
“I will send off a wire at once, and then I will take my leave.”

“Certainly not,” she said. “You must stay to lunch, if you don’t mind a house of mourning,” she added sadly.

At that moment a knock came at the door, and the butler entered.

“The post, Miss,” he said, presenting a salver, “and the postman brought this telegram at the same time. Is there any answer?”

She broke the envelope and read, a look of pleasure passing over her face.

===Chinese===
“我这就去发一封电报，然后就告辞。”

“那可不行。”她说，“您一定要留下用午饭——倘若您不介意这是一户居丧之家的话。”她哀然补充道。

正在这时有人敲门，管家走了进来。

“邮件到了，小姐。”他端上托盘说，“邮差同时送来了这封电报。要回电吗？”

她撕开信封读过，脸上掠过一丝喜色。


===Original===
“This is from Eric﻿—Mr. Sanders, he is coming down here today. I am so glad. It will save you the trouble of wiring.”

Collins said nothing. The butler had handed him a letter in Sinclair’s writing. He put it into his pocket, and rose to his feet. “I am very glad for your sake,” he said. “You will be glad to have a man’s advice. I suppose you will be coming to Town?”

“Of course. I ought to go at once, but it is such a shock. I think I must wait till tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“这是埃里克发来的——桑德斯先生。他今天就下来。我太高兴了。也省得您费事拍电报了。”

科林斯没有作声。管家方才还递给他一封信，是辛克莱的笔迹。他把信揣进口袋，站起身来。“我为您高兴。”他说，“有位先生替您拿拿主意，您会宽慰些。您是要进城去吧？”

“当然。我本该立刻就去，只是这打击太重了。我想必须等到明天。”


===Original===
“If you will excuse me, I will just go and read this letter, then,” he said, and took his departure.

He went into the garden and to his old seat, and broke the seal of the letter.

It was short, and he read it twice, a puzzled look on his face. It ran:

===Chinese===
“那么恕我失陪，我这就去读读这封信。”说完他便告退了。

他走进花园，回到原先那张座椅上，拆开了信的封缄。

信很短，他读了两遍，一脸困惑。信上写道：


===Original===
Dear Collins,

If you are expecting to find out anything in Devonshire, you are on a wild goose chase. Lewis has fled, and we have damning evidence against him. Come at once if you want to be in at the death. What’s your game, anyway?

Yours in haste,

A. Sinclair.

“I must get back,” he muttered to himself. “Whatever is Sinclair after?”

===Chinese===
亲爱的科林斯：

你要是指望在德文郡查出点什么，那你是在白费力气追野鹅。刘易斯已经逃了，我们手里握着他不利的铁证。想亲眼见到收网，就即刻回来。你到底在打什么主意？

匆匆。

亚瑟·辛克莱

“我必须回去了。”他喃喃自语，“辛克莱究竟在追查什么？”


===Original===
A gong sounded within the house, and he slowly rose to his feet and went in. Miss Watson was waiting for him, and they sat down. She was lost in her own mournful thoughts, and would scarcely eat anything. She tried hard to rouse herself. Collins was a brilliant conversationalist, and had a charm of manner which few could resist. He set himself to interest her, not without success.

At the end of the meal he told her he must get back at once, and noticed that she gave a look almost of relief, though she tried to hide it.

“I am deeply grateful to you for coming down here, and for your offer of help,” she said.

===Chinese===
宅子里响起了锣声，他缓缓起身走进屋去。沃森小姐正等着他，两人入席。她沉浸在自己的哀思里，几乎什么也吃不下，却仍竭力振作。科林斯是位谈吐生风的妙人，风度又有一种鲜有人能抵挡的魅力。他存心引她说笑，倒也并非全无成效。

一餐将尽，他告诉她自己必须立刻赶回去，同时注意到，她眼中几乎闪过一丝如释重负的神色，尽管她极力掩藏。

“您专程赶下来，又肯慨然相助，我深为感激。”她说。


===Original===
“Not at all,” he answered. “I will go to your house and do anything I can in London. Of course, there will have to be an inquest, but we will spare you all we can.”

“We?” she said, in surprise. “Then you are mixed up in this?”

“Oh, there is no secret,” he said. “I am a barrister, as I told you, but I do a little in helping in an amateur way with these sort of cases. It is my hobby.”

“A rather horrible hobby,” she said, “but of course it is necessary. I hope you find out the criminal﻿—and yet, I don’t know, in some ways I hope you don’t.”

“The murderer of your father,” he blurted out, scandalised at such sentiments.

===Chinese===
“哪儿的话。”他答道，“我会到您府上去，在伦敦尽我所能。当然，一场审讯是免不了的，但我们会尽力让您少受煎熬。”

“我们？”她诧异地说，“这么说，您也牵涉在这案子里？”

“哦，这没有什么好保密的。”他说，“正如我说过的，我是个大律师，不过这类案子，我也以业余的身份帮点忙。这是我的癖好。”

“一个怪可怕的癖好，”她说，“可这当然有其必要。我盼望你们查出凶手——可是，我说不好，从某些方面讲，我又盼望你们查不出来。”

“杀害您父亲的凶手啊。”他脱口而出，这等念头令他骇然。


===Original===
“Oh, I know I ought to want him punished, and yet, the awful trial, the cold cell, and then the last horrible scene. I am afraid I am always on the side of the criminal. Of course, you think that’s dreadful.”

“I think it does more justice to your heart than to your head,” said he with a smile.

“English justice is such a cold, merciless thing. When I hear of people who come forward to what they call ‘further the ends of justice,’ I always think it is either for notoriety or for reward.”

He laughed.

===Chinese===
“哦，我知道我本该盼他受罚。可是，那可怕的审判，冰冷的牢房，还有最后那恐怖的一幕。只怕我永远站在罪犯那一边。您一定觉得这念头可怕极了。”

“我只是觉得，这番话见出您的心肠，胜过您的头脑。”他含笑说。

“英国的司法是何等冷酷、无情的东西。每当听说有人挺身而出，去做他们所谓的‘伸张正义’，我总觉得那不图名，便图利。”

他笑出了声。


===Original===
“It’s a good thing everyone does not think as you do,” said he.

“I was taught as a child that vengeance belongs to God, not to man, and I believe it is a worse punishment to leave the criminal to his conscience than to punish him.”

“You say that because you have never come in contact with the real criminal,” said he. “He has no conscience.”

“I don’t believe that. I am sure I would always rather hide a fugitive from justice than give him up.”

===Chinese===
“幸亏不是人人都有您这番想法。”他说。

“我从小受的教导是：复仇在上帝，不在人。而且我相信，把罪犯留给他的良心去责罚，比惩办他更为严厉。”

“您这么说，是因为您从未接触过真正的罪犯。”他说，“那种人没有良心。”

“我不信。我敢说，换了是我，宁可把逃犯藏起来，也决不交出去。”


===Original===
“This is rank treason,” he said; “but I admire your sentiments.”

“But don’t agree with them?”

“We must each of us act according to our lights,” he answered more gravely than he intended.

===Chinese===
“这可是不折不扣的叛逆之论。”他说，“不过我钦佩您的情怀。”

“却不肯苟同？”

“我们每个人都得凭自己的良知行事。”他答道，语气比本意郑重了几分。


===Original===
She held out her hand.

“If you will excuse me, I will say goodbye. The car will be ready for you, and, I hope, we shall meet again in happier circumstances,” and she gave a pathetic little smile.

When she had gone, he stood where he was.

“What a fool I was to start bandying words with her in her present state. Now for London. You’ve no time for sentiment.”


===Chinese===
她伸出了手。

“恕我失陪，就此道别。汽车会替您备好。但愿我们再度相见时，境况会快活些。”说着，她凄然淡淡一笑。

她走后，他仍伫立原地。

“我真是个蠢货，在她这般境况下，竟还同她唇枪舌剑起来。这就回伦敦。你可没工夫伤什么感怀了。”

## IV. The Missing Letter / 第四章 失踪的信


===Original===
Sinclair was sitting at his desk, and his brows were knitted. Before him was a letter.
