He read it over again for the third time, and then told the clock that he was damned. Then he picked up the envelope, and examined it closely.

It was the morning after the murder.

This was what he read.

===Chinese===
辛克莱坐在书桌前，眉头紧锁。面前摆着一封信。

他把这封信重读了一遍——已经是第三遍——然后对着挂钟骂了一声“该死”。接着他拿起信封，仔细端详起来。

这是凶案发生后的第二天早晨。

信的内容如下。

===Original===
89, Leveson Square,

					London, W.

Dear Mr. Sinclair,

I am writing to you, but I have grave doubts whether this letter will ever reach you, and therefore, I am not telling you more than necessary. I am in the hands of one of the cleverest ruffians that this generation has produced. My life is in imminent danger if it is not already forfeited. There is not time for explanations.

Follow these instructions carefully.

===Chinese===
勒维森广场 89 号，

伦敦西区

亲爱的辛克莱先生：

我提笔给您写这封信，却十分怀疑它能否送到您手上，因此，我只说必要的话，绝不多讲。我已落入当今这一代最狡猾的恶棍手中。我的性命危在旦夕——倘若不是早已不保的话。没有时间解释。

请仔细遵照下列指示行事。

===Original===
Find my son who has disappeared for some years, but was last heard of in Monte Video. Tell him to look in the place where I hid my will in his presence, and he will find all the necessary documents to bring a great criminal to justice. I cannot be more specific. I am writing to you because I know you have done good service and are painstaking. Boyce is a fool. Sylvester Collins is a theorist who will be no help to you in this matter. Do not consult him. If I am dead when this reaches you, act as I have said. If nothing has happened, I rely on your honour to destroy this letter and I will send for you. I am very tired.

Yours faithfully,

James Watson.

===Chinese===
去找我那失踪数年的儿子，最后听说他在蒙得维的亚（Monte Video）。让他到当年我当着他的面藏遗嘱的地方去找，他会找到一切必要的文件，足以将一个大罪犯绳之以法。我不能说得再具体了。我之所以写信给您，是因为我知道您屡建功绩、办案勤勉。博伊斯是个蠢货。西尔维斯特·科林斯是个只会空谈的理论家，在这件事上帮不了您什么忙。不要同他商量。此信送到时我若已身亡，请照我说的去做。若什么事都没发生，我全凭您的名誉，请销毁此信，我会派人去请您。我实在疲惫不堪。

您忠实的，

詹姆斯·沃森

===Original===
Sinclair sat long over this letter. What a vista of happenings did it conjure up. He was a plain man. Why did not Sir James write plainly, give the name of the criminal straight out and save further bother? Why all this mystery? What had happened in that grim library in the afternoon? Oh, bother it all, what a maze of evidence. If only it had been a straightforward murder, with plenty of blood and clues as in a detective story.

No; he would not tell Collins. He had something up his sleeve﻿—well, let them both follow their own line.

He took a pen and paper, and put down his facts. Here was one thing cleared up. This was the letter which Sir James had posted himself, after his interview with the unknown man.

===Chinese===
辛克莱对着这封信枯坐良久。它勾起了多少风云变幻啊。他是个直性子的人。詹姆斯爵士为什么不写得明明白白，直接把凶手的名字说出来，省去这些麻烦？何必这样故弄玄虚？那天下午，那间阴森的书房里到底出了什么事？唉，烦死了，这团证据的乱麻。但愿这只是一桩简简单单的谋杀案，像侦探小说里那样血迹遍地、线索齐全，该有多好。

不；他不会告诉科林斯。他自己也藏着牌——好吧，就让他们两人各查各的线索吧。

他取来纸笔，把已知的事实一一记下。有一件事总算弄清楚了。这就是詹姆斯爵士与那陌生男子会面之后，亲自去投寄的那封信。

===Original===
That, at any rate, corroborated the housekeeper’s evidence. Then the visitor had threatened his life; if not, why was the danger hanging over him so greatly that he dare not venture further than the post?

A message was brought in. It was a wire from Collins to say he was on the way to London. “Do nothing till I come,” it ended.

“That’s like his cheek,” said Sinclair to himself.

===Chinese===
这一点至少印证了女管家的证词。这么说来，那来访者必定威胁过他的性命；不然，那危险为何沉沉压顶，吓得他除了去投信之外，哪儿也不敢去？

有人送进来一份电文。是科林斯拍来的电报，说他已在来伦敦的路上。电文末尾一句是：“我来之前，什么都别做。”

“好大的架子。”辛克莱暗自说道。

===Original===
He put the letter carefully away in his pocket book, and took his hat and stick. “I am going to Leveson Square,” said he to the messenger. “There is no answer.”

Once inside the house, through the usual, morbid crowd who gathered outside, he met the plainclothes officer on duty.

“Anything to report?” he said, in answer to the other’s salute.

===Chinese===
他把信小心收进记事本，取了帽子和手杖。“我这就去勒维森广场，”他对送信人说，“不必回电。”

穿过外面照例聚集的那群病态看热闹的人群，他一进宅子，便见到了当班的便衣警员。

他一边向对方还礼，一边问：“有什么要报告的吗？”

===Original===
“No, sir,” said he. “They are still at work on the floor and ceiling and the walls.”

A gang of expert men had been engaged to search for a means of exit from the room by which the murderer had escaped. There had been found no trace of a secret door, or so much as a crevice through which a mouse could get. They had even ripped up all the boards, and taken off the oak panelling from the walls. The ceiling had been examined all over and the chimney sounded. There was nothing.

“Have you found anything in the nature of a letter or anything, anywhere?” he asked of the man in charge.

===Chinese===
“没有，长官。”对方说。“他们还在查验地板、天花板和四面的墙壁。”

一队行家里手受聘前来，搜寻凶手逃脱的出口。结果连暗门的痕迹都没找到，甚至连一条能容老鼠通过的缝隙也没有。他们甚至撬起了全部地板，卸下了墙上的橡木护壁板。天花板整个查验过，烟囱也探测过了。一无所获。

“有没有在任何地方发现类似信件之类的东西？”他问那位负责人。

===Original===
“Nothing, sir, but we can search all the furniture and books.”

“Please do so. It is of great importance, and say nothing about it to anyone.”

“Very good, sir,” said the man who was keen on this job, and wanted to stand well with the superintendent.

===Chinese===
“没有，长官，不过我们还可以把全部家具和书籍搜一遍。”

“请务必照办。此事关系重大，对任何人都不要透露一个字。”

“遵命，长官。”那人说。他正热衷于这件差事，一心想给警司留个好印象。

===Original===
Sinclair addressed the plainclothes officer.

“Has the body been searched?”

“Yes, sir. All that was found on him has been put on the dining-room table.”

“Good,” he said, and went into the room.

Here were all the little things a man carries about with him, which look so pitiable when he is dead. A fountain pen, pocket book, cigar case, and a leather case containing a miniature of his dead wife and his daughter.

===Chinese===
辛克莱转向那名便衣警员。

“尸体搜检过了吗？”

“是，长官。从他身上找到的所有物品都摆在餐厅的桌子上了。”

“好。”他说着，走进了那个房间。

这里摆着一个男人随身的那些零星物件——人一死，它们看上去便格外凄凉。一支自来水笔、一本记事本、一只雪茄盒，还有一只皮匣，里面装着他亡妻和女儿的一幅袖珍画像。

## V. A Mysterious Visit / 第五章 神秘的来访

===Original===
By dining on the train, Collins had just time to do a Gilbert and Sullivan opera. He had a seat permanently booked, which was to be disposed of if he did not turn up in time.

After the excitement of his daily life, he found these plays, which he knew almost by heart, very refreshing. It was the dear old *Yeoman of the Guard* this night, and he lay back and listened with his eyes half shut, absorbing the delicious tunes like a rare old vintage.

“It is easier to die well than to live well, for in sooth I have tried both,” says Colonel Fairfax.

How many cases he had come across in his work where this was true. Some of the worst of men had earned the admiration of men by their brave end.

===Chinese===
在火车上用过晚餐，科林斯的时间刚好够看一场吉尔伯特与沙利文（Gilbert and Sullivan）的轻歌剧。他在剧场常年订着一个座位，若他未能及时到场，这个座位便转让出去。

日常生活紧张刺激惯了，这些他几乎能背出台词的戏，反倒令他心神一爽。今晚演的是那出心爱的老戏《皇家卫兵》（*Yeoman of the Guard*），他半阖着眼仰在椅背里，像品味一款稀有的陈年佳酿，把悦耳的曲调细细汲取。

“死得体面比活得体面容易，老实说，这两样我都试过。”费尔法克斯上校（Colonel Fairfax）唱道。

干他这一行，经手过多少案子，这句话都应验了。有些十恶不赦之徒，就凭临死前的勇敢，赢得了世人的钦佩。

===Original===
He made his way home in the purple night through back streets and half-lighted squares which he always preferred to the rush and dazzling brightness of West London, when he had thinking to do.

He arrived at his flat to find Sinclair waiting for him, as he had almost expected.

“You’re a nice fellow,” said the latter. “I’ve been waiting for you for over an hour. Your man did not know where you had gone.”

“Anything wrong,” said the other, carelessly.

“Wrong,” said Sinclair. “I should think there was. You can’t have a Home Secretary murdered for nothing. The Premier sent for Boyce this morning, and half the Cabinet have been round or calling up. They all have ‘theories’ which they want us to work out.﻿ ﻿… Luckily, Boyce is in his element, and professes great hopes of capture and all that sort of thing.”

===Chinese===
紫色的夜色里，他穿过后街和灯影疏落的广场往家走——每逢要思考事情，他总爱走这些地方，而不喜欢西伦敦那喧嚣刺目的繁华。

回到公寓，他发现辛克莱正在等他——这几乎在他意料之中。

“你可真够朋友，”后者说。“我等了你一个多钟头了。你那仆人不知道你上哪儿去了。”

“出什么事了吗？”另一个漫不经心地说。

“出事？”辛克莱说。“可不是嘛。堂堂内政大臣遭人谋害，总不能算没事。今天早上首相把博伊斯召了去，半数内阁成员不是登门便是打电话。他们人人都有‘理论’，要我们照着推演。……好在博伊斯正得其所哉，满口宣称大有希望破案，诸如此类。

===Original===
Collins helped Sinclair to a generous whiskey and soda, took a more modest one for himself, and sat down.

“Now let’s hear all about it,” he said.

“Well,” said the other. “We have done a good deal of spade work, and the negative results are of use anyhow, though our many critics would not say so. First, as to the room. It has been so thoroughly examined that there is no possibility of the murderer having got out by any secret means.”

“I could have told you that,” said Collins almost contemptuously.

“How?” said the other.

“Well, nowadays, people in modern London houses do not have trap doors and secret panels, and all that sort of thing. That’s kept for detective stories.”

===Chinese===
科林斯给辛克莱调上一大杯威士忌苏打，自己倒了杯小些的，坐了下来。

“好了，说来听听吧。”他说。

“唔，”对方说。“我们做了大量基础的排查工作，虽然批评我们的人不这么看，但排除掉种种可能总归有用。首先是那个房间。已经查得非常彻底，凶手绝无可能经由任何秘密途径出去。”

“这一点我本可以直接告诉你们。”科林斯说，语气近乎轻蔑。

“怎么说？”对方问。

“哼，如今伦敦的现代宅邸里，谁家也不会有活板门、暗格嵌板之类的东西。那是留给侦探小说用的。”

===Original===
“Then how in the world did he come and go?”

“I can’t tell you. That’s what we’ve got to find out.”

“Perhaps Mrs. Simmons was an accomplice?”

“Not necessarily, but how did you get on with her?”

“She came, and you remember Boyce said he was going to examine her himself. The great man was engaged with higher game, and I did the examining. We got her whole statement down, and turned her inside out. I am sure she was telling the truth. She had nothing to add to what she said yesterday.”

===Chinese===
“那他究竟是怎么进去、又怎么出来的？”

“我说不上来。这正是我们要查明的。”

“莫非西蒙斯太太是同谋？”

“未必。你跟她谈得怎么样？”

“她来了。你总记得吧，博伊斯本说要亲自讯问她。那位大人物忙着应付更要紧的对象，讯问便由我来进行。我们录下她的完整口供，把她里里外外盘问了个透。我敢肯定她说的是实话。除了昨天讲过的，她再没有补充。”

===Original===
Collins grunted.

Sinclair looked at him for a moment, then continued.

“We could get nothing further about the letter sent to the Central News. It was, as you said, posted at Bridge Street, but of course no one can say any more than that. I have sent it to an expert on typewriting to see whether we can ascertain what machine was used.”

“Good.”

“The telephone has given us nothing. They cannot say where the call came from, and it was probably a Public Telephone Office. What a nerve the man had.”

===Chinese===
科林斯哼了一声。

辛克莱看了他片刻，又接着往下讲。

“寄给中央新闻社的那封信，再没有查出更多。如你所说，信是在桥街（Bridge Street）投寄的，但当然也仅止于此，谁都说不出更多。我已把信送交一位打字鉴定专家，看能否查明用的是哪台机器。”

“很好。”

“电话那头一无所获。他们说不清电话从哪儿打来，多半是公共电话局。那家伙好大的胆子。”

===Original===
“Yes, but it was clever. Haven’t you noticed that where there is nothing but the bare deed, it is easier to concentrate on that, but all these extraneous matters lead one away from the essentials?

“Now I see you are dying to tell me. What about Lewis?”

Sinclair gave a start. This man’s instinct was uncanny.

“As I told you, Lewis has bolted. He did not turn up again yesterday, and I sent a man to see if he was ill. He had gone home, coolly packed his things and paid his bill, telling his landlady he would not be back, and gone.”

===Chinese===
“是的，不过这一手很聪明。你注意到没有：当手头只剩下一桩光秃秃的罪行时，人反而容易集中心思于其上；而这些节外生枝的东西，只会把人引离本质。”

“现在我看你憋得急不可耐了。刘易斯怎么样？”

辛克莱吓了一跳。此人的直觉准得吓人。

“正如我告诉你的，刘易斯跑路了。昨天他没再露面，我派了个人去看看他是不是病了。原来他回过住处，不慌不忙收拾好东西、结清账目，跟房东太太说不再回来了，然后人就走了。”

===Original===
“And so you think he is the murderer?”

“It is suspicious, but you have not heard all. Of course, this, coupled with his curious manner the day before, caused me to make enquiries. Two important facts have come to light. On the afternoon of the murder he told his typist that he was going out. He was very restless, and said he could not work, and then he seemed to come to a decision, and said, ‘I must go and see Sir James Watson,’ and took his hat and stick.”

“When was this?” said Collins, leaning forward.

“About 2:30, and he did not return till just before I sent for him after the telephone message came.”

Collins laughed.

“Then, you suppose that, having planned the murder and written the letter saying that it had taken place, and posted it, he tells his typist he is going to do it and comes back in time to call you up, and me too, and then answers your bell.”

===Chinese===
“于是你就认定他是凶手？”

“确有嫌疑，可你还没听完。当然，这事加上他前一天的古怪举止，促使我展开调查。两件重要事实浮出水面。凶案当天下午，他告诉打字员说自己要出去。他坐立不安，说没法干活，随后像是拿定了主意，说：‘我得去见詹姆斯·沃森爵士。’说完拿起帽子和手杖。”

“这是什么时候？”科林斯俯身向前问。

“2:30 左右。直到电话通报之后我派人去传他，他才在传唤前不久赶回来。”

科林斯笑了。

“那么，你的设想是：他计划好谋杀，写好那封宣告凶案已成的信并且投寄出去，然后告诉打字员他要去动手，再及时赶回来打电话把你叫去、把我也叫去，最后还应你传唤的铃声赶到。”

===Original===
“I suppose nothing,” said Sinclair, nettled. “I am giving you facts, and I haven’t done.”

“Fire away, then.”

“When we searched his rooms after he had gone, his landlady said he always kept a revolver in the top drawer in his bedroom. The day before yesterday she saw him take it out, and put it in his pocket.”

“My dear fellow, this is too crude for words. Couldn’t he have shut his door, or taken some precaution?”

“Of course, I know that, but it is this sort of mistake which hangs men.”

===Chinese===
“我什么设想都没有，”辛克莱动了气。“我给你摆的是事实，而且还没摆完。”

“那就接着说。”

“他跑掉之后，我们搜了他的房间。房东太太说，他卧室的头层抽屉里一直放着一把左轮手枪。前天她亲眼看见他取出来，揣进了口袋。”

“亲爱的伙计，这拙劣得没话说了。他难道不会关上门，或是采取点防备措施？”

“这个我当然知道。可正是这类失误，把人送上绞架。”

===Original===
“Well, go on.”

“That’s all, but it is enough to go on for me. Why has he fled, tell me that?”

“My dear fellow, let me demolish your house of cards. First, would a man who is so cool a hand that he can do what he has done, show nervousness and fright when asked to go to the house? Would he not rather welcome the chance, as these vainglorious murderers invariably do?”

“Perhaps.”

“Then, from what you tell me, he seems to have made up his mind to go and see Sir James suddenly. That doesn’t fit in.”

===Chinese===
“好，继续。”

“就这些了，但对我来说已经足够。他为什么要跑，你说说看？”

“亲爱的伙计，让我来拆掉你这座纸牌搭的屋子。第一，一个手段如此冷静、能把这桩事办成这样的角色，被请去出事的宅子时会露出紧张和惶恐吗？按这类虚荣自负的杀人犯的一贯做派，他岂不更会巴不得有这个机会？”

“也许吧。”

“还有，照你所说，他似乎是突然拿定主意去见詹姆斯爵士的。这跟你的设想对不上。”

===Original===
“How did he get into the house and out again the second time?”

“It’s no good asking questions. He may have had accomplices for all we know.”

“Ah, now you are opening up a new question altogether. We have rather assumed that there was only one man in it, but let’s keep to Lewis.”

“All right, then. He had access to official paper, and knew my signature well. He had letters of mine, and could have copied it. Then he knew my habits, and where I should be found, and he knew where to find you. He might even have called up from the Yard itself.”

===Chinese===
“那他第二次又是怎么进宅、怎么出去的？”

“光这么问没用。说不定他有同伙。”

“啊，这下你可掀出一个全新的问题了。我们此前一直假定案中只有一人。不过，还是先说刘易斯。”

“那好。他拿得到公文信纸，对我的签名又了如指掌。他手里存着我的信，大有可能描摹下来。他还熟悉我的习惯，知道能在哪儿找到我，也知道去哪儿找你。他甚至可能就是从苏格兰场里打的电话。”

===Original===
“That’s one to you, certainly,” said Collins. “I can see Lewis having a thin time if you catch him. What do you know about him, anyway?”

“Nothing much. He has been here for three years. He came from a merchant’s office, and applied for a clerkship. He was well recommended, and was always keen on his work, and very reliable. So I took him on as my right-hand man and confidential clerk. He was known there as my ‘familiar.’ ”

“That’s all straightforward. Have you taken any action?”

“I have sent a full description of him to the papers, and posted him as ‘wanted.’ ”

===Chinese===
“这一分确实算你赢。”科林斯说。“真要让你们逮着刘易斯，有他受的。话说回来，此人底细你知道多少？”

“不多。他来部里三年了。原先在一家商行任职，前来应征文书之职。推荐很好，工作一贯勤勉，人也极可靠。我便收他做了左膀右臂兼机要文书。部里的人都称他是我的‘亲信’。”

“这些都顺理成章。你们采取行动了吗？”

“我已把他的详细体貌特征发往各报，将他列为‘通缉犯’。”

===Original===
“Oh, my God,” said Collins, jumping up, “when will you people learn the folly of this? You know what will happen? First, you put him at once on his guard. Then we shall have letters from Tokyo and Leningrad, besides Brighton and Battersea, from people who have seen him and talked to him. You know that always happens, and if, by any chance, he is guilty, we shall have his body washed ashore by the Thames in about a month’s time. Or,” he added grimly, “possibly if he is innocent.”

“Well, that’s the official procedure. I cannot go behind that, especially in a case like this.”

Collins laughed, and sat down.

“It may not be a bad thing,” he said. “It may put the murderer into a false position of security.”

“You don’t think much of this, then?”

===Chinese===
“哦，我的老天，”科林斯跳了起来。“你们这些人什么时候才能明白这么干的蠢处？知道会有什么结果吗？首先，你们立刻打草惊蛇。然后，东京、列宁格勒，外加布莱顿和巴特西的信就会纷纷飞来，人人都说见过他、同他攀谈过。你知道这种事回回发生。而万一他真有罪，不出一个月，他的尸首就会被冲上泰晤士河岸。要不，”他冷冷地补了一句，“他没罪，也可能落得这下场。”

“唉，这是官定的程序。我没法绕开，尤其在这样一桩案子上。”

科林斯笑着坐了回去。

“这未必是坏事，”他说。“兴许能让凶手误入一种虚假的安全。”

“这么说，你不大看好这条线索？”

===Original===
“I reserve judgment, but if you are right he was one of the cleverest scoundrels of this generation.”

Sinclair started. The words were the same as in the letter he had received, and which was burning in his pocket.

“But you, what did you find in Devonshire?”

“A charming house and a charming girl. It was a rotten job to have to break the news to her.”

“Then why on earth did you go?”

“Oh, I learnt some interesting facts about the family.”

===Chinese===
“我保留判断。不过如果你说得对，他就是这一代人里最狡猾的恶棍之一。”

辛克莱猛然一惊。这话同他收到的那封信上的字句一模一样，那封信此刻还在他口袋里灼灼发烫。

“倒是你，在德文郡查到了什么？”

“一座迷人的宅子，一个迷人的姑娘。只是向她报噩耗这桩差事，实在糟糕透顶。”

“那你到底为什么要去？”

“哦，我打听到这家人的一些耐人寻味的情况。”

===Original===
“It’s a dark business altogether, and the worst is that we shall have no peace till it is solved. I have had our staff working all day tracing the movements of well-known criminals who are at large, and any who may have had a grievance against the Home Secretary. Also of any lunatics who are at large, and, as you know, there are many of them.”

They smoked in silence for a while.

“Have you any clue at all? Don’t tell me if you would rather not, but I fancied you were holding something back?” said Sinclair.

“You have asked me, and I will tell you. I have. The only reason I have not confided in you is, not from lack of confidence, but because I was afraid of just such action as you have taken in the case of Lewis. You see, you are bound to do certain things by your position. If this develops I will tell you.”

“Thanks,” said the other, his face clearing.

===Chinese===
“整桩案子一团漆黑，最糟的是不破案，我们就休想安生。我一整天都让人手追查那些在逃知名罪犯的行踪，尤其是可能同内政大臣结过怨的人。还有在逃的疯子——你知道，这种人可多得很。”

两人默然抽了一会儿烟。

“你到底有没有一点线索？不愿说就不说，不过我总觉得你藏着什么没讲。”辛克莱说。

“你问了，我就告诉你。有。我之所以没向你交底，不是信不过你，而是怕的正是你在刘易斯这件事上采取的那种行动。你要明白，你的职位缚着你，有些事你非做不可。等事态有了进展，我会告诉你的。”

“多谢。”对方说，脸色明朗起来。

===Original===
“Did you notice anything curious in Mrs. Simmons’ statement?” said Collins, after a few minutes’ silence.

“In what particular?”

“I commend it to your notice. She said that when the unknown visitor had been with Sir James for some time, she heard a ring from the library. When she went upstairs, Sir James was showing the visitor out. If so, who rang, and why?”

“Just a moment, I have the statement here,” and he pulled out his pocket book, and took out a folded paper. “Here we are. Yes, you are quite right, I did not notice it at the time. You think that curious?”

“I think it﻿—Hush, what was that?”

===Chinese===
“西蒙斯太太的口供里，你可注意到什么蹊跷？”沉默了几分钟后，科林斯说。

“蹊跷在哪儿？”

“我提请你注意。她说，那位身份不明的访客同詹姆斯爵士待了一阵之后，她听见书房里响起铃。她上楼时，詹姆斯爵士正送客出门。果真如此，按铃的是谁？为什么按？”

“等等，口供我带在身上。”他掏出记事本，抽出一张折着的纸。“在这儿。嗯，你说得完全对，我当时没留意。你觉得这事蹊跷？”

“我觉得——嘘，那是什么动静？”

===Original===
“What?” said the other.

“I could have sworn I heard someone moving.”

“Your man, probably. I heard nothing.”

“My man does not live in. Don’t move.”

He got to his feet without noise, and went to his desk. Opening a drawer he took out an automatic pistol, and went to the door. With a swift movement he flung it open. Outside was darkness. Collins turned on the light and looked around.

===Chinese===
“什么？”对方问。

“我敢发誓，我听见有人在走动。”

“多半是你那仆人吧。我什么也没听见。”

“我的仆人不住在这儿。别动。”

他悄无声息地站起身，走到书桌前，拉开抽屉取出一把自动手枪，又走到门边。他猛地把门拽开。门外一片漆黑。科林斯拧亮灯，四下查看。

===Original===
“Nothing,” he said. “Must have been my imagination. Hullo, what’s this?”

He stooped down and picked up a piece of paper from the floor.

It was a sheet torn from a pocket book, and he brought it into the room, closing the door carefully.

“Read that,” he said, and for all his coolness there was a tremble in his voice. “You are both on the wrong track. Poor fools,” and that was all.

“Come,” said Collins, incisively. “We must search the flat.”

===Chinese===
“没人。”他说。“准是我神经过敏。咦，这是什么？”

他俯下身，从地板上拾起一张纸。

那是从记事本上撕下的一页。他把它拿进屋来，小心地关上了门。

“念念这个。”他说。纵然一向镇定，他声音里也带着颤。“你们两个都查错了方向。可怜的蠢货。”纸上就这一句，再无其他。

“来，”科林斯干脆地说。“我们必须把公寓搜一遍。”

===Original===
It did not take long, as the flat was not a large one. There was not a trace of anyone, and the door was fast.

“Well, well, things are getting lively. We have to deal with a pretty customer. We shall have to look out for ourselves.”

Sinclair was white. He got up and helped himself to a whiskey.

Collins picked up the statement of Mrs. Simmons from the floor, and read it carefully through.

Then he folded it up and handed it back to Sinclair.

===Chinese===
公寓不大，没费多少工夫。没有半个人影，大门也闩得好好的。

“好哇，事情热闹起来了。咱们要对付的是个厉害角色。往后得多加小心了。”

辛克莱面无人色。他站起来，给自己倒了杯威士忌。

科林斯从地板上拾起西蒙斯太太的口供，从头到尾仔细读了一遍。

然后他把纸折好，交还给辛克莱。

===Original===
“Was there anything else found on his person of interest?” he said.

“Yes,” said Sinclair, and took from his pocket a leather case. “The other things were just the ordinary things a man carries: they are at the house. This I brought with me. It contains miniatures of Sir James’ wife and daughter. She is a beautiful girl, you saw her today?”

Collins looked at the miniature long. It showed Miss Watson as a very young girl, with quaint curls encircling her face, but from the eyes there looked out the same brave innocence, and there was the wistful curve of the lips which he had seen in the girl of today.

He turned to the other picture, and gave a start. An intent look came into his face, and he looked long and earnestly.

Sinclair looked up.

===Chinese===
“他身上还搜出什么值得注意的东西吗？”他问。

“有。”辛克莱说着，从口袋里取出一只皮匣。“其余物件都是男人随身的寻常东西，留在宅子里了。这只我随身带来了。里面是詹姆斯爵士夫人与女儿的微型画像。那姑娘很美，你今天见过了吧？”

科林斯久久端详那幅微型画像。画中的沃森小姐还是个年纪很轻的姑娘，一圈雅致的鬈发环绕着脸庞，但那双眼睛里同样透出勇敢的天真，唇边也是他在今日那位姑娘脸上见过的、带着渴盼的弧线。

他转向另一幅画像，蓦地一怔。他脸上现出专注的神情，久久地、认真地端详着。

辛克莱抬起头来。

===Original===
“They are very much alike, aren’t they?” he said. “Anyone would know them for mother and daughter. Do you know, when I saw that photo miniature I almost seemed to recognise it, there is something familiar.”

Collins composed his face before he answered.

“The likeness is striking. It’s very strange,” he said.

“Strange?” said the other.

===Chinese===
“她们长得真像，是不是？”他说。“谁都能一眼认出是母女。你知道吗，我头一回看见那幅照相小像时，几乎觉得认得它，有几分说不出的眼熟。”

科林斯定了定神，才作回答。

“像得惊人。真是奇怪。”他说。

“奇怪？”对方问。

===Original===
“Yes, strange, because I, too, seem to recognise it. I saw her portrait at the Vale today.”

“Perhaps that is why it looks familiar,” said Sinclair.

“Perhaps,” said Collins, carelessly, but his eyes were still on the picture.

“You had better stay here for the night after what has happened﻿—it’s very late, and it’s no good playing the fool,” he said.

“Do you know, I think I will. No one is sitting up for me.”

===Chinese===
“是的，奇怪，因为我也好像认得它。今天我在谷园（the Vale）见过她的画像。”

“也许因此你才觉得眼熟吧。”辛克莱说。

“也许吧。”科林斯随口应道，目光却仍停留在画像上。

“出了这种事，你今晚最好就睡在这儿——天已经很晚了，犯不上逞强冒险。”他说。

“说真的，我看我就留下吧。反正也没人守着等我回去。”

===Original===
“Good. I shan’t be sorry to have you,” and he laughed.

“We can go to Leveson Square tomorrow, or rather today,” he said. “Let’s turn in. I can rig you out.”

He rose and turned to the door, slipping the leather case into his pocket.

It was not the first time that Sinclair had accepted Collins’ hospitality, and he knew from experience what an excellent host he made.

===Chinese===
“好。有你做伴，我求之不得。”说着他笑了。

“明天——不，该说今天——我们可以去勒维森广场。”他说。“睡吧。衣物用品我给你张罗。”

他起身朝门口走去，顺手把皮匣塞进口袋。

辛克莱不是头一回叨扰科林斯的款待了，凭以往的经验，他深知这位主人招待人有多周到。

## VI. At Leveson Square / 第六章 勒维森广场

===Original===
The blinds were drawn at the house in Leveson Square, and a crowd was still gazing up at the blank windows. There is always something gruesome in drawn blinds, and the policeman standing at the door added to the air of mystery and dark deeds.

Collins and Sinclair, refreshed by an excellent breakfast, arrived in Collins’ car. They had been to the Yard first.

Within, the appearance of the house was as though a bomb had been dropped. All the oak panelling from the library was piled in the hall, with furniture and books. The library door was open, and the floor was covered with plaster. The men had made small holes in the ceiling at various places, as well as having examined the floor above. The search had been very thorough.

===Chinese===
勒维森广场的宅子放下了百叶窗，人群仍仰头望着那一片遮得严严实实的窗户。放下的百叶窗向来透着几分不祥，站在门口的警察，更为这宅子平添了神秘与罪恶的气息。

科林斯和辛克莱美美地用过一顿早餐，精神焕发，乘科林斯的汽车赶到。他们先去了苏格兰场。

宅子里的景象，就像挨过一颗炸弹。书房的橡木护壁板全拆了下来，同家具和书籍一起堆在门厅里。书房门敞开着，地板上落满灰泥。搜寻的人在天花板上各处凿了小孔，楼上的地板也查验过了。搜得可谓彻底。

===Original===
In a room upstairs lay the silent figure of the dead statesman.

It had been decided that the body should remain in the house and the doctor’s examination had taken place at the house.

A Home Office expert had conducted this, and the fussy little doctor, who had been summoned at the first, had wormed his way in to assist. He was full of importance.

They met the doctors in the dining-room.

===Chinese===
楼上一个房间里，静卧着这位亡故政治家的遗体。

已决定将遗体暂留宅中，医生的检验也在宅子里进行。

主检由内政部的一位专家主持，最初应召而来的那位大惊小怪的小个子医生，也钻营进来从旁协助，此刻一副自命不凡的做派。

他们在餐厅里会见了两位医生。

===Original===
“Well?” said Sinclair. “Have you found anything?”

“We have extracted the bullet,” said Broughton, the Home Office doctor. “As might have been expected, it fits the empty cartridge, and is exactly similar to the other five.”

“What a pity,” said Collins.

The doctor looked at him in surprise.

Sinclair gave a laugh.

“Nothing straightforward satisfies Collins,” he said. “He loves mystery. He would have liked you to have found a rifle bullet, or no bullet at all.”

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”辛克莱说。“有发现吗？”

“子弹我们取出来了，”内政部医生布劳顿（Broughton）说，“不出所料，弹头同那枚空弹壳正相吻合，与其余五发也一模一样。”

“真遗憾。”科林斯说。

医生诧异地望着他。

辛克莱笑了。

“顺顺当当的结论从来满足不了科林斯，”他说，“他偏爱谜团。要是你们挖出一颗步枪子弹，或者干脆一颗子弹也没有，他才称心呢。”

===Original===
“There’s no accounting for tastes,” said the other. “For my part I am glad enough when I find things fitting in.”

“And that’s all?” said Collins.

“There’s one thing I can’t make out, it’s probably nothing, but it’s queer. I have had a very extensive experience with this sort of thing, as you know. The bullet had only just penetrated the brain. Now, a revolver bullet of that calibre, fired at the distance it was, should have smashed the back of the skull, and made a dreadful wound. It is the heavy Army type. I sent a policeman for a sheep’s head, which is harder than the human skull, and fired at it in the back garden. Look at the mess it has made,” and he showed them the gruesome sight.

“Have you any suggestion to make?” said Sinclair.

“The only thing I can think of is that a half charge was used to deaden the noise. But then, why put a half charge in one cartridge only?”

“I give it up,” said Sinclair.

===Chinese===
“人的口味真是没法说，”对方道，“就我而言，只要样样都对得上号，我就谢天谢地了。”

“就这些？”科林斯说。

“有一桩事我琢磨不透。多半不算什么，可着实蹊跷。你们知道，这类事情我经验极其丰富。那颗子弹才刚刚射入脑部。要知道，那样口径的左轮子弹，在那样近的距离击发，本该把后脑壳击得粉碎，留下一处可怕的创伤。那是重型的军用弹。我派了名警察去买来一颗羊头——羊头比人的头骨还硬——在后园里对它开了一枪。瞧瞧它砸成了什么模样。”说着，他把那副骇人的东西拿给他们看。

“你有什么见解？”辛克莱问。

“我唯一想得通的，是装了减半的火药，为的是压低枪声。可话说回来，为什么偏偏只在一发子弹里装半份火药呢？”

“我猜不着了。”辛克莱说。

===Original===
Collins was taking little notice. He was in a furious mood. The morning papers had come out with full details, not only of the crime, but of the telephone messages and the letter to the Central News, which made excellent copy, but was in the highest degree indiscreet.

“Who the devil has done this?” he had said in the car.

Sinclair had hastily disclaimed all knowledge of it.

“Then it must be Boyce,” said Collins. “He is the only other that knows about it. The fool!”

===Chinese===
科林斯没怎么在意。他正满腔怒火。早晨的报纸把详情和盘托出，不仅凶案本身，连那两通电话和寄给中央新闻社的信也一并刊载——那些内容见报固然是绝好的素材，行事却轻率到了极点。

“这到底是哪个混账干的好事？”在车里他就这么说过。

辛克莱急忙撇清，说自己毫不知情。

“那就只能是博伊斯了，”科林斯说，“知道内情的再没有旁人。这个蠢货！”

===Original===
Presently the man himself came in, puffing and blowing, for he had walked.

Collins tackled him at once.

“I say, Boyce, did you tell the Press all those details which have come out today?”

Boyce looked uncomfortable.

“No,” he said. “I have told the Press nothing. I only told the facts to one or two of the Cabinet who were asking me about things yesterday.”

“Then you’ve put your foot into it properly,” said Collins, bluntly. Boyce began to bluster after the manner of a man who knows he is in the wrong.

===Chinese===
不多时，这位正主儿亲自到了，气喘吁吁——他是一路步行来的。

科林斯当即迎头发问。

“我说，博伊斯，今天捅出来的那些细节，是你告诉报界的吧？”

博伊斯面露窘色。

“不是，”他说，“我对报界一个字也没讲。我只是把事实告诉了一两位内阁成员，他们昨天向我打听情况来着。”

“那你可是把祸闯到家了。”科林斯直言不讳地说。博伊斯随即嚷嚷起来，一副明知理亏偏要强辩的做派。

===Original===
“I don’t see that any harm is done, and anyway it is a matter for my Department to decide. I really don’t see what it has to do with you, Collins.”

“You’ll see,” said the other, shortly.

Boyce was offended, and, like men of his kind, began to sulk. He ignored Collins, and turned to Sinclair.

“Any news of the missing man? I see you have a full description of him, but no photo. That’s a pity.”

“We could not find one, sir,” said Sinclair. “He seems to have been one of those men who do not care to have their photo taken.”

===Chinese===
“我看没造成什么损害。再说了，这事该由我的部里定夺。我实在看不出这跟你有什么相干，科林斯。”

“你会看出来的。”对方简短地说。

博伊斯觉得受了冒犯，便像他这类人一样闹起别扭来。他不理科林斯，转向辛克莱。

“那个失踪的人有消息吗？我看你们掌握了他的详细体貌特征，却没有照片。真可惜。”

“我们找不到，先生，”辛克莱说，“他似乎是不爱照相的那种人。”

===Original===
“Suspicious, very, that,” said Boyce.

Collins laughed contemptuously.

Boyce shot him an angry look.

“We must lay him by the heels soon﻿—he won’t be able to get out of the country,” he said in a challenging way.

===Chinese===
“这一点非常可疑。”博伊斯说。

科林斯轻蔑地笑了笑。

博伊斯狠狠瞪了他一眼。

“我们必须尽快把他缉拿归案——他休想逃出国去。”他挑衅似的说。

===Original===
“I don’t think we can do any more here,” said Collins.

“You’ve searched all these books and the furniture for any documents?” said Sinclair.

“What do you expect to find?” said Boyce.

“One never knows,” said Sinclair hastily.

The man addressed said, “Yes, sir, and we have been through all his papers as you told us; there appears to be nothing of importance. He was very methodical, and did not appear to keep any private documents here. Perhaps they are in Devonshire.”

===Chinese===
“我看我们在这儿再也查不出什么了。”科林斯说。

“这些书籍和家具，都搜查过有没有文件了吗？”辛克莱说。

“你还指望找到什么？”博伊斯说。

“这可说不准。”辛克莱急忙说。

被问的那人答道：“搜过了，长官。您的吩咐我们都照办了，他的全部文件都翻检过，似乎没有什么要紧的东西。他做事极有条理，看来没有把任何私人文件存放在这儿。也许都在德文郡。”

===Original===
“We are trying to find the whereabouts of the new Baronet, who was last heard of in Monte Video,” said Sinclair.

“I see you are,” said Boyce; “but I should have thought that would have been for the relatives to do. It does not seem a Home Office matter.”

“Perhaps not,” said Sinclair; “but as he was Home Secretary?”

“Exactly, as an act of courtesy, perhaps,” and Boyce assumed a manner of importance. He had become a prominent man in the last few days. Sinclair breathed a sigh of relief. He was thinking of the letter which he had no intention of showing to anyone else.

===Chinese===
“我们正设法查找新任从男爵的下落，最后一次听说他是在蒙得维的亚。”辛克莱说。

“我看得出来，”博伊斯说，“不过我以为，那该是亲属们去办的事。这似乎不是内政部份内的公务。”

“也许不是，”辛克莱说，“可谁叫他是内政大臣呢？”

“正是，或许算是出于一番礼节吧。”博伊斯摆出一副非同小可的架势。这几日他已成了一位显要人物。辛克莱暗暗松了口气。他心里正想着那封信——他压根不打算把它给任何人看。

===Original===
There was a ring at the door, and the policeman on duty brought in a telegram.

“For you, sir,” he said, handing it to Collins.

He read it while the others watched. Not a muscle moved.

“No answer, thanks,” he said to the policeman, and put it in his pocket.

“Anything important?” said Boyce, officiously.

“Oh, no,” said the other.

===Chinese===
门铃响了，值班的警察送进来一封电报。

“给您的，长官。”他说着，把电报递给科林斯。

旁人都望着他。他读着电报，脸上纹丝不动。

“不必回电，谢谢。”他对警察说，随手把电报揣进口袋。

“有什么要紧事吗？”博伊斯多嘴地问。

“哦，没有。”对方说。

===Original===
He turned into the library, and looked round.

“What the devil is he doing with telegrams sent to this house?” said Boyce, irritably.

The remark called for no answer.

The telegram was from Miss Watson to say she was coming to Town that afternoon, and would he meet her.

It did not ask for an answer, which pleased him somehow. He strolled out of the room, and said:

===Chinese===
他转身走进书房，环顾四周。

“他把电报拍到这宅子里来，到底搞的什么名堂？”博伊斯焦躁地说。

这话没人接茬。

电报是沃森小姐拍来的，说她当天下午来伦敦，问他可愿去接她。

电报没有要求回电，这不知怎的让他高兴。他信步走出房间，说道：

===Original===
“What a pity some of our spook merchants cannot come and make an incantation or beat tom-toms or something, and conjure up the scene for us. It would be most interesting.”

“What is more important than mere interest,” said Boyce, “is to bring the criminal to justice.”

“Oh, I suppose so, but it’s so dull when the problem is solved, especially if it turns out banal. It’s like a game of cricket, when you expect an exciting ending, and the other side all get out for about 20.”

“I am afraid I do not play cricket,” said Boyce, curtly.

Collins eyed him, “No, you wouldn’t,” he said, and made an enemy of him forthwith.

===Chinese===
“真可惜，我们那些装神弄鬼的大师不能到这儿来念咒作法、敲敲手鼓什么的，替我们把当时的情景召唤出来。那可就有趣极了。”

“比有趣更要紧的，”博伊斯说，“是把凶手绳之以法。”

“哦，话是这么说。可谜底一旦揭开，就索然无味了，尤其倘若真相原来平庸无奇。就像一场板球赛，你满心盼着激动人心的收场，对方全队却只得了约 20 分便集体出局。”

“恐怕我是不打板球的。”博伊斯生硬地说。

科林斯端详着他。“是啊，你是不会打的。”他说，当场便树了一个仇敌。

## VII. Valuable Information / 第七章 宝贵的情报

===Original===
Collins was punctual to the minute at Paddington. He had dressed himself up for the occasion, though he felt contempt for so doing. The express from Wilton-on-Sea was up to time.

From a first-class compartment Mabel Watson descended. Her face was white and calm, but she wore no veil. Collins walked towards the door and stopped. After the girl, a man descended from the carriage. He was tall and dressed in good taste, but had a weak looking face, with a wandering light moustache and straw-coloured hair.

His eyes were a cold blue. He was the sort of man that women “rave about.” Collins took careful stock of the man, and then advanced with raised hat. The girl gave a smile of recognition, and shook hands.
===Chinese===
科林斯分秒不差地准时到了帕丁顿（Paddington）车站。为了这个场合他把自己打扮得整整齐齐，心里却鄙夷这样做。从滨海威尔顿（Wilton-on-Sea）开来的快车正点抵达。

梅布尔·沃森从头等车厢走了下来。她脸色苍白，却很平静，没有戴面纱。科林斯朝车门走去，又停住脚。姑娘之后，一个男子也下了车。他个子高大，衣着雅致得体，可那张脸透着懦弱，两撇浅色小胡子稀稀拉拉，头发是麦秆般的黄色。

他有一双冷冷的蓝眼睛，是那种让女人们“如痴如狂”的男人。科林斯把此人细细打量了一番，然后举帽上前。姑娘含笑相认，同他握了手。

===Original===
“It is very good of you to come and meet me,” she said. “This is Mr. Eric Sanders, Mr. Collins.” The men shook hands and exchanged a look not too friendly. They were antipathetical.

“I have brought my old nurse with me, and John. I shall go to an hotel, of course. I suppose our house is in the hands of the police,” and she shuddered. “We stayed at Ackroyd’s when we had let our house one year,” she said.

“A very good hotel, and quite convenient,” said Collins.

“John, tell the porter to get a taxi, please,” she said.
===Chinese===
“您能来接我，真是太好心了。”她说。“科林斯先生，这位是埃里克·桑德斯先生（Eric Sanders）。”两个男人握了手，交换了一个不大友好的眼色。他们彼此看着都不顺眼。

“我把老保姆和约翰也带来了。我当然要去住旅馆。想必咱们家的宅子还在警察手里。”说着她打了个寒噤。“有一年我们把房子租了出去，那时候就住在阿克罗伊德旅馆（Ackroyd's）。”她说。

“那是一家很好的旅馆，也相当方便。”科林斯说。

“约翰，麻烦你叫搬运员去喊一辆出租车。”她说。

===Original===
“I have brought my car,” Collins interposed, “and if you care to come with me, the servants can come on with the luggage.”

“That is very kind of you, but I will bring Nurse, if you don’t mind.”

Sanders had been standing by, gloomy and resentful.

Collins led the way to the car.

“Will you come with me, then?” said he.

“I should like to,” she replied. “Eric, will you take care of Nurse?” She got into her seat, and Sanders, with not too good a grace, helped the old nurse into the back seat.
===Chinese===
“我把车开来了，”科林斯插言道，“您若愿意同我一道走，仆人们可以带着行李随后跟来。”

“您太客气了，不过我想带上保姆，您别见怪。”

桑德斯一直站在一旁，阴郁而愤懑。

科林斯在前头引路，朝汽车走去。

“那么，您肯赏光同我走吗？”他说。

“我很愿意。”她答道。“埃里克，你照看一下保姆好吗？”她在自己的座位上坐好，桑德斯老大不情愿地把老保姆扶上了后座。

===Original===
The drive was all too short. In spite of the terrible cloud hanging over her, her youth was strong, and she felt the relief of getting away from Devon and her thoughts there.

They had tea in a private sitting room, and Collins laid himself out to keep her mind off the tragedy.

“You must come to a theatre tonight,” he said.

“Oh, I couldn’t possibly,” she answered.
===Chinese===
这段车程实在嫌短。尽管有可怕的阴云压在心头，她年轻的活力终究强韧，离开了德文郡、撇开了那里的种种思绪，她感到一阵轻松。

他们在旅馆一间专用起居室里用了茶，科林斯千方百计引开她的心思，不让她去想那桩惨剧。

“今晚您一定得去看场戏。”他说。

“哦，我怎么可能去呢。”她答道。

===Original===
“If I may say so, I don’t agree with you. I know what is in your mind. You don’t think it would be right after what has happened, but if you stay in, you will only brood over things and make yourself miserable, and,” he added earnestly, “I am sure your father would not have wished you to do that. I am not asking you to forget him, but you have had a bad ordeal to go through, and must keep yourself going.”

“What do you think, Eric?” she said, addressing Sanders, who had been silent during the meal.

“Of course, you must please yourself, but I should hardly have thought it was quite the thing,” he said.

There was something in his tone which annoyed her.
===Chinese===
“请恕我直言，我不这么看。我知道您心里在想什么。您觉得出了这样的事再去看戏不像话，可您若是闭门不出，只会把事情翻来覆去地想，把自己折磨得愈发凄苦。况且，”他诚恳地补上一句，“我确信令尊绝不愿您这样。我不是要您忘掉他，只是您刚经历了一场严酷的磨难，总得撑持下去。”

“埃里克，你怎么看？”她冲着桑德斯问道；这顿茶他从头到尾都没作声。

“当然，你自己的事自己做主，只是我总觉得这样做不大得体。”他说。

他的语气里有点什么东西惹恼了她。

===Original===
“Why not?” she said.

“Well,” he said, floundering. “I suppose it’s a matter of taste, but in the circumstances﻿—”

She gave a toss of her head, and turned to Collins.

“Thank you; yes, I will accept your kind invitation.”

“I hope you will join us, too,” said he politely to Sanders.

For a moment he was about to refuse, then he said, “Thank you, very much.”
===Chinese===
“为什么不大得体？”她说。

“呃，”他狼狈地支吾着。“我看这属于各人口味的问题，可眼下的情形——”

她把头一扬，转向科林斯。

“谢谢您；好吧，我接受您的美意。”

“希望您也一起来。”他礼数周到地对桑德斯说。

他一时几乎要回绝，随后才说：“那么，多谢了。”

===Original===
“Then I will get a box for four. Of course, Nurse will come as well.”

“That is very kind of you. It would be a great treat for her. Only in that case you must both dine here first. You know I feel very guilty in doing this, but it will only be a very quiet dinner.”

“I quite understand,” said Collins, “and I will get a box for Gilbert and Sullivan’s. I know the management there, and it is just what you want, something soothing and not too gay. Now, I know you will want to go to Leveson Square. I will go and see that everything is all right for you there. Come on, in about half an hour’s time.”

He saw with a thrill of pleasure the look of gratitude come to her expressive eyes.
===Chinese===
“那我就去订一间四人包厢。当然，保姆也一同去。”

“您真是周到。那对她可是件难得的乐事。只是那样的话，你们俩得先在这儿吃晚饭。您知道的，我这样做心里实在过意不去，不过只是一顿非常安静的便饭。”

“我完全理解，”科林斯说，“我会去订一间吉尔伯特与沙利文剧目的包厢。那边的经理我认得，而且那正合您现在的需要：让人心境宽舒，又不过分喧闹。好了，我知道您还想去勒维森广场。我这就先去看看，那边是不是一切都替您准备妥帖了。半个钟头后请动身。”

他怀着一阵快意的震颤，看见感激的神色浮上了她那双脉脉传神的眼睛。

===Original===
After he had taken his leave, she turned to Sanders.

“I think you might be a little more gracious to Mr. Collins, he is most kind.”

“You seem to have made great friends with him at short notice,” he said, churlishly.

She bridled up. “And if I have, I suppose I can choose my friends.”

“And forget the old one for the new.”
===Chinese===
他告辞之后，她转身对桑德斯说。

“我看你对科林斯先生不妨客气些，他心肠好极了。”

“你倒好，才打了个照面，就跟他交上好朋友了。”他悻悻地说。

她陡然作色。“就算交上了，我想我自己的朋友总还由得我自己挑。”

“有了新的，就忘了旧的。”

===Original===
“What nonsense. You are behaving like a spoilt child. I have watched you all the time.”

“What do you know of this fellow, anyway? He is only a sort of policeman. I suppose he didn’t tell you that?”

“If it is any satisfaction to you, he did, and perhaps we had better stop discussing Mr. Collins any more.”

He got up and wandered round the room.

“I suppose I had better go,” he said.

Her lip curled with contempt. “You can please yourself, but I should hardly have thought that my oldest male friend would have deserted me at such a time.”
===Chinese===
“胡说八道。你现在的样子活像个被宠坏的孩子。我从头到尾都看在眼里呢。”

“再说，这家伙你究竟知道些什么？他不过是个当警察的。这话我猜他没同你说吧？”

“若是这话能让你痛快些——他告诉过我。也许我们最好别再议论科林斯先生了。”

他起身在屋里走来走去。

“我想我还是走的好。”他说。

她的嘴唇轻蔑地一撇。“随你的便。只是我万万没有想到，我相识最久的男性朋友，竟会在这种时候弃我而去。”

===Original===
“Forgive me. I will say no more. Of course I will stay with you.”

“All right. It is time we started for Leveson Square.”

“What a brute I have been,” he said. “I ought to have thought of you.”

“Would you fetch a taxi?” she said.
===Chinese===
“原谅我。我不再说了。我当然会陪着你。”

“那就好了。该动身去勒维森广场了。”

“我方才多像个畜生啊。”他说。“我本该先替你着想的。”

“你去叫辆出租车来，好吗？”她说。

===Original===
Collins was at the door when they drove up.

He bowed slightly to Mabel, in the deferential but not subservient manner of a courtier.

“Everything is ready for you,” he whispered. “I brought your old nurse with me. I knew you would like to have her here. She is waiting in the bedroom.” She felt a sense of pleasure at the thoughtfulness.

“I will go up,” she said.
===Chinese===
他们的车驶到时，科林斯已经候在门口。

他向梅布尔微微一躬，那副恭谨而不失身份的姿态，正像一位廷臣。

“一切都为您准备好了。”他低声说。“我把您的老保姆也先接了来。我知道您会愿意让她待在这儿。她正在卧室里等着。”这份体贴周到让她心头一暖。

“我这就上去。”她说。

===Original===
The two men were left alone in the hall.

“I suppose you know this place well,” said Collins, carelessly.

“Of course. I was private secretary to Sir James,” said the other, stiffly.

Collins lit a cigarette, and offered his case. The other could not well refuse. “Thanks,” said he.
===Chinese===
大厅里只剩下两个男人。

“想必您对这宅子很熟悉。”科林斯随口说道。

“当然。我从前是詹姆斯爵士的私人秘书。”对方生硬地说。

科林斯点上一支烟，递过烟盒。对方不便回绝。“多谢。”他说。

===Original===
“This is a wretched business,” said Collins.

“It is very terrible, but of course you are used to these crimes in your profession.”

“Yes,” he said solemnly, “and we get used to all kinds of criminals,” and he looked into the empty library.

“Have you any theory as to who did the murder?” said Sanders.
===Chinese===
“这件事真是糟糕透顶。”科林斯说。

“确实可怕，不过在您这一行里，这类罪案自然司空见惯。”

“是啊，”他正色道，“什么样的罪犯我们都见惯了。”说着，他朝那间空荡荡的书房望了进去。

“对行凶的人，您有什么推想吗？”桑德斯说。

===Original===
“I never allow myself the luxury of theories,” said Collins.

“Prig,” said the other under his breath.

“It is strange how the murderer escaped, isn’t it?” said Collins. “I wonder if you ever heard tell of any secret doors, or trap doors, or anything of that sort?”

“Oh, really, are we back in the Middle Ages? This is a modern, London house. Besides, by the look of things your men have had a pretty thorough hunt.”
===Chinese===
“我从不纵容自己享受推想的奢侈。”科林斯说。

“假正经。”对方压低声音嘀咕道。

“凶手究竟是怎么逃出去的，很蹊跷，不是吗？”科林斯说。“不知道您可曾听说过这宅子里有什么暗门、活板门，或诸如此类的机关？”

“哦，得了，难道我们又回到中世纪去了？这是一栋现代化的伦敦宅子。再说了，瞧这光景，你们的人已经搜得够彻底了。”

===Original===
“When did you see Sir James last?” said Collins, lighting another cigarette from his old one.

“Oh, I was with him at the Home Office in the morning of the day.”

“Fancy, and you little thought then that you would never see him again alive,” said Collins musingly.

The other was silent.

“Do you know,” continued Collins, “you mentioned the Middle Ages. How much easier detection was then. All you did was to parade suspects in front of the departed, and when the right man arrived blood gushed out from his mouth, and you spotted a winner every time.”
===Chinese===
“您最后一次见到詹姆斯爵士，是什么时候？”科林斯说着，用将熄的烟头又点上一支。

“哦，出事那天上午，我陪他在内政部。”

“想想看，您那时哪里想得到，从此再也见不到活着的他了。”科林斯若有所思地说。

对方沉默不语。

“您知道吗，”科林斯接着说，“您方才提到中世纪。那时候的侦破可容易多了。只需让嫌疑犯在死者面前列队走一遭，正主儿一到，死者嘴里便涌出血来——回回都能一押即中。”

===Original===
“What a horribly morbid mind you must have,” said Sanders with a shudder.

“I am glad I am not mixed up with crime.”

“I have not any great sense of horror of crime, murder least of all. There are so many reasons for that,” and he looked straight at the other man.

“Miss Watson will be down soon. I hope it has not upset her too much. You are an old friend. Wouldn’t you like to go up and see.”
===Chinese===
“您的脑子得多阴森可怕啊。”桑德斯说着打了个寒战。

“幸亏我从不跟犯罪沾边。”

“我对犯罪并没有多大的恐怖之感，对谋杀尤其如此。这其中的缘故可多着呢。”说着，他直勾勾地盯着对方。

“沃森小姐很快就下来了。希望这没有让她太难过。您是她的老朋友，不想上楼去看看吗？”

===Original===
“No, thanks. I would rather not. But I will knock at the door.”

He went up the stairs, and Collins followed him with his eyes.

“I wonder if that blood would gush out,” he said to himself.
===Chinese===
“不了，谢谢，我宁可不去。不过我可以去敲一敲门。”

他上楼去了，科林斯的目光一路追随着他。

“不知那血会不会涌出来呢。”他自言自语道。

===Original===
Miss Watson stayed in London until the inquest was over. This was hurried forward out of deference to the position of the deceased. She had to give evidence of identification.

There was nothing fresh in spite of the efforts of those engaged on the case. Tremendous excitement was aroused, not only because of the fact that the murdered man was a Cabinet Minister, but on account of the bizarre events which had surrounded the mystery.

All efforts to trace the ownership of the revolver had failed. Lewis’s landlady could only state that she thought it was his, as it looked like it. But a Webley is so common a type that this did not count for much. The number was an old one, and the weapon had probably passed through many hands.

The police did not press their case against any particular individual, and the jury returned the usual verdict against some person or persons unknown.
===Chinese===
沃森小姐在伦敦一直住到验尸审讯结束。出于对逝者地位的尊重，审讯提前进行。她不得不出庭，作辨认遗体的证词。

尽管办案人员竭尽全力，案情却毫无新的进展。此事激起了轩然大波，不仅因为被害人是位内阁阁员，更因为环绕在这桩疑案周围的种种离奇情事。

追查那支左轮手枪归属的努力尽数落空。刘易斯的房东太太只能说，她觉得枪是他的，因为看上去像。可韦伯利是再普通不过的型号，这话算不得什么凭据。枪身上的号数是旧式的，这支枪恐怕早已辗转多人之手。

警方没有针对任何具体的个人提出控告，陪审团照例裁定凶手系“某一名或数名身份不明者”。

===Original===
Collins had been most assiduous in his attempts to make Miss Watson’s part as small a one as possible, and had endeavoured to keep her spirits up, without intruding himself. Sanders, in spite of all his efforts, was still sulky, and plunged into the work of going over Sir James’ papers, which fell to his lot.

The ordeal was over, and all those women of Society who had crammed themselves into the court were trying to sort themselves out again. Opinion was about equally divided between Lewis and a lunatic as the villain of the piece.

Collins sought the back room where the witnesses had gone.
===Chinese===
科林斯一直竭尽殷勤，尽量让沃森小姐少抛头露面，努力为她宽心打气，又绝不唐突叨扰。桑德斯尽管百般努力，依旧闷闷不乐，一头扎进了清理詹姆斯爵士遗存文件的工作里——这份差事落在了他的头上。

磨难结束了，那些把法庭挤得满满当当的上流社会太太们正乱作一团地重整仪容。至于本案的元凶，舆论约莫对半分：一半说是刘易斯，一半说是某个疯子。

科林斯寻到了证人们去的后厅。

===Original===
His face was stern. He walked directly to Mrs. Simmons, who was sniffing in a corner.

“I would like to have a word with you, if I may,” he said.

“Certainly, sir,” she replied.

“Would you kindly come into the next room, there are too many people here?” She rose and followed him without a word.
===Chinese===
他面色严峻，径直走向正在角落里抽抽搭搭的西蒙斯太太。

“如果可以，我想同您说几句话。”他说。

“当然可以，先生。”她答道。

“劳驾到隔壁房间来一下好吗？这里人太多了。”她一言不发，起身随他去了。

===Original===
In the little side room he faced her.

“Mrs. Simmons, you gave evidence on oath. You know the nature of an oath?”

“Oh, yes, sir,” she replied, without hesitation.

“You swore to tell the truth, didn’t you?”

“Why, yes, sir, of course.”
===Chinese===
在那间小侧室里，他与她面对面站着。

“西蒙斯太太，您是宣誓之后作的证。您知道誓言意味着什么吧？”

“哦，知道，先生。”她毫不犹豫地答道。

“您宣过誓要说真话，是吗？”

“是啊，先生，当然。”

===Original===
“Very good. In your evidence you said that you did not know who the man was who called on Sir James?”

With all her control, a look of relief passed rapidly over her face.

“Quite right, sir, I had no idea. I could not see him. It might have been you, sir, for all I know.”

“It won’t do, Mrs. Simmons. You were asked whether anyone else had come to see Sir James after that, and you said ‘No.’ That was a lie.”
===Chinese===
“很好。您在证词里说，您不知道那位来访詹姆斯爵士的男子是谁？”

她再能自持，脸上还是倏地掠过一丝宽慰之色。

“一点儿不错，先生，我毫不知情。我瞧不见他。没准儿就是您呢，先生，我也说不上。”

“这可说不通，西蒙斯太太。庭上问您，在那之后还有没有别人来见过詹姆斯爵士，您答了声‘没有’。那是谎话。”

===Original===
If one has seen a child’s balloon touched with a lighted cigarette, the collapse of this woman was not more complete.

She seemed to shrivel up. She tried to speak, but all she could do was to lick her lips and open her mouth.

Collins waited.

“You had better tell me,” he said. “If I had wanted to have this known publicly, couldn’t I have had this out in court? Shall I tell you who called?”
===Chinese===
谁要是见过孩子的气球被点着的香烟一碰便瘪掉的情形，便知道这女人此刻垮得有多彻底——比那气球有过之而无不及。

她像是整个人都枯缩了下去。她想开口，却只能舔舔嘴唇、张一张嘴。

科林斯等着。

“您还是自己说的好。”他说。“我若想把这事公开，难道不能当庭抖搂出来？要不要我来告诉您，来的是谁？”

===Original===
“Oh, no, no, sir,” she moaned, raising her hand as though she were threatened with a blow.

“You know perfectly well that Mr. Eric Sanders came, and you let him in?”

“Oh, sir, how do you know?” she said, with a look of blank astonishment.

“The best thing for you is to tell me exactly what did happen. Perhaps I know more than you think.”
===Chinese===
“哦，别，别，先生。”她哀声说道，抬起手来，仿佛怕要挨一记打。

“您明明一清二楚，来的是埃里克·桑德斯先生，而且是他，是您放进来的？”

“哦，先生，您是怎么知道的？”她说，一脸的茫然错愕。

“您最好的办法，就是把实际发生的事情原原本本告诉我。也许我知道的比您以为的要多。”

===Original===
She looked at him in a frightened way.

“Oh, sir, he had nothing to do with the murder, sir, I am sure he didn’t.”

“Tell me exactly what did occur, or must I call the police?” he said.

“What happened was this, sir,” she said, gazing round her, as if for help. “After the visitor had gone, and I am sure I wish I knew who he was, I was in the dining-room, as I told in court.

“Mr. Eric came in without so much as a knock, he being free of the house. He seemed to be very excited, and he says ‘I must see Sir James.’ I says, ‘He does not want to be disturbed.’ Then he says ‘I’ll just knock at his door and ask him.’ ”
===Chinese===
她惊惶地望着他。

“哦，先生，他同这桩谋杀案没有一点关系，先生，我敢肯定没有。”

“把实际发生的事情一五一十告诉我，否则难道要我去叫警察？”他说。

“事情是这样的，先生。”她说着，环顾四周，像在求援。“那位客人走了之后——说真的，我真希望自己知道他是谁——我正在餐厅里，这我在庭上都说了。

“埃里克先生连门也不敲一声就进来了，他在宅子里是随意出入惯了的。他像是激动得很，说道：‘我必须见詹姆斯爵士。’我说：‘他不想让人打扰。’他便说：‘那我去敲敲他的门，当面问他。’”

===Original===
“One moment, Mrs. Simmons. Was this after Sir James had sent the letter?”

“Oh, yes, sir, after he had come in.”

“You are quite sure of that?”

“Quite sure.”

“Go on.”
===Chinese===
“且慢，西蒙斯太太。这是在詹姆斯爵士寄出那封信之后吗？”

“哦，是的，先生，是在他进来之后。”

“这一点您能完全肯定？”

“完全肯定。”

“讲下去。”

===Original===
“Mr. Eric knocked at the door, but there was no answer. Then he knocks again, but there wasn’t a sound. So he writes something on a card, and slips it under the door, and says, ‘Sir James, do you mind reading the card,’ and he waits.

“There was a noise inside, as though someone was a moving very slowly, and a voice said, ‘go away.’ ”

Collins drew in a quick breath. “You are quite certain about that?”

“Oh, yes, sir, I was standing by the door. I was afraid he would come out, and there would be blows.”

“Why?”
===Chinese===
“埃里克先生敲了敲门，里头没有应声。他又敲了一回，还是一点声息也没有。于是他在一张卡片上写了几个字，从门底下塞了进去，说：‘詹姆斯爵士，劳您看一看这张卡片。’然后他就等着。

“里头起了一阵响动，仿佛有人极慢极慢地挪动，随后一个声音说：‘走开。’”

科林斯猛地吸了一口气。“这一点您确信无疑？”

“哦，是的，先生，我当时就站在门边。我生怕他会出来，两个人动起手来。”

“为什么？”

===Original===
“Because, sir, Mr. Eric and Sir James had had a terrible set-to the night before.”

“Do you mean they came to blows?”

“Oh, no, sir, I meant a figure of speech. Only words.”

“What about?”
===Chinese===
“因为，先生，前一天晚上，埃里克先生和詹姆斯爵士狠狠地吵了一场。”

“您是说，他们动了手？”

“哦，不是，先生，我那是打个比方。只是口角而已。”

“为了什么事？”

===Original===
“Well, sir, it isn’t my business, but I think it was over Miss Mabel.”

“Go on,” said Collins.

“Then Mr. Eric says, ‘Won’t you see me for a minute?’ ”

“Was there any answer?”

“Only a horrible sort of groan, so I says to him, ‘you’d better get out of this, quick. The master’s got a loaded revolver in there.’ I don’t know what made me say that, but I wanted to get rid of him.”
===Chinese===
“呃，先生，这本轮不到我多嘴，不过我想是为了梅布尔小姐的事。”

“接着讲。”科林斯说。

“后来埃里克先生说：‘您就不肯见我一小会儿吗？’”

“有什么回音吗？”

“只有一声怪可怕的呻吟。于是我对他说：‘你还是赶快离开这儿的好。老爷在里头，手里有支上了膛的左轮手枪呢。’我自己也不知道怎么会说出这话来，只是一心想打发他走。”

===Original===
“What did he say?”

“He says, ‘so have I, if it comes to that.’ But he turned and went out without another word. Oh, sir, I hope I haven’t done wrong in telling you.”

“Don’t get excited, you have done quite right, but you must not tell anyone else, mind that.”

“I will, sir, and I am sure that he had nothing to do with it, sir. That’s why I said nothing about it.”

“Now, one more question, and I have done. Think carefully before you answer. Was it Sir James who answered from the room?”
===Chinese===
“他怎么说？”

“他说：‘真到那个地步，我也有一支。’不过他转过身，一句话没再说就出去了。哦，先生，但愿我告诉您这些，不算做错了事。”

“别激动，您做得对，只是绝不可再对任何人讲起，千万记住。”

“我不会讲的，先生，而且我敢肯定，他同这件事没有一点关系，先生。正因为这样，我当时才只字未提。”

“好了，再问一个问题，就问完了。回答之前仔细想一想。在房里应声的，是詹姆斯爵士本人吗？”

===Original===
All the colour fled from her face, and she seemed on the verge of a collapse.

“Oh, sir, I don’t know. I have thought of nothing else since this terrible affair. I don’t believe it was. It was like him, but muffled and sort of mumbling.” She was shivering now, and on the verge of breaking down. Collins drew a flask from his pocket and took out the stopper.

“Drink this. It will do you good. I have finished now.”

“Thank you, sir,” she said, taking a deep drink.
===Chinese===
她脸上的血色霎时褪尽，眼看就要瘫倒了。

“哦，先生，我说不上。打这桩可怕的事情发生以来，我心里想的没有别的，全是这个问题。我不相信那是他。声音像他，可是发闷，含含糊糊的，像是咕哝出来的。”这时她浑身发抖，眼看就要支撑不住。科林斯从口袋里掏出一只酒壶，拔开了塞子。

“把这个喝了。对您有好处。我已经问完了。”

“谢谢您，先生。”她说着，深深呷了一大口。

===Original===
“And you won’t tell anyone. You must keep absolutely silent. I shall not tell unless I find it necessary. Now, mind, not a word. You will do infinite harm if you do.”

The effect of the strong drink had made her maudlin, and she sank weeping on a chair.

“Oh, what would Miss Mabel say?” she said between her sobs.

Collins gave one look at her, and then left the room. He had got the information he wanted.
===Chinese===
“还有，您不能告诉任何人。您必须绝对保持缄默。除非我发觉确有必要，我也不会说出去。记住，一个字也不许露。您若是说了出去，会造成无穷的祸害。”

烈酒下了肚，她变得伤感涕零，颓然坐到椅子上哭了起来。

“哦，梅布尔小姐会怎么说呢？”她抽抽噎噎地说。

科林斯朝她望了一眼，便离开了房间。他想要的情报，已经到手了。

## VIII. Enter Mr. Allery / 第八章 艾勒里先生登场

===Original===
After the inquest there was nothing to keep Miss Watson in London. A sad cortege started from Leveson Square after dark, for, in the circumstances, they had no wish for public display.

The body of the dead statesman was being taken to Devonshire for burial. In all the arrangements Collins had played an important but unobtrusive part, and now, on the departure platform, he had quietly seen to her comfort in the little ways a woman appreciates.

Papers were ready, seats booked at the dining table, and the carriage reserved by a well-tipped guard.

===Chinese===
死因研讯结束后，再没有什么能把沃森小姐留在伦敦了。一行哀伤的送葬人马趁天黑后从勒维森广场出发——处此境况，他们不愿招摇于众人眼前。

这位亡故政治家的遗体将运往德文郡安葬。在各项安排中，科林斯出力甚多，却不露痕迹；此刻在发车的站台上，他又悄悄打点，让她一路舒适——用的都是女人最领情的那些细微法子。

车票证件一应备齐，餐车的座位订好了，车厢也由一名得了丰厚赏钱的列车员预留了下来。

===Original===
Eric Sanders had already gone on, as it was necessary to go through all the dead man’s papers, and, as private secretary, he was assisting the police in this matter.

An aged and lachrymose aunt had turned up from somewhere, and was acting chaperon.

The old nurse had been relegated to what the aunt called her proper place. Collins had completely won the heart of the old nurse with an easy courtesy which is neither patronage nor familiarity, and she had sworn by him ever since he had taken them to the theatre. On that occasion he had, with great tact, laid himself out to amuse the old lady, while Mabel was left to her sulky Eric, which accentuated the contrast.

Now he had taken as much care in seeing to the comfort of the two old servants (for the butler was going back with them), as with Miss Watson herself﻿—a fact she was quick to note and appreciate.

===Chinese===
埃里克·桑德斯已先行一步，因为死者的全部文件都必须逐一清查，而作为私人秘书，他正在此事上协助警方。

一位上了年纪、动辄垂泪的姑母不知从哪儿冒了出来，充任起监护陪伴的年长女伴。

老乳母则被打发回姑母所谓的“分内位置”上去。科林斯以一份既不居高临下、又不过分亲昵的从容礼数，彻底赢得了老乳母的心；自他带她们去瞧戏那回起，她便对他信服得五体投地。那天晚上，他极其得体地想方设法逗老太太开心，梅布尔则守着她那位闷闷不乐的埃里克——两相对照，愈发分明。

如今，他对两位老仆人（老管家也要随他们一同回去）旅途舒适与否的操心，丝毫不亚于对沃森小姐本人——这一层，她很快就察觉了，并且记在心上。

===Original===
They were standing on the platform, waiting for the train to start, and Mabel gave him her hand.

“I am most awfully grateful to you for all you have done,” she said. “I don’t know how I should have got on without you. This has been a very trying time, but it would have been far worse but for your management.” He was pleased at the compliment.

“It has been a real pleasure to do what I could, and no trouble, for I have had to be in the thick of things, from my position.”

A cloud crossed her face.

===Chinese===
两人站在站台上，等着火车开动，梅布尔向他伸出了手。

“您所做的一切，我真不知该怎样感谢才好，”她说，“没有您，我简直不知道怎么撑过来。这段日子实在难熬，若不是有您操持，还要糟得多。”这番话让他颇为受用。

“能尽一份力，我由衷高兴，也毫不费事——以我的身份，本来就免不了身处这一切事务的中心。”

她脸上掠过一丝阴云。

===Original===
“Yes, of course. You are trying to find the criminal.”

“I am trying first to find your brother.”

“Oh, I hope you do. It would be so comforting at this time if he were here.”

“I will let you know directly I hear anything.”

“Oh, but I hope you will do more than that. You must come and see us when we are more settled,” she added wistfully.

===Chinese===
“是啊，当然。您是在追查凶手。”

“我首先是想找到你哥哥。”

“哦，但愿您找到。这种时候，他若能守在身边，该是多大的安慰。”

“一有消息，我立刻告诉你。”

“哦，可我希望的不止这些。等我们安顿下来，您一定要来看我们。”她又添了一句，语气里满是渴望。

===Original===
They were joined by Mr. Allery, the senior partner of Allery & Watkins, the family lawyers. Collins and he had seen a good deal of each other during these few days, and had grown to like each other.

He took Mabel’s hand in a fatherly way.

“Now, you must not get down there and fret and worry. It’s all terribly sad, but you are young, and when you have got over the first grief, you must rouse yourself up. There’s nothing worse than moping.”

“Then the best way you can help is to come and see me,” she said with a smile, “and bring Mr. Collins with you. You are both golfers, and there are several excellent links near us.”

“I shall be delighted,” said the lawyer. “I need a change. Only you must remind me, or I shall never come,” and he laughed in a genial, full-blooded way.

He had a charming, old-world courtesy, and what in doctors is called a good bedside manner.

===Chinese===
艾勒里（Allery）先生这时走了过来，同他们站在一处，他是这家人的律师行“艾勒里与沃特金斯”（Allery & Watkins）的高级合伙人。这几天科林斯同他过从颇多，两人已渐生好感，彼此投缘。

他慈父般地握住梅布尔的手。

“听我说，到了那边，可不能愁眉苦脸、自寻烦恼。这一切固然伤心透顶，可你还年轻，熬过头一阵哀痛，就得打起精神来。天底下没有比顾影自怜更糟的事了。”

“那么，您能帮我的最好法子，就是来看我，”她含笑说，“把科林斯先生也一并带来。你们两位都打高尔夫，我们家附近就有几处顶好的球场。”

“那我太乐意了，”律师说，“我正需要换换环境。只是你得记着催我，不然我一辈子也去不成。”说罢，他发出一阵爽朗浑厚的笑声。

他有一种迷人的老派礼数，兼有医生行里所谓的出色“临诊风度”。

===Original===
The train moved off, leaving the two men standing on the platform.

“Poor girl,” said Allery. “I have known her since she was a baby. It’s a sad blow to her, but, between ourselves, it may be a good thing. Sir James was a fine man, but he was very selfish with her. She was absolutely tied up with him, and could go nowhere. He warned off all possible suitors, and made her live the life of a nun.”

“I gather that Sanders rather bit his fingers there.” Allery looked at him shrewdly.

“You haven’t taken long to find that out,” he said. “Yes, I believe he did. He is an able young fellow, of good family, and in every way desirable, but for some reason Sir James would not hear of an engagement. Of course, it was very awkward, as he was his private secretary, and Sir James was too fair minded a man to get rid of him for domestic reasons which would have ruined his prospects. But it couldn’t have gone on. He actually talked to me about altering his will if Mabel did not give Eric up.”

===Chinese===
火车开动了，把他们两人撇在站台上。

“可怜的姑娘，”艾勒里说，“她还在襁褓里我就认得她了。这对她是沉重的一击。不过咱们私下说说，这兴许倒是件好事。詹姆斯爵士人固然出色，对她却自私得很。她整个儿被他拴住了，哪儿也去不成。凡是可能的追求者，都被他一个个挡了回去，让她过着修女一般的日子。”

“我料想，桑德斯在那上头没少碰钉子。”艾勒里精明地看了他一眼。

“你这么快就摸出来了。”他说，“是啊，我看他确实碰了钉子。那是个能干的青年，家世又好，各方面都无可挑剔，可不知怎么，詹姆斯爵士就是不肯松口提订婚。当然，事情很尴尬，因为他是他的私人秘书，而詹姆斯爵士为人太公道，不肯为了家事缘故辞掉他——那会毁了他的前程。可这样下去终究不是办法。他当真同我谈过，要是梅布尔不肯放弃埃里克，他就要改遗嘱。”

===Original===
Collins looked interested.

“We can’t stand talking here. Let me give you a lift in my car. Where can I take you?”

“That’s most kind of you. I have an hour before my train goes, so I shall just go to my club. You’d better come in for a few minutes.”

In the club smoking room, after a general talk, Collins gently led the conversation back to the subject about which he wanted information.

===Chinese===
科林斯露出了感兴趣的神色。

“咱们总不能站在这儿说话。坐我的车吧，我送你一程。送你去哪儿？”

“那太谢谢你了。离我上火车还有一个钟头，我就到俱乐部去坐坐。你不如也进来待上几分钟。”

在俱乐部吸烟室里寒暄了一阵之后，科林斯不着痕迹地把话头引回了那个他想要打听的题目上。

===Original===
“You knew young Watson, I suppose?” The lawyer’s face hardened.

“Yes,” he said shortly, “I knew him. A wastrel.”

“I rather gathered,” said Collins, “that he was just a somewhat wild youth, who went the pace at Oxford.”

“I am afraid it did not stop at that,” said the other, and closed his mouth in a way that indicated he did not wish to continue. Collins saw it, and asked no more.

Allery, however, volunteered one bit of information.

===Chinese===
“小沃森，你该是认得的吧？”律师的脸色顿时一沉。

“认得，”他简短地说，“我认得他。一个败家子。”

“我倒以为，”科林斯说，“他不过是年少荒唐了些，在牛津放浪了一阵罢了。”

“恐怕事情不止于此。”对方说完便闭上了嘴，那神情分明表示不愿再谈下去。科林斯看在眼里，也就不再追问。

不过艾勒里倒主动透露了一点内情。

===Original===
“Sir James made a curious will. It will be proved soon, and so there is no particular secret. If his son was found, and was willing to settle down in Devonshire, he was to have a half share after certain small legacies had been paid. If not, the whole was to go to Mabel. She gets half in any case. It was left to me to decide whether he was fulfilling the conditions. That’ll be a nice task.”

“Supposing we can’t find him.”

“Then Mabel takes her half and the interest on the other, which remains in trust, until it is decided that he is dead. The discretion rests with me.”

“A very reasonable arrangement,” said Collins.

===Chinese===
“詹姆斯爵士立了份古怪的遗嘱。很快就要呈请认证，所以也算不得什么特别的秘密。倘若他的儿子找了回来，又愿意在德文郡安家定居，那么在付清几笔小额遗赠之后，他可以得一半产业；若不然，全部归梅布尔。无论如何，她都得一半。至于那儿子算不算履行了条件，全由我裁定。这可真是份好差事。”

“要是我们找不到他呢。”

“那梅布尔取她应得的一半，另一半留在信托里，收益归她，直到判定他死亡为止。裁量权也握在我手里。”

“安排得非常在理。”科林斯说。

## IX. A Confession / 第九章 供词

===Original===
Sinclair and Boyce were sitting in the former’s room, waiting for Collins.

“I wonder how he will like this?” said Boyce.

“Not at all,” said the other, making a face. “He is so fond of a problem that he dislikes a simple solution to anything.”

He shuffled uncomfortably. He did not altogether like it, himself. Collins entered and greeted the others.

===Chinese===
辛克莱和博伊斯坐在前者的房间里，等着科林斯。

“不知他见了这个会作何感想？”博伊斯说。

“一点儿也不会喜欢，”对方说着，做了个鬼脸。“他太迷恋难题了，无论什么事，简单明了的答案他都看不入眼。”

他浑身不自在地挪动了一下。这事连他自己心里也不大是滋味。科林斯进来，同两人打了招呼。

===Original===
“Well?” he said. “I got your message.”

Boyce took his cigar from his mouth.

“We have got a confession of the murder of Sir James.” If he expected Collins to show any feeling, he was disappointed.

“Who is it?” he said, quietly seating himself.

“A man called Jackson. He came into the Vine Street police station late last night and said he wished to give himself up for the murder.”

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”他说，“口信我收到了。”

博伊斯从嘴边取下雪茄。

“有人投案，供认谋杀了詹姆斯爵士。”倘若他指望科林斯闻言动容，那他失望了。

“是谁？”他说着，从容落座。

“一个叫杰克逊的人。昨天深夜他走进瓦因街（Vine Street）警察局，说要为这桩凶案投案自首。”

===Original===
“Of course,” said Collins contemptuously, “we shall have plenty of them.”

“Wait a moment. This man is well known to the police. He has been in an asylum for years, but unfortunately his people got him certified sane, and had him out. He had homicidal tendencies. He has made a complete statement.”

“A statement from a lunatic. Really, Boyce.”

“Well, listen to this, then,” said Boyce, and spread a document out on the desk.

He began to read:

===Chinese===
“那还用说，”科林斯不屑地说，“这种人往后有的是。”

“先别忙。这个人在警察局里是挂了号的。他在疯人院里关了好些年，不幸的是，他家里人设法摘到了一纸‘神志正常’的鉴定，把他弄了出去。此人有杀人的狂性。他已经写下了一份完完整整的供状。”

“一个疯子的供词。得了吧，博伊斯。”

“那好，你且听听这个。”博伊斯说着，把一份文件在桌上摊开。

他开始念道：

===Original===
“I, John Henry Jackson, being of sound mind, and having been warned by the police, declare the following statement to be a true account of what I have done. On the afternoon of the —﻿—﻿—, I killed that dog, Sir James Watson, because he is not fit to live. He will not hear the cry of the prisoner or the oppressed, but his heart has been hardened like Pharoah. I went to his house in Leveson Square, which I had been watching for an opportunity, and he opened the door to me. He was delivered into my hands, but I would not slay him then. I knew that the time had come, so I wrote to the Central News Agency, with whom I have often had correspondence, and told them of his death. I had called at Scotland Yard some days before and seen Superintendent Sinclair.” Boyce paused, and Sinclair looked up with a start.

“While he was not looking, I took some of his paper, and a letter he had signed, and so I wrote on the paper to show that it was not murder. Sir James came out and posted a letter, and I nearly killed him then, but the time had not come, so I followed him into the house, and shot him. It is a good deed and I do not mind having done it.

(Signed) J. H. Jackson.

===Chinese===
“我，约翰·亨利·杰克逊（John Henry Jackson），神志健全，并已听受警察之告诫，兹声明以下陈述确为本人所作所为的真实记录。某日下午，我杀掉了那条狗——詹姆斯·沃森爵士——因为他不配活在世上。他不肯倾听囚徒与受压迫者的呼号，他的心已刚硬如法老。我去他在勒维森广场的宅子——那宅子我早盯上了，专候时机——他亲自给我开了门。他既已交付在我手中，但那时我还不愿杀他。我知道时辰已至，于是写信给中央新闻社——我素来与他们通信频繁——把他的死讯告知了他们。几天之前，我曾到苏格兰场登门，见到了辛克莱警司。”博伊斯顿了顿，辛克莱猛地抬起头来。

“趁他不留意，我拿了他的一些信纸，又拿了一封他签过字的信，于是我就在那信纸上写下字句，好表明这不是谋杀。詹姆斯爵士出门投寄了一封信，我那时险些就杀了他，但时辰未到，我便尾随他进了宅子，开枪打死了他。这是一桩义举，我做了也不觉得有什么。

（签名）J. H. 杰克逊。

===Original===
“Well, what do you make of that?”

Sinclair and Collins looked up, and their eyes met.

“Absolute rubbish,” said Collins, “the man is obviously a lunatic.”

“What about the reference to you?” said Boyce to Sinclair.

“I do remember a wild man coming here with some grievance. I don’t know how he got in at all. Lewis let him in, but I have no recollection of him taking any letter of mine.”

“Still, he may have done,” said Boyce.

===Chinese===
“怎么样，二位有何高见？”

辛克莱和科林斯抬起头来，四目相接。

“一派胡言，”科林斯说，“这人明摆着是个疯子。”

“那提到你的那一段呢？”博伊斯问辛克莱。

“我确实记得有这么个撒野的人来过这儿，嚷着有什么冤情。我根本不知道他是怎么进来的。是刘易斯放他进来的，可我完全不记得他拿走过我什么信件。”

“话虽如此，兴许还是拿了。”博伊斯说。

===Original===
“Of course,” said the other. “There is always a tray full of typewritten letters of no great importance, waiting to go out. They would not be registered till they had been collected and one might have gone. It’s possible.”

“The whole thing is preposterous. What about the telephone messages that Sinclair and I had?” said Collins.

“Oh, he says he sent them from a Call Office.”

Collins laughed. “This is all your doing, Boyce. I knew how it would be. You let the whole story out to the papers, and this lunatic has been turning it over in his mind till he really believes he has done it. It’s quite a common phase. I wonder you take the trouble to go into the matter.”

“Wait a moment. You always think no one can be right but yourself. You have not heard all. A policeman has identified him. He saw him several times hanging about Leveson Square. And he has been further examined and his story hangs together. I don’t think there is any doubt that we have got the right man.”

===Chinese===
“这个自然，”对方说，“案头总搁着满满一托盘打字誊清的信件，都不是什么要紧的，等着发出。那些信要等一并收走后才逐封登记，混丢一封谁也不会察觉。有此可能。”

“整件事荒唐透顶。那辛克莱和我接到的两通电话，又怎么说？”科林斯道。

“哦，他说那是从公用电话局打的。”

科林斯笑了。“这全是你一手惹出来的，博伊斯。我早料到会有这一出。你把全部案情捅给了报界，这个疯子翻来覆去地琢磨，琢磨到最后真信了人是他杀的。这种病症屡见不鲜。真亏你还肯费神去查究。”

“先别急。你总以为除了自己，谁也不许是对的。你还没听完呢。有一名警察把他认了出来——曾好几回瞧见他在勒维森广场一带转悠。而且他又受了进一步的盘问，供词环环相扣，对得上茬。我认为毫无疑义，我们已经拿住了正主儿。”

===Original===
“I am perfectly sure you haven’t,” said Collins.

Boyce turned to him. “Of course it will be a disappointment to you. I know you always like something out of the way, with mystery and special features. What do you think, Sinclair?”

“I don’t know, sir, I don’t know what to say. I don’t somehow think he is our man.”

“I am quite sure he is; he’s got to be. You understand. And I expect loyal support from my Department,” said Boyce, with decision.

“It won’t do, Boyce, old fellow,” said Collins.

===Chinese===
“我倒可以完全肯定，你们没有。”科林斯说。

博伊斯转向他。“这自然叫你大失所望。我知道你向来偏爱不入俗套的东西，要有谜团，要有别致的看点。你怎么看，辛克莱？”

“我说不好，先生，我不知道该说什么。可不知怎么的，我总觉得他不是我们要找的人。”

“我确信就是他；也非得是他不可。你心里明白。我指望本部门上下忠心支持。”博伊斯斩钉截铁地说。

“这一手行不通，博伊斯老兄。”科林斯说。

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

“I’ll tell you. Have you ever seen the Mikado? If so you may remember a song beginning, ‘As some day it may happen that a victim must be found.’ ”

“I am afraid I don’t follow you,” said the other coldly.

“Between us three there is no need for bluff. You are being worried by all kinds of people to solve the problem. You see that your professional reputation is at stake and that much hangs on it. And you see here a good chance of finding a victim, who will not suffer any hardship in any case, as he obviously ought to be under lock and key.

“It is the Stenie Morrison case over again, only here you will not have to have an innocent man tried, because this man will not be able to plead. It will all work out finely. This man will go to Broadmoor, where he will be quite happy, and there will be much praise for the smart Commissioner.”

===Chinese===
“你这是什么意思？”

“我告诉你。你看过《日本天皇》（The Mikado）吗？看过的话，兴许记得一支歌，开头唱道：‘说不定哪一天，或许就得找出一个牺牲者来。’”

“恕我跟不上你的话。”对方冷冷地说。

“咱们三人之间，用不着打马虎眼。各路人马都在催逼你破案，搅得你不得安生。你眼看自己的职业声名系于此案，干系非小。而眼前恰好有个送上门的牺牲品——反正这人横竖吃不了什么亏，他本来就明摆着该关起来才是。

“这不过是斯特尼·莫里森案（Stenie Morrison case）的重演，只是这一回，你不必让无辜的人受审，因为这个人压根儿无法答辩。一切都顺顺当当地了结。此人送进布罗德莫尔（Broadmoor），在那儿他会快快活活，而那位精明干练的总监，自会博得一片赞誉。”

===Original===
Boyce flushed scarlet. “You mean I should fake up a charge against a man I knew was not guilty,” he said.

Collins shrugged his shoulders. “I have known it done,” he said, and turned to Sinclair. “Well, here ends the wonderful mystery of Leveson Square. There will be no difficulty in getting a case. I have not been at the Bar for nothing. There will be no defence, because there will be no trial. Personally, I could drive a horse and cart through the whole thing. So could you. But it will be beautifully stage-managed.”

Boyce rose in anger. “I suppose because you are a freelance you think you can say what you like, unless you are trying to make a joke in rather doubtful taste. I do not see that any useful purpose would be served by continuing this discussion,” and he went out.

The other two looked at each other. Collins burst out laughing.

===Chinese===
博伊斯涨得满脸通红。“你的意思是，我该对一个明知无辜的人捏造罪名。”他说。

科林斯耸了耸肩。“这种事我见过有人做。”他说，随即转向辛克莱。“好了，勒维森广场这桩妙不可言的疑案，至此落幕。要立个案子毫不费事。我这些年大律师不是白当的。不会有辩护，因为根本不会有审判。就我个人而言，这案子我能驳得体无完肤，你也能。可这出戏，自会有人把它导演得漂漂亮亮。”

博伊斯怒气冲冲地站起身。“想必因为你是局外自由身，就觉得可以信口开河——要么你就是存心讲个趣味颇嫌低级的玩笑。我看不出把这番讨论继续下去还有什么益处。”说罢，他走了出去。

余下两人面面相觑。科林斯放声大笑。

===Original===
“Cheer up,” he said, “you look as if you had just missed backing the winner.”

“That’s just what I have done,” said Sinclair gloomily. “It’s all right for you, but I have to do what I am told. I know this is all wrong.”

“Do you? So do I,” said Collins quietly.

The other looked up quickly. “You are very certain.”

“Exactly. And so are you.” They both stared hard at each other for a moment. “I wonder what you have got hold of?” said Sinclair.

“That’s just what I was wondering,” said Collins.

===Chinese===
“打起精神来，”他说，“瞧你这副模样，活像刚错过了给头马下注似的。”

“我不正是错过了嘛。”辛克莱郁郁地说。“你自然说得轻巧，我却只能奉命行事。我知道这案子全不对头。”

“是吗？我也知道。”科林斯平静地说。

对方霍然抬眼。“你倒是笃定得很。”

“正是。你也一样。”两人都狠狠盯了对方好一阵。“我倒想知道，你手里究竟攥住了什么？”辛克莱说。

“我正纳闷的，也是这个。”科林斯说。

===Original===
“There’s one thing. This will put the real man right off. He will think he’s safe and may return,” said Sinclair.

“Return? What do you mean?”

“Oh, nothing.”

A clerk entered, and laid an early edition of the *Evening Rag* on the table. Sinclair picked it up.

“Read that,” he said, indignantly.

===Chinese===
“有一桩事。这么一来，真凶可就彻底高枕无忧了。他会觉得自己稳当了，说不定还会回来。”辛克莱说。

“回来？你这是什么意思？”

“哦，没什么。”

一名文员走进来，把一份早版的《晚间小报》放在桌上。辛克莱拿了起来。

“你瞧瞧这个。”他愤然说道。

===Original===
Across the page was printed:

**“Murder of the Home Secretary,”

“Suspect Arrested,”

“Makes Full Confession.”**

There followed an account of how that brilliant Civil Servant, F. D. Boyce, Commissioner of Police, after devoting his nights and days to the problem, had at last effected the arrest of a violent lunatic who had made a full confession of the dastardly crime.

===Chinese===
横贯整版赫然印着：

**“内政大臣遇害”，

					“嫌犯落网”，

					“全盘供认。”**

接下去是一篇报道，说那位才华出众的文官、警察总监 F. D. 博伊斯，如何为破案日夜操劳，终于将一名狂暴的疯子捉拿归案，此人对那桩滔天罪行已供认不讳。

===Original===
There followed a paragraph in praise of their worthy official, and in self-laudation of the Press generally, whose cooperation had been so effectual.

Collins put it down with a smile.

“So that’s that,” he said. “I wash my hands of the case.”

On his way back to his flat he stopped at a Post Office, and sent off a wire. “Delighted to accept your kind invitation. Will come tomorrow,” and addressed it to “Miss Watson, The Vale, Holbrook.”

===Chinese===
其后还有一段文字，把这位可敬的官员称颂了一番，又把报界自己也夸奖了一通，说他们通力合作，成效卓著。

科林斯微微一笑，把报纸放下。

“就这么着吧，”他说，“这案子我撒手不管了。”

回公寓的路上，他在一家邮局停了停，发出去一封电报：“承蒙盛情相邀，欣然从命。明日抵达。”收报人是霍尔布鲁克谷园的沃森小姐。

## X. The Portrait / 第十章 画像

===Original===
A surprise awaited Collins on his arrival at Wilton-on-Sea. Eric Sanders was on the platform, and came forward with a pleasant smile. He was a changed being. The sulky petulance was gone, and he seemed like a man from whom a load of care has been removed. His manner was friendly without being effusive.

Collins surveyed him keenly.

He was too used to studying human nature not to notice the change, and too clever to show that he saw it.

They drove to the Vale among the autumn trees and over the hills from whence magnificent views stretched out beneath them. Eric opened the conversation.

===Chinese===
科林斯抵达滨海威尔顿时，一件出乎意料的事正等着他。埃里克·桑德斯就站在月台上，面带愉快的笑容迎上前来。他像换了个人。那副阴沉沉的乖戾神气不见了，仿佛卸下了压在心头的重担。他待人友善，却不过分热络。

科林斯锐利地打量着他。

他研究人性惯了，断不会看不出这一变化；可他又太聪明，决不显出自己看在了眼里。

汽车穿过秋日的树林，翻过山丘，向谷园驶去，壮丽的景色在脚下层层铺展。埃里克先开了口。

===Original===
“You people have done a smart bit of work capturing the murderer of Sir James so quickly,” he said. “The papers were full of it this morning.”

“Yes,” said Collins, dryly. “I read them on the way down.”

“He seems to be a desperate ruffian. I didn’t quite make out how it was he was actually caught.”

“He wasn’t,” said Collins, “he gave himself up.”

“Oh, I see. It was not clear in the account,” he looked at Collins doubtfully wondering how far it was right to ask him questions without breach of etiquette.

===Chinese===
“你们干得真利落，这么快就抓住了杀害詹姆斯爵士的凶手。”他说，“今天早上的报纸登得满满当当。”

“是啊。”科林斯淡淡地说，“来的路上我读过了。”

“他看上去是个亡命的恶徒。我没太弄明白，他究竟是怎么落网的。”

“他不是被抓住的，”科林斯说，“他是自首的。”

“哦，原来如此。报道里没写清楚。”他望着科林斯，将信将疑，拿不准问到什么份上方才不失礼数。

===Original===
“This is a wonderful piece of country,” said Collins. “It’s one of the finest views I know just before we go down again into the valley. It’s like the view from the Delectable Mountains.”

“You’ve read the *Pilgrim’s Progress*?” said the other in surprise.

Collins gave a laugh. “Oh, I don’t spend all my time in bones and blood, though problems do interest me.”

At the risk of courting a snub Eric said, “I expect you are sorry this one is over so quickly?”

“Oh, there will be plenty more,” said he lightly.

===Chinese===
“这一带的乡野真美。”科林斯说，“重新下到谷里之前的那段路，是我平生见过的最好的景色之一。就像从怡乐山（Delectable Mountains）上望出去的风光。”

“您读过《天路历程》？”对方惊讶地说。

科林斯笑了。“哦，我并非一天到晚泡在白骨和鲜血里，虽说疑案确实让我着迷。”

埃里克冒着碰钉子的风险说：“这一桩这么快就了结，您恐怕觉得遗憾吧？”

“哦，往后有的是。”他轻描淡写地说。

===Original===
On their arrival Mabel met them at the door and greeted Collins warmly﻿—a trifle too warmly﻿—there was just a touch of over effusiveness, which his quick eye noticed.

“We’re quite a party,” she said. “It is really too big so shortly after﻿—” she hesitated: “while we are in mourning. Mr. Allery is here, with his wife and daughter.”

When they sat down to dinner that night there was indeed an atmosphere of quiet enjoyment far removed from the horror of the past days. Mr. Allery had had a word with Collins.

“I came as a duty. I was so much afraid that the poor little girl would mope. It’s no earthly good crying over spilt milk. She has all her life before her. Besides,” he added with a smile, “I think her old aunt is far from an ideal chaperon. My wife is used to all occasions.”

===Chinese===
两人抵达时，梅布尔在门口迎候，热络地招呼科林斯——只是热络得略过了头——那一点过分的亲热没能逃过他敏锐的眼睛。

“我们这一伙人可不算少。”她说，“这么快就摆这么大的排场，实在……”她迟疑道，“我们还在服丧呢。艾勒里（Allery）先生来了，带着太太和女儿。”

当晚众人落座用晚餐时，席间的确是一片安宁的欢愉，与连日来的恐怖恍如隔世。艾勒里先生先前已同科林斯攀谈过几句。

“我是尽一份本分来的。我实在放心不下，怕这可怜的小姑娘闷出病来。覆水难收，哭也无益。她往后的日子还长着呢。再说，”他含笑补了一句，“我看她那位老姑妈可算不上理想的陪护人。我太太什么场面都应付得来。”

===Original===
“You mean?”

“You’ll see, my boy,” said the old lawyer with a chuckle, “The course of true love is running smoother.”

Then the ladies had come in.

The dinner was a merry one; Allery had a fund of humour culled from his long experience, and he found an able supporter in Collins. Sanders was no fool, and now that he was absolutely happy he took his part. He had taken Miss Allery in, but Collins noted that he was sitting next to Mabel. Collins had taken in the Aunt, who was only a cousin of Sir James. He was sitting with his back to the windows from which the setting sun still shone into the room, for they had dined early. In front of him was a great fireplace, and over the mantel was a large portrait of Sir James in court dress.

===Chinese===
“您是指？”

“到时候你就明白了，小伙子。”老律师呵呵一笑，“真爱之路越走越顺了。”

随后女士们进了餐厅。

这顿晚饭吃得很欢畅。艾勒里阅世既久，攒下满腹幽默，科林斯又是个得力的捧场人。桑德斯并非庸手，如今诸事顺遂、心花怒放，也来凑趣。他挽艾勒里小姐入席，科林斯却注意到他坐在梅布尔身旁。科林斯陪的则是那位姑妈——她不过是詹姆斯爵士的一位表亲。他背对窗户而坐，落日的余晖仍从窗口照进屋来，因为晚饭用得早。他面前是一座大壁炉，壁炉架上方悬着詹姆斯爵士一幅身着宫廷礼服的大画像。

===Original===
“Fancy,” Sanders was saying, “I find Mr. Collins spends his spare time reading the *Pilgrim’s Progress*.”

“And very good taste, too,” said Allery. “It contains some of the most glorious pieces of English ever written.

“Not one of our modern writers can touch it.”

It was getting dark in the room.

“I think,” said the aunt, “we might have a light, my dear?”

“Certainly,” said Mabel. “John, turn on the electric light.”

===Chinese===
“真没想到，”桑德斯正说，“我发现科林斯先生竟把闲暇时光用来读《天路历程》。”

“而且品位也很高。”艾勒里说，“那里面有历来用英文写就的最光辉的一些篇章。

我们当代的作家，没有一个能望其项背。”

屋里渐渐暗了下来。

“我看，”姑妈说，“咱们可以点灯了吧，亲爱的？”

“当然。”梅布尔说，“约翰，把电灯打开。”

===Original===
At that moment a last ray, almost bloodred, came from the dying sun through the window, and shone full on the portrait over the fireplace. Collins was idly looking at it, when his face suddenly became rigid and fixed. An intent look came into his eyes, and he stared hard at the portrait. Then the brilliant light came on. At that moment he felt rather than saw that Mabel was watching him. He turned to her and she looked down in confusion, and a red pervaded her face. They both recovered and their eyes met. He read in them a certain uneasiness or dread.

Instantly he composed his features and said, “That’s better, but the sunset was very beautiful.”

“We’ve missed the first News bulletin,” said Allery, “but we must get the second. I always feel lost in the country when there’s no wireless. Miss Watson has a splendid receiving set.”

===Chinese===
就在那一刻，沉落的残阳吐出最后一缕近乎血红的余晖，穿过窗口，正正照在壁炉上方的画像上。科林斯原本正漫不经心地望着它，脸色忽然僵住凝固。他眼中露出专注的神情，死死盯住那幅画像。随即，明亮的灯光亮了起来。也就在那一刻，他感觉到——与其说看见，不如说感觉到——梅布尔正在注视他。他朝她转过头去，她慌乱地垂下眼帘，红晕漫上了脸。两人都回过神来，目光相遇。他从那双眼睛里读出了几分不安，或者说，恐惧。

他当即收敛神色，说道：“这样好多了，不过方才的落日真美。”

“第一次新闻广播咱们已经错过了，”艾勒里说，“第二次可得听听。一到乡下没有无线电，我总觉得心里没着落。沃森小姐有一台顶好的收音机。”

===Original===
While conversation was general Collins leant over to Allery.

“Better not say too much about the news,” he said. “There will be something about the murder, certain to be, and it may distress her.”

“Perhaps you are right, but as a matter of fact, she has been much more cheerful since she heard that they had got the man.”

===Chinese===
趁众人谈兴正浓，科林斯俯身凑近艾勒里。

“新闻的事，大家还是少说为妙。”他说，“里面肯定要提到那桩凶案，那是免不了的，说不定会让她伤心。”

“也许你说得对。不过说实在的，自从听说那人抓到了，她的精神好多了。”

===Original===
The receiving set was in the old oak-pannelled hall in a neat cabinet. The company foregathered here at ten o’clock for the news. Eric was the operator. After the hundred and fortieth chess move between two Scotsmen, and the usual dismal forecast of the weather, an account was given of the preliminary examination of Jackson, who was being kept under observation by Home Office doctors. Two facts emerged, that the Home Office was satisfied in a guarded way that he was the man, with plenty of the cautious word “alleged,” and that he was hopelessly mad. Collins smiled as he listened. He had seated himself on a cushion in the shadow where he could watch Mabel’s expressive face. He saw a look of relief, and something more, a puzzled look on her face.

After the news an announcement was broadcasted, as had been done for the past few days, asking for information as to the whereabouts of Ronald, now Sir Ronald Watson, last heard of at Monte Video, etc.

As the loud speaker announced this, Collins saw a swift glance pass between Mabel and Allery.

When the Savoy bands were in full blast, Sanders and Allery departed to finish an interrupted game of billiards. The four ladies continued a game of bridge. Collins had joined with neither party, but watched each in turn. When Mabel was “dummy” she came across to him. “I wish you were not out of things like this,” she said, “I feel I am not doing my duty as hostess.”

===Chinese===
收音机安放在老橡木护壁的大厅里，装在一只精巧的柜子中。十点钟，众人聚到这里听新闻，由埃里克掌机操作。两位苏格兰棋手的对局播到第一百四十着，加上照例一通阴郁的天气预报之后，播报了对杰克逊（Jackson）的初步侦讯——此人正由内政部的医生们监护观察。播出的内容说明两点：其一，内政部已谨慎地认定他就是凶手，满篇皆是“据称”这类字斟句酌的措辞；其二，他已经疯得无可救药。科林斯听着，含笑不语。他坐在暗处的一只坐垫上，从那里可以观察梅布尔富于表情的面庞。他看到她脸上一副如释重负的神情，还有别的——一种困惑的神色。

新闻过后，播出了同前几天一样的启事：征求有关罗纳德——如今已是罗纳德·沃森爵士——下落的线索，最后一次得到音讯是在蒙得维的亚，等等。

喇叭里播出这一条时，科林斯看见梅布尔和艾勒里飞快地交换了一个眼色。

萨伏伊乐队奏得正热闹的时候，桑德斯和艾勒里退席去续完一局先前未完的台球。四位女士接着打她们的桥牌。科林斯两边都没加入，只轮流在一旁观战。轮到梅布尔做“明手”时，她踱到他身边来。“我不愿看您这样被晾在一边，”她说，“我觉得自己没尽到女主人的本分。”

===Original===
“Not a bit of it,” he replied. “I am enjoying myself.”

“I suppose you are feeling more at rest now that this horrible affair has been cleared up?”
