“Of course it ends the matter as far as I am concerned﻿—for the present,” he said. “And you?”

“Oh, I told you,” she said. “I would much rather it turned out to be a man who was not responsible. There will be nothing done to him, I suppose?”

“He will be confined to Broadmoor for life, now. He has been there before, you know. They won’t let him out again.”

“How sad,” she said; “but it’s better than a man being hanged, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“哪里的话。”他答道，“我自得其乐着呢。”

“这桩可怕的案子水落石出了，您心里想必安稳多了吧？”

“就我而言，这事自然算是了结了——就目前而言。”他说，“你呢？”

“哦，我说过的。”她道，“我倒宁愿结果是个不负刑事责任的人。不会把他怎么样的，对吧？”

“他这回要被关进布罗德莫尔（Broadmoor），终身不放了。要知道，他先前就进过那儿。他们不会再放他出来了。”

“多惨哪。”她说，“不过总比把一个人送上绞架强，是不是？”

===Original===
“If he’s guilty,” said Collins.

“Of course they will have to prove their case, won’t they?” she said.

He gave a scornful laugh. “Oh, they will do that all right,” he said.

“Do you mean whether he is guilty or not? But that is too dreadful.”

“If a man once gets into the clutches of the Law it doesn’t matter much whether he’s guilty or not. He’s about as much chance as a fly in a spider’s net.”

===Chinese===
“那得看他是不是真有罪。”科林斯说。

“他们当然得先证实罪名成立，不是吗？”她说。

他轻蔑地一笑。“哦，这个他们自会办得妥妥帖帖。”他说。

“您是说，不论他有没有罪？那也太可怕了。”

“一个人一旦落入法律的爪牙，有没有罪已无关紧要。他的指望，同蛛网里的苍蝇差不多。”

===Original===
“What an awful thing. But you were a barrister once yourself?”

“That’s why I say that,” he answered with meaning.

“But we must not keep on talking about this, it will make you morbid.”

“Come on, Mabel, we are waiting for you,” came from the table.

===Chinese===
“这想法真可怕。可您从前自己不也当过出庭律师吗？”

“所以我才这么说。”他意味深长地答道。

“不过咱们可不能尽谈这些，谈多了您要钻牛角尖的。”

“快来呀，梅布尔，就等你了。”牌桌那边有人喊道。

===Original===
Collins strolled out into the garden where a bright moon was shining. What should he do? Let things slide altogether, and the Law take its course? That was best, but a curious streak of vanity and desire for mystery goaded him on to fresh research. There were other problems beside the main plot which called for solution.

There was the curious disappearance of Lewis. And what about Eric Sanders? Besides he grudged an easy triumph for the oily Boyce. He would like to upset his applecart.

The scent of the flowers and a cool breeze were delightful after London. He wandered round the house like a nocturnal cat, and came to the dining-room window. He stopped dead. Yes; he would have another look, while the others were busy.

Returning to the house he went to the dining-room and turned on the electric light. Sir James was staring down at him from over the mantelpiece. He lit a cigarette and sat on the edge of the table, gazing keenly at the portrait. He sat there motionless, in thought. He had an unusually keen sense and he felt, rather than saw, that someone had come into the room. He made no sign. A quiet voice at his elbow said, “It’s a very good likeness, and cleverly painted, isn’t it?” He turned without haste. Allery was standing beside him with an inscrutable smile on his face.

===Chinese===
科林斯踱进园子，一轮明月正朗照着。他该怎么办？索性撒手不管，听凭法律走完它的程序？那本是上策，可他心底那一线古怪的自负与嗜谜的欲望，又鞭策他作一番新的探究。这桩主案之外，还有别的问题有待解答。

刘易斯的离奇失踪，便是一桩。埃里克·桑德斯又怎么说？再者，他也不甘心让油滑的博伊斯轻轻松松全功告成。他倒很想搅了他的如意算盘。

花香与清凉的晚风，比伦敦惬意多了。他像一只夜游的猫绕着宅子逡巡，来到餐厅窗前，猛地站住。对；趁众人忙着的当口，他要再看上一眼。

他回到宅内，走进餐厅，打开电灯。詹姆斯爵士正从壁炉架上方俯视着他。他点上一支烟，坐在桌子边上，敏锐地凝视那幅画像。他坐在那里一动不动，陷入沉思。他的感官异乎寻常地灵敏，觉得——毋宁说是感觉到——有人进了屋。他不动声色。一个平静的声音在他肘边响起：“这张像画得真像，笔致也高明，是不是？”他不慌不忙地转过身。艾勒里站在他身旁，脸上挂着一丝高深莫测的微笑。

===Original===
“I came to look for you as we have finished, and I thought you might like a nightcap before turning in.”

“Thanks, I will come along,” said Collins. “I never saw Sir James to speak to. He had a remarkable face. A strange mixture of hardness and sympathy. The mouth is hard as a rock, but the eyes are sympathetic.”

“You are a student of these things, of course,” said the lawyer. “But you are quite right. He was a contradiction, but his intellect always ruled his emotions.”

“Was his son anything like him?”

“In character, yes; in face he was too young to say. He was undeveloped.”

Collins turned out the light and they went to join the others.

===Chinese===
“我们打完了，我来找你，想着你睡前也许乐意喝上一杯。”

“多谢，我就来。”科林斯说，“我从未同詹姆斯爵士说过话。他有一张不同寻常的脸。严酷与悲悯奇妙地交织。那张嘴硬得像岩石，那双眼睛却含着温情。”

“这些事你自然是行家。”律师说，“不过你说得完全对。他本人就是个矛盾体，但他的理智始终驾驭着感情。”

“他儿子像他吗？”

“性情像；长相嘛，他那时太年轻，还说不上。还没长开呢。”

科林斯关了灯，两人便去同众人会合。

## XI. An Apparition / 第十一章 幽灵显形

===Original===
The next three days passed outwardly in the usual enjoyments of a country house-party. They golfed and motored and played tennis. Behind the scenes many things were happening.

It was obvious to anyone that Eric Sanders and Mabel had come to an understanding, though a definite engagement so soon after the death of Sir James was repugnant to her.

On the other hand, so long as she had had to fight a battle with her father on behalf of Eric, she had been passionately devoted to him; but now that all opposition had ceased at the gates of the vault, and things had become easy, she almost resented the air of ownership with which Eric treated her. Womanlike she did not like being regarded as being cheaply won. She turned to Collins and was much in his company to show her lover that he had not a monopoly of her society.

===Chinese===
接下来的三天，表面上仍是乡间别墅聚会惯常的种种乐事。他们打高尔夫、兜风、打网球。暗地里，许多事情正在发生。

谁都看得出来，埃里克·桑德斯和梅布尔之间已有了默契，只是在詹姆斯爵士死后这么快便正式订婚，令她心生抗拒。

话说回来，当初为着埃里克同父亲抗争的时候，她曾一片热忱地爱着他；可如今一切反对都止于墓穴门口，事情变得顺遂了，埃里克待她那副占有者的做派，反倒叫她近乎生出怨恨。女人家的心性，她不愿被人看作轻易到手的战利品。她转而亲近科林斯，常与他做伴，好让她的恋人明白，他并无独占她交往的特权。

===Original===
Here was ground ready for sowing. He had gone through life with a cynical view of women, partly because he had come in contact with the sordid side of human nature, and partly because he had led a lonely life.

From the first moment that he had seen Mabel his interest had been aroused, and that interest was growing to something more intimate. She either did not or would not see, and the situation was soon acute. She was attracted to him by the very distaste for his profession, for she had inherited much of her father’s contradiction in character.

His invitation had been indefinite, and with all the party he was very popular. He carefully refrained from referring to the subject of the murder, and tried to lead the conversation away from it. And so the age-long battle began.

===Chinese===
这里正是一片现成待播的沃土。他半生都以玩世不恭的眼光看待女人，一半是因为他见惯了人性卑污的一面，一半是因为他一直过着孤独的日子。

从见到梅布尔的第一眼起，他的兴致便被勾了起来，而这兴致正渐渐酿成某种更亲密的东西。她要么没有看见，要么不愿看见，局面很快就变得一触即发。她恰恰是因着对他那一行的嫌恶才被吸引——她身上承袭了父亲性格中许多自相矛盾之处。

他的邀请本来就没说定期限，而他在全体宾客中也极得人缘。他小心翼翼地绝口不提凶案，还设法把话题从那上面引开。于是，那场亘古以来的战役打响了。

===Original===
An excursion had been arranged to the Cheddar Gorge and Caves, and as they passed down that wonderful panorama of riven rocks, unsurpassed in England, the grandeur of the scene drew them together. Each had a vein of savagery in their natures to which the wild aspect of the scenery appealed. Collins was driving the car, and Mabel had taken her place with him.

“Stop a moment here,” she whispered. “This is wonderful.”

Eric was sitting morosely in the back.

Far up, the trees growing on the edge of the precipice looked like the toy trees of a Japanese garden, and the fantastic rocks formed castles of fairy legend. The twisted road curved steeply down into the depths.

===Chinese===
一行人安排了一次去切达峡谷及洞穴的游览。当他们驶过那一带裂岩嶙峋的壮丽全景——在全英格兰无可匹敌——景色的雄浑便把众人的心拉到了一处。每个人天性里都潜藏着一丝野性，眼前荒莽的景致恰恰触动了它。科林斯亲自开车，梅布尔坐在他身旁的位子上。

“在这儿停一会儿，”她低声说，“太美了。”

埃里克闷闷不乐地坐在后座。

极高处，崖畔生长的树木宛如日本庭园里的假山小树，奇形怪状的岩石堆成一座座童话传说中的城堡。蜿蜒的道路陡峭地盘旋而下，直没深谷。

===Original===
“I would like to come here by moonlight,” she said. “It would be wonderful.”

“Why not have a picnic here, then?” he answered. “It is quite warm still.”

“We will,” she said gaily. “Come on, or the others will get restive. My aunt sees nothing in these rocks﻿—in fact, she is rather frightened.” They descended to the caves no less wonderful.

===Chinese===
“我倒想月夜再到这儿来，”她说，“那一定美极了。”

“那何不就在这儿野餐一回？”他答道，“天气还相当暖和呢。”

“就这么说定了。”她兴高采烈地说，“走吧，不然其他人该等得不耐烦了。我姑母可看不出这些岩石有什么好——说实在的，她还挺害怕呢。”他们随即下到那些毫不逊色的奇妙洞穴里。

===Original===
There were no tourists at that time so they made a party by themselves and went in with a guide.

Far in the depths of “Solomon’s Temple” with its amazing stalactites lit by electric light, they saw the entrance of a dark cave.

The guide was speaking: “That cave has been explored for over two miles, but is not open to the public. We do not know where it ends. There must be exits somewhere, or the air would not keep so fresh.”

“What a place for a criminal to hide from justice,” said Collins. “If one of our friends could make his way from Dartmoor here, he might stay for months with an accomplice to bring him food.”

“Only in the summer,” said the guide; “the caves are flooded in the winter.”

===Chinese===
那时节没有游客，他们便自成一行，跟着一名向导进了洞。

在“所罗门圣殿”的深处——洞中奇异的钟乳石被电灯照得通明——他们望见一个黑洞洞的洞口。

向导正在讲解：“那个洞已经探查了两英里开外，但不对外开放。谁也不知道它通到哪里。洞里必定另有出口，不然空气不会这么清新。”

“真是个罪犯躲藏避罪的绝妙去处，”科林斯说，“要是我们有位朋友能从达特穆尔一路摸到这儿，再有个同伙给他送饭，藏上几个月都不成问题。”

“那也只在夏天，”向导说，“一到冬天，洞里就全被水淹了。”

===Original===
Mabel shivered.

“How cold and damp it would be,” she said, “and fancy a storm coming on and the water dashing down on one from above.”

“Really, Mabel,” said Sanders, “you are getting very morbid, let’s get out and have some lunch.”

She turned on him.

===Chinese===
梅布尔打了个寒噤。

“那得又冷又湿，”她说，“再想想赶上暴风雨，水从头顶上劈头浇下来的滋味。”

“真是的，梅布尔，”桑德斯说，“你变得太阴郁了，快出去吃点午饭吧。”

她朝他转过身去。

===Original===
“How unromantic you are. I would like to buy this place, and turn it into a sort of Monte Christo’s cave.”

“And get chronic rheumatism,” said Allery, laughing.

“Well, I must give way to the craving for lunch,” she said, and led the way out.

===Chinese===
“你这人真没情调。我倒想把这地方买下来，把它变成一座基督山式的洞窟。”

“再落下一身慢性风湿。”艾勒里笑着说。

“好吧，我总得向馋虫让步了。”她说着，领头走了出去。

===Original===
When they had got back in the afternoon, Mabel’s old nurse met them with a startling announcement. Old John, the butler, had seen a ghost, and was prostrated with terror.

“Nonsense,” said Collins, “ghosts don’t come in the day time, it’s against all the rules of the game. Let’s have him up and question him.”

Mabel did not take it quite so lightly. “Poor old man,” she said, “he has been brooding on my father’s death, and I expect he has imagined things.”

“Better ask for the cellar key,” said Sanders.

===Chinese===
下午他们回到宅子，梅布尔的老乳母迎上来，带来一个惊人的消息：老管家约翰见了鬼，吓得瘫倒了。

“胡说，”科林斯说，“鬼魂白天是不出来的，这违反了游戏的一切规矩。把他叫上来问问吧。”

梅布尔可没有这么轻松。“可怜的老人，”她说，“他一直为我父亲的死郁郁不乐，我看他是自己想出些影子来了。”

“最好把酒窖的钥匙要回来。”桑德斯说。

===Original===
Mabel turned on him, angrily. “That’s a mean thing to say. John has been with us now for twenty-five years and I have never known him the worse for drink.”

Sanders hastily apologised.

Allery intervened. “Perhaps I had better see him, and talk to him.”

“If I may suggest it,” said Collins, “why not just send for him in the ordinary way, and let him tell his story? These old family servants are entitled to being treated to respect.”

===Chinese===
梅布尔气冲冲地朝他转过脸去。“这话说得可真刻薄。约翰在我们家做了二十五年了，我从没见他贪杯失态过。”

桑德斯连忙道歉。

艾勒里出来打圆场。“或许还是我去见见他，跟他谈谈。”

“要是我可以提个建议，”科林斯说，“何不就按平常的样子把他唤来，让他自己讲讲事情的经过？这些世家老仆，理应受到尊重的对待。”

===Original===
Mabel gave him a grateful look. Without waiting for an answer, Collins rang the bell. It was answered by John himself.

He was looking white and scared, but made no remark.

After giving some trivial order, Mabel said in a casual way:

“I hear you have had an unpleasant experience, John. Would you care to tell us about it?”

The old man was dying to do so, and plunged into his story at once.

===Chinese===
梅布尔向他投去感激的一瞥。科林斯不等有人答话，便按了铃。应铃而来的正是约翰本人。

他脸色发白，神色惊惶，却没有说话。

梅布尔先随口吩咐了一件无关紧要的小事，然后若无其事地说：

“听说你遇上了不痛快的事，约翰。愿意讲给我们听听吗？”

老人正巴不得一吐为快，立刻便讲了起来。

===Original===
“Thank you, Miss Mabel, it has upset me somewhat, but I am sorry I mentioned it. While you were away I had occasion to go into the dining-room to fetch some silver. When I opened the door there was Sir James standing on the hearthrug as plain as life.”

“How was he dressed?” said Collins, quickly.

“He was in court dress, with a sword on, and he was holding his hat in his hand.”

Sanders burst out laughing.

“Exactly, and I suppose the picture frame was empty,” he looked round in surprise. No one else had laughed.

===Chinese===
“谢谢您，梅布尔小姐，这事让我心里有些发慌，方才不该提起的。你们出门那会儿，我要到餐厅去取几件银器。一开门，就见詹姆斯爵士站在炉前地毯上，活生生地，跟真人一样。”

“他穿着什么衣服？”科林斯立刻问。

“他穿着宫廷礼服，身上佩着剑，手里还拿着帽子。”

桑德斯放声大笑。

“一点儿不错，我猜那会儿画框准是空的吧。”他环顾四周，一脸诧异。没有别一个人笑。

===Original===
“I was too frightened to look, sir,” he said.

“What happened then?” said Collins in a soothing tone.

“I don’t quite know, sir, I think I must have turned faint. I believe he turned and looked at me, and when I looked again he was gone.”

“But why were you so frightened?” said Collins.

“It was a warning, sir, I am sure of that. He came to tell me my time is up. Well, I suppose we all have to go sometime, and I have tried to do my duty.”

===Chinese===
“我吓坏了，没敢去看，先生。”他说。

“后来呢？”科林斯用抚慰的语气问。

“我也说不清，先生，我想我一定是晕了一阵。我恍惚觉得他转过身来瞧着我，等我再看时，他已经不见了。”

“可你为什么吓成那样？”科林斯问。

“那是个预兆啊，先生，我敢肯定。他是来告诉我，我的时辰到了。唉，人终归都有走的一天，我自问也已尽了本分。”

===Original===
Collins cast a quick glance round the room. Sanders was frankly sceptical. Mrs. Allery and her daughter were looking scared. The Aunt was not there. Mabel was dead white, and her eyes nearly closed; but Collins saw one quick look pass between her and Allery, though the face of the old lawyer was inscrutable.

There was a silence in the room.

Collins went to the old servant with a quick movement and took his hand.

“Now listen to me, John. I will tell you exactly what happened. You have been allowing yourself to brood over your old master’s death, and it has got on your nerves. You were probably thinking of him because the house was quiet, and when you came into the dining-room, the sun was shining right on to the portrait of Sir James, and you imagined he was standing on the rug. It is really quite a common thing. It’s what we call hallucination. You must not let it worry you. It’s not a warning or anything like that, and you must not think any more of it.”

===Chinese===
科林斯飞快地环视了一眼屋里。桑德斯一副公然不信的神气。艾勒里太太和她的女儿面露惧色。姑母不在场。梅布尔脸色惨白，双眼几乎闭上了；但科林斯瞥见她和艾勒里之间飞快地交换了一个眼色，尽管老律师的脸深不可测。

屋里一片沉默。

科林斯一个箭步走到老仆跟前，握住他的手。

“你听我说，约翰。我来告诉你到底是怎么回事。你一直放任自己惦念老主人的死，把神经都弄出毛病来了。宅子里静悄悄的，你多半正想着他；走进餐厅的时候，阳光恰好照在詹姆斯爵士的画像上，你就想象他站在地毯上了。这种事其实很常见，就是我们所说的幻觉。你千万别为这个烦恼。那不是什么预兆，也不是诸如此类的东西，别再去想它了。”

===Original===
He spoke with such conviction that the old man was greatly relieved.

But Collins had gone to the old man for another reason. John was standing with his back to a looking-glass, and Collins could see the room. He saw two things, a look of intense annoyance on the face of Sanders, and an expression on Mabel’s face in which gratitude was mixed with relief.

“Mr. Collins is right,” she said. “That is the explanation. I am sure there is nothing else in it. Now don’t worry any more about it.”

“Thank you, sir, thank you, Miss Mabel, it has relieved me a lot,” and the old man went out.

===Chinese===
他说得那样斩钉截铁，老人顿时宽心了不少。

不过科林斯走到老人跟前，还有另一层缘故。约翰背对着一面穿衣镜站着，科林斯正可以从镜中看清整间屋子。他看见了两样东西：桑德斯脸上恼怒至极的神色，和梅布尔脸上感激与释然交织的神情。

“科林斯先生说得对，”她说，“就是这么回事。我敢说这里头绝没有别的缘故。好了，别再为这事烦心了。”

“谢谢您，先生，谢谢您，梅布尔小姐，我心里松快多了。”老人说着退了出去。

===Original===
“Well done, Collins,” said Allery, “you have disposed of the ghost in quick time. I hope the old fellow will forget all about it.”

Collins laughed and helped himself to a cigarette from a silver box, but he watched the other. Allery drew a handkerchief from his pocket, and furtively wiped his forehead.

The company drifted off to various interests. Sanders and Mabel found themselves alone for a moment.

===Chinese===
“干得漂亮，科林斯，”艾勒里说，“一眨眼工夫就把鬼打发掉了。但愿这老头儿会把这事忘个干净。”

科林斯笑了，从一只银匣里取了支香烟，眼睛却始终留意着对方。艾勒里从口袋里掏出手帕，悄悄抹了抹额头。

众人各自散开，各寻各的乐子。桑德斯和梅布尔一时单独留在那里。

===Original===
“I think I will go to Town tomorrow, if you don’t mind,” he said.

“Why should you go?” she said.

“Well, I seem to be always putting my foot into it somehow, and I don’t think I am wanted here,” he added petulantly.

She stiffened. “You must please yourself. I hope you don’t find it very boring here?”

===Chinese===
“要是你不介意，我想明天上伦敦去。”他说。

“你为什么要走？”她说。

“唉，我这嘴总不知怎么就得罪人，我看这里也并不怎么需要我。”他赌着气补了一句。

她板起了脸。“悉听尊便。但愿你在这里没觉得太乏味？”

===Original===
“You know I don’t, Mabel, but you seem to have changed towards me. You are always trying to snub me in front of Collins, and you treat me almost as a stranger. Why can’t we announce our engagement, and then we should know where we stand?”

She flushed. “Really, Eric, you sometimes behave like a spoilt child. You know my reasons. It is too soon after my father’s death, especially as he disapproved, and I would like to wait till my brother comes back.”

“But he may be dead, and we may have to wait for years. He has been advertised enough for, and if a man will not come to take up a title and position, there must be something wrong.”

“What do you mean?” she said, angrily.

===Chinese===
“你知道我并不觉得，梅布尔，可你对我的态度似乎变了。你总当着科林斯的面给我难堪，待我几乎像个陌生人。我们何不把订婚的事公布出来？那样彼此心里都有个数。”

她脸红了。“真是的，埃里克，你有时候任性得像个惯坏的孩子。我的理由你是知道的。父亲尸骨未寒，尤其他生前还反对这门亲事，我想等哥哥回来再说。”

“可他也许已经死了，那我们就得等上好几年。寻他的启事也登得够多了，一个人若是不肯回来继承爵位和地位，其中必有蹊跷。”

“你这话什么意思？”她怒道。

===Original===
“Oh, I don’t mean anything dishonourable, but something to prevent him coming. He may be out of touch of civilisation,” he said, lamely.

“Very well,” she said, “then I will go further. I will not become engaged until my brother comes back. I am certain he is not dead.”

“That means, I suppose, you have ceased to care for me?”

“It means nothing of the sort and you know it. If I did not care for you, should I have stuck to you against my father’s wishes?”

===Chinese===
“哦，我不是说他有什么见不得人的事，只是说有什么事绊住了他，回不来。他也许与文明世界断了音讯。”他讷讷地说。

“那好，”她说，“我把话说得更明白些。哥哥一天不回来，我一天不订婚。我确信他没有死。”

“这么说，我想，是你已经不再爱我了？”

“绝不是这个意思，你心里清楚。我若是不爱你，当初会违逆父亲的心愿，一直守着你不放吗？”

===Original===
“Why don’t you say that you will marry the man who finds your brother?” he said, bitterly.

“Eric, you go too far,” and she threw up her head.

“I go too far! Well, Mabel, since you have said that, I will tell you I think it is you to whom that would apply. You are ‘carrying on’﻿—there is no other phrase for it, vulgar as it is﻿—with that fellow Collins. You are always with him, and I can see by the way he looks at you that he is getting too fond of you. You have only known him for a short time.”

“That’s enough,” she interrupted. “You had better say no more or we shall come to a real quarrel. I think you better go as you have suggested, and you can think things over. I am going to see how my aunt is,” and she went out without another word.

“Curse the fellow,” said Sanders. “What does he mean by foisting himself here, and staying on. He’s trying to cut me out with his damnable polite manners. And she’s everything in the world to me.”

===Chinese===
“你何不直说，谁找到你哥哥，你就嫁给谁？”他怨愤地说。

“埃里克，你太过分了。”她猛地昂起头。

“我过分！好啊，梅布尔，既然你这么说，我索性告诉你，我看这话该用在谁身上。你正在跟那个科林斯‘勾勾搭搭’——这话难听，可再没有别的词了。你总是同他在一起，看他瞧你的那副眼神，我就知道他对你越来越动了情。你认识他统共才多久。”

“够了。”她打断他。“你最好别再说了，不然我们真要大吵一场。我看你就照你自己说的走吧，也好把事情想清楚。我去看看姑母怎么样了。”她再没说一句话，径自走了出去。

“这该死的家伙。”桑德斯骂道。“他算什么意思，硬挤到这里来，还赖着不走。他想凭他那套可恶的斯文做派把我挤掉。而她可是我在这世上的一切。”

## XII. What Happened in the Night / 第十二章 夜里发生的事

===Original===
The incident of the day had cast a gloom over the party.

Mabel’s aunt had been in bed all the day, with one of her sick headaches. Collins was down before the others, and had a word with old John. The old servant was devoted to him.

“I hope you got over your scare,” said he.

“Oh, yes, thank you, sir, but it frightened me at the time. But you were probably right. I have been thinking about the master a lot lately.”

“I have often come across cases like that,” said Collins. “Now I suppose you came in quite quietly, without making any noise?”

“Yes, sir, as the family were out I was wearing carpet slippers, as I suffer with my feet a good deal, and the door was only ajar.”

===Chinese===
白天的那桩事，给满座宾客蒙上了一层阴云。

梅布尔的姑母犯了偏头痛的老毛病，在床上躺了一整天。科林斯比旁人先下了楼，同老约翰说了几句话。这位老仆人对他敬爱有加。

“但愿你那场惊吓已经缓过来了。”他说。

“哦，缓过来了，谢谢您，先生，可当时实在吓得不轻。不过您多半说对了。我近来常常想起老爷。”

“这类事我见得多了。”科林斯说。“这么说，你进来的时候悄没声儿的，一点响动也没有？”

“是的，先生。一家人都出去了，我这双脚常疼得厉害，就趿着毡底拖鞋，那扇门也只是虚掩着。”

===Original===
They were standing in the dining-room.

Collins walked to the fireplace.

“And you thought you saw Sir James standing here,” he said, but he took in a breath. With an effort he controlled his voice.

“Don’t let me stop your work,” he said.

“Thank you, sir,” said the other, hurrying to complete his preparations.

Collins stooped quickly.

===Chinese===
两人正站在餐厅里。

科林斯走到壁炉前。

“你就是在这儿，以为看见了詹姆斯爵士站着。”他说，却倒吸了一口气。他费了好大的力气，才把声音稳住。

“别让我耽搁你干活。”他说。

“谢谢您，先生。”老约翰说着，赶紧去忙他手头的活计。

科林斯急忙俯下身去。

===Original===
The impression of two feet was plainly visible on the thick rug. No one had been in the room since the morning, as they had all been out for lunch. Hastily Collins walked up and down the rug two or three times, sliding his feet over the soft pile.

Then he glanced up at the portrait.

“I wonder,” he said.

Dinner was a dreary performance. Even Collins was preoccupied. The first news bulletin had told them that Jackson had been declared by the doctors unfit to plead. It remained to be seen whether there was sufficient evidence to convict him as the murderer.

===Chinese===
厚厚的地毯上，两只脚印的印痕清清楚楚。打早上起就没有人进过这间屋子，因为大家都出门吃午饭去了。科林斯赶紧在地毯上来回走了两三趟，双脚在柔软的绒面上拖蹭着。

随后他抬眼望了望那幅画像。

“我不免纳闷。”他说。

那顿晚饭吃得没精打采。连科林斯也心事重重。第一份新闻公报已经告诉他们，医生们判定杰克逊无受审能力。至于证据是否足以将他定罪为凶手，尚待分晓。

===Original===
“How is your aunt?” said Allery, to make conversation.

“Oh, she is not very bad. She takes to her bed at intervals.”

“Nothing serious, I hope?” said Mrs. Allery.

“Only nerves.”

“What a blessing she did not hear about the ghost,” said Sanders.

===Chinese===
“你姑母怎么样了？”艾勒里没话找话地说。

“哦，不算太糟。她隔三差五就要卧床一回。”

“没什么要紧的吧？”艾勒里太太说。

“不过是神经上的毛病罢了。”

“亏得她没听说闹鬼的事。”桑德斯说。

===Original===
“I think the less said about that the better,” said Allery.

“I quite agree,” said Collins with meaning. “Once these things get about they get exaggerated, and you will have the psychical research people offering to investigate, which would never do,” and as he said this he glanced at Allery.

“No,” said he without a flicker of the eyelid, “we don’t want this to get about at this time.”

“Cunning old ruffian,” said Collins to himself.

There was an awkward silence. John had come into the room.

===Chinese===
“我看这事越少提越好。”艾勒里说。

“我完全赞成。”科林斯意味深长地说。“这种事一旦传扬出去，就越传越离谱，到时自有心灵研究学会那班人自告奋勇来调查——那可万万使不得。”说这话时，他朝艾勒里瞥了一眼。

“是啊，”他眼皮也不眨一下地说，“眼下我们可不想让这事传出去。”

“好一个老奸巨猾的家伙。”科林斯暗自想道。

一阵尴尬的静默。约翰进了屋。

===Original===
After dinner Collins seized an opportunity when his hostess was alone for a moment. “May I have a word with you?” he said.

Mabel felt almost inclined to make an excuse, but braced herself.

“Certainly,” she said. “Is it anything private?”

“Not at all,” he said, with a smile. “Only that I shall be leaving you tomorrow. I must return to Town, and I wanted to thank you for a very delightful visit.”

“How provoking,” she said. “Mr. Sanders is also going. It is a breakup of the party, and I was enjoying it so much.”

===Chinese===
饭后，科林斯瞅准女主人独自待了片刻的时机。“我能跟您说句话吗？”他说。

梅布尔几乎想找个借口推脱，但还是打起了精神。

“当然可以，”她说，“是私事吗？”

“完全不是，”他含笑说，“只是我明天就要告辞了。我得回伦敦去，这一趟做客十分尽兴，特来向您道谢。”

“真扫兴，”她说，“桑德斯先生也要走。这一桌人就要散了，我正玩得高兴呢。”

===Original===
In spite of the words Collins noted an insincerity which was foreign to her nature.

“I must get back to my work,” he said.

“Not about﻿—my father? You have finished with that, haven’t you?”

“The matter has been taken out of my hands,” he answered.

“When all this has passed over, you must come and see us again. You have been very good and helpful.”

===Chinese===
话虽如此，科林斯却听出一种言不由衷，这可不是她的天性。

“我得回去做我的事了。”他说。

“不是为了——我父亲的事吧？那案子您已经不再经手了，对吧？”

“那案子已经不由我过问了。”他答道。

“等这一切都过去，您一定要再来看我们。您一直热心相助，帮了大忙。”

===Original===
“Miss Watson, you will forgive me saying a thing I have perhaps no right to say, but I rather fancy Mr. Sanders does not care for my presence here.”

“Surely you are mistaken. Why should he not like you? I thought you got on very well together.”

“It won’t do, Miss Watson, you know differently. And I expect you can guess the reason. So I had better go.”

This was said with such a charm of manner that it disarmed her from the haughty tone she would have assumed.

“Well, I am very sorry. But perhaps you two will get to know each other better.”

===Chinese===
“沃森小姐，恕我说一句我或许无权说的话——我总觉得桑德斯先生不大乐意我待在这儿。”

“您一定是弄错了。他怎么会不喜欢您呢？我还以为你们处得挺融洽。”

“装是装不过去的，沃森小姐，您心里明白不是这么回事。我想您也猜得到缘故。所以我还是走为好。”

他说得这般风度动人，她原会摆出的那副冷傲口吻，也就无从发作了。

“唉，我实在遗憾。不过，你们两位往后也许会更了解彼此。”

===Original===
“Perhaps we shall,” he muttered under his breath.

Allery entered. “Oh, Mr. Allery,” said Mabel, “here’s Mr. Collins going off tomorrow. This will mean the breakup of our party.”

“Well, I am afraid we shall have to go too, very soon. My business will not wait, you know.”

“Oh, you must not go,” she said, with a look of terror coming into her eyes.

Allery laughed. “I dare say we can manage another day or two,” he said.

===Chinese===
“也许会吧。”他压着嗓子嘀咕了一句。

艾勒里走了进来。“哦，艾勒里先生，”梅布尔说，“科林斯先生明天就要动身了。咱们这一伙人就要散了。”

“这个嘛，恐怕我们也很快就得走了。您知道，我的公事拖不得。”

“哦，你们可千万别走。”她说，眼里露出惊恐的神色。

艾勒里笑了。“再待上一两天，我想总还使得。”他说。

===Original===
When Sanders heard that Collins was going the next day, he was both relieved and angry.

“Just my luck,” he thought, “if I had kept quiet, I need not have gone myself.”

Collins paced his room restlessly. Things were taking shape in his mind. Something was going on which his keen intellect could not explain, but which gave rise to wild conjecture.

He was fully dressed, but had a pair of slippers on. He would know the truth that night somehow.

===Chinese===
桑德斯听说科林斯次日要走，又是如释重负，又是暗自恼火。

“真是倒霉，”他心想，“早知道沉住气不吭声，我自己就用不着走了。”

科林斯在房间里来回踱步，烦躁不安。种种头绪正在他脑子里渐渐成形。有什么事情正在发生，他敏锐的头脑还解释不了，却由此生出种种荒诞的猜想。

他衣裳穿得齐齐整整，脚上却趿着一双拖鞋。不管用什么法子，当夜他都要把真相探个明白。

===Original===
The wind had got up, and was howling round the old house, making the timbers creak and the windows shake, till it died down to a moaning sound.

Several times he went carefully on to the landing and listened.

It was an ideal night for ghosts to walk.

===Chinese===
起风了，风在老宅四周呼啸，刮得梁木咯咯作响、窗户簌簌发颤，到最后低伏成一腔呜咽。

他几次蹑手蹑脚走到楼梯口去听。

这正是鬼魂出行的理想夜晚。

===Original===
He would piece the puzzle together. There was Jackson, the lunatic. He knew he was not the murderer, though the police would certainly make out a case against him. Very well. Then there was the strange disappearance of Lewis, on which Sinclair was basing a case until his official position compelled silence.

Then there was his own piece of evidence which was closing in. There was something else.

When he and Sinclair had discussed the matter in his flat, the latter had taken out the statement of Mrs. Simmons from his pocket book. He had done more. There had slipped on the floor a letter. Collins’ keen eyes had seen the signature “James Watson” and the date. Under pretence of reading the statement he had picked up the letter and rapidly read it. So Sinclair had kept this from him, for some reason. What was he afraid of? Did he know more about the murder than he cared to own? There was nothing but his word that he had been in the office on the fateful afternoon. What a lark if the sober Sinclair﻿—but he broke off suddenly. His quick ear had caught something that sounded in the house in spite of the wind, a stealthy step. He moved noiselessly to the landing.

===Chinese===
他要把这团乱麻拼拢来。一头是疯子杰克逊。他明知那人不是凶手，尽管警方必定会给他罗织出一套罪案来。很好。另一头是刘易斯的离奇失踪，辛克莱本想循着这条线索立案追查，只是官职在身，不得不三缄其口。

再有，就是他自己掌握的那件证据，正一步步收紧。此外还有别的蹊跷。

在他的公寓里两人商讨此案时，辛克莱曾从记事本里取出西蒙斯太太的口供。他做的还不止于此。当时有一封信滑落在地板上。科林斯敏锐的眼睛瞥见了署名“詹姆斯·沃森”和日期。他借着读口供的姿势，把信拾起来匆匆读了一遍。原来辛克莱出于某种缘故，把这封信瞒着他。他怕什么呢？关于这桩凶案，他知道的是否比他肯承认的更多？出事那天下午他在办公室，除了他本人的一面之词，别无佐证。倘若一本正经的辛克莱竟——可他猛然收住了思绪。灵敏的耳朵捕捉到宅子里有一个声音穿透了风声：一阵鬼鬼祟祟的脚步。他悄没声地移向楼梯口。

===Original===
There was a stirring in the house, as the wind increased in volume, but the other sound was quite distinct.

Very quietly Collins closed the door, and went to the window. Outside, the old ivy came round, but Collins preferred the safety of a rope. Even this would have been no easy work for a man who was not in condition. He hung for one moment turning round in the air as the wind caught him.

Once on the ground he made his way cautiously round the house till he arrived at the dining-room window. Here he paused. A wild gust of wind, with a wisp of rain in it, caught him, as he stood listening. Not a sound was heard from within, and no light was showing.

===Chinese===
风声愈紧，宅子里处处簌簌作响，可那另一个声响却清晰可辨。

科林斯轻轻带上房门，走向窗口。窗外有老常春藤可以攀缘，但科林斯更信得过绳索的稳妥。即便如此，对身手不佳的人来说也绝非易事。风兜住他的当儿，他在半空悬了一瞬，身子打着转。

一落地，他便小心地绕着宅子摸过去，一直走到餐厅窗外。他停住脚。他伫立倾听时，一阵狂风夹着雨丝扑到他身上。屋里悄无声息，也不见灯火。

===Original===
Was it a fool’s errand after all? The whole house was dead still. Collins felt his way round the corner. By the old, oak door he paused. All was dark, but a sort of ghostly radiance was shining on an ancient elm.

He stepped back from the house, and presently saw, high up in the gabled roof, a beam of light was shining from a slit in a shutter or a badly-fitting blind. Probably some servant who could not sleep, or was frightened at the weather.

Cold and wet he returned to beneath his window, and with the practised skill of an athlete hauled himself up.

He stood in thought. Unless he had made a mistake things were happening in this house which were, to say the least, interesting. He opened the door, and slid down the bannisters without noise. Once in the hall he waited, holding his breath. The dining-room door was open, and, faint as it was, he caught the sound of a living thing breathing.

===Chinese===
难道这一趟纯属白跑？整座宅子死一般寂静。科林斯摸索着拐过墙角。在那扇老橡木门旁，他停下脚步。四下漆黑，只有一种幽幽的微光，照在一株老榆树上。

他退离宅子几步，旋即看见高处人字屋顶上，一线灯光正从百叶窗的缝隙或是不甚合缝的遮光帘边透出来。多半是哪个睡不着的仆人，或是被这天气吓着了的。

他又冷又湿地回到自己窗下，凭着运动员般的老练身手，攀着绳子引体而上。

他站着思忖。除非是他弄错了，这座宅子里正发生着一些事情，说得轻些，也是耐人寻味。他打开房门，悄无声息地顺着楼梯扶栏滑了下去。一到门厅，他便屏息等待。餐厅的门开着，尽管细微，他还是捕捉到一个活物呼吸的声音。

===Original===
Like a cat he stole across the intervening space, and carefully put his hand round the edge of the doorway. Inch by inch the fingers crept till they touched the switch. A flood of light illuminated the room, and showed a man standing on the hearthrug, rigid. It was Eric Sanders. In his hand was a revolver. For a moment the two men gazed at each other without a word. A look of hate was on the face of Sanders.

“So,” he said, “it was you. I thought I could not be mistaken. You foul brute, you’re not fit to live,” and he raised his pistol.

“You’re very free with your shooter,” said Collins coolly. “May I ask for an explanation?”

“It is no good my saying anything. Of course you will deny everything, and so will she, but I heard.”

“You will excuse me, but I haven’t the faintest idea what on earth you are talking about.” His face was stern. “We don’t want to rouse the whole house at this hour. Hadn’t you better tell me what the trouble is? In the first place, what are you doing here at all?”

“You know perfectly well. It’s no good lying. I heard everything and came down here to see you. You are not going out of this room alive.”

===Chinese===
他猫一般悄然穿过这段空当，小心地把一只手探进门框内侧。手指一寸一寸往里挪，终于摸到了开关。灯光一下子照亮了整个房间，照出一个人直挺挺地站在炉前地毯上。是埃里克·桑德斯。他手里握着一把左轮手枪。两人无言对视了片刻。桑德斯脸上带着一股恨意。

“好啊，”他说，“原来是你。我早想到不会弄错。你这下流的畜生，简直不配活着。”说着，他举起了手枪。

“你这枪倒是使得挺随便。”科林斯冷冷地说。“能否请你解释一下？”

“我说什么都没用。你们当然会一口否认，她也会，可我亲耳听见了。”

“恕我直言，我压根儿不明白你究竟在说什么。”他面色严厉。“这个钟点，咱们总不想惊动全宅的人吧。你不如把为难之处讲出来？首先，你到底在这儿做什么？”

“你心里一清二楚。撒谎没用。我什么都听见了，才下楼来找你。你休想活着走出这间屋子。”

===Original===
Collins slowly drew out his case, and lit a cigarette. He knew a hasty action might force the issue.

“What did you hear?” he asked, casually.

“Oh, it’s no good. I could not sleep, you know why. Then I thought I would try a whiskey, which I never touch as a rule, so I came down. As I passed Mabel’s bedroom, I heard talking and﻿—I know I ought not to have done, but I listened.”

“If it interests you to know,” said Collins, “I do not even know where Miss Watson’s bedroom is, so if I were you, I should hesitate to make any insinuations.”

The other was shaken by his firm tones.

“But I tell you I heard a man’s voice in there, and Mabel called him dear. And then she said ‘Go to the dining-room, I will join you there.’ ”

===Chinese===
科林斯慢慢掏出烟盒，点上一支烟。他知道一个仓促的举动就可能逼得对方摊牌。

“你听见什么了？”他随口问道。

“哼，说了也没用。我睡不着，原因你是知道的。后来想喝杯威士忌试试——我平日是滴酒不沾的——就下了楼。路过梅布尔的卧室时，我听见里面有说话声，于是——我知道不该那样，可我听了。”

“如果你有兴趣知道，”科林斯说，“我连沃森小姐的卧室在哪儿都不知道。所以换了我是你，开口之前最好先掂量掂量，少来含沙射影。”

对方被他斩钉截铁的语气动摇了。

“可我告诉你，我听见里面有个男人的声音，梅布尔还唤他亲爱的。接着她说：‘到餐厅去，我在那儿跟你碰头。’”

===Original===
“And you pretend to love this girl, and dare to make such foul accusations. If Miss Watson was talking to anyone, it is her own business, and I am sure she has her own reasons. You ought to be ashamed of yourself. As for my being here, if you want to know, I could not sleep, and I heard someone moving about the house. I am an investigator as you know, and apart from the question of burglars, I am convinced there is something happening in this house which requires investigating. So I came down and found you here.”

Sanders looked at him doubtfully.

“But I tell you, Mabel was talking with a man in her bedroom.”

“You make me sick with your insinuations. How do you know it was a man? A woman can imitate a man’s voice as a man can a woman’s.”

===Chinese===
“你还自称爱这位姑娘，竟敢编出这样下流的指控。沃森小姐若是同谁说话，那是她自己的事，我确信她自有她的道理。你应当感到羞愧。至于我为什么在这儿——你想知道的话——我睡不着，听见有人在宅子里走动。你知道我是干调查这一行的，且不说防贼这一层，我确信这座宅子里有事发生，很值得查一查。于是我下了楼，就撞见了你。”

桑德斯将信将疑地望着他。

“可我告诉你，梅布尔确实在卧室里同一个男人说话。”

“你这些含沙射影的话真叫我恶心。你怎么知道是个男人？女人能学男人的嗓音，正如男人也能学女人的。”

===Original===
Sanders was in perplexity, and slowly put the revolver on the table. Without any sudden movement Collins picked it up. “Is this yours?” he asked casually.

“No,” said Sanders. “It belonged to Sir James. I found it here among his papers.”

“Sir James was very fond of pistols,” said the other, “he had one in London, too.”

“Yes,” said Sanders, “he was always afraid of being attacked.”

“I wonder you did not have one, too,” said Collins.

===Chinese===
桑德斯一时惶惑，慢慢把左轮手枪放到桌上。科林斯不慌不忙、不带一点突然的动作，把它拿到手里。“这是你的？”他随口问。

“不是，”桑德斯说，“是詹姆斯爵士的。我在他的文件堆里发现的。”

“詹姆斯爵士素来喜欢手枪，”对方说，“在伦敦他也备着一支。”

“是的，”桑德斯说，“他总担心遭人袭击。”

“奇怪，你倒没有备一支。”科林斯说。

===Original===
“I did,” said Sanders and stopped.

Collins was quite at his ease. Sander’s fit of wild jealousy was passing away. “Lost it?” he said.

“Yes, I got rid of it,” said Sanders in some confusion.

“But we must not stay here; if you tell me on your word of honour it was not you I heard, I will apologize for my words.”

“Certainly I will, but it is to Miss Watson that an apology is due, not to me.”

“Of course I cannot mention it to her, she would never forgive me. And I hope you will not do so.”

===Chinese===
“我有过。”桑德斯说到这儿，住了口。

科林斯神色自若。桑德斯那阵狂乱的妒火正渐渐平息。“弄丢了？”他说。

“嗯，我把它处理掉了。”桑德斯有些慌乱地说。

“不过咱们不能待在这儿了。你要是以名誉向我担保，我听见的不是你，我就为方才的话道歉。”

“我当然担保。只是该道歉的对象是沃森小姐，不是我。”

“这话我当然不能对她提，她永远不会饶恕我的。我也希望你别提。”

===Original===
Collins looked straight at him.

“I should advise you to keep these fits of excitement within bounds﻿—and,” he added slowly, “when they do come on, to leave your revolver behind you.”

“What do you mean?” said Sanders, turning white.

“When you called on Sir James Watson and asked to see him, you were in one of those fits. It is dangerous.”

“What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
科林斯直直地望着他。

“我劝你把这阵阵狂躁收敛着点儿——还有，”他慢悠悠地补了一句，“真要发作的时候，把手枪留在家里。”

“你这是什么意思？”桑德斯说着，脸色发白。

“你去拜访詹姆斯·沃森爵士、求见他的那回，正是犯了这样一阵毛病。那可是危险的。”

“你这话什么意思？”

===Original===
“When you left your card under the door, with a note to say you must see him at once, I don’t suppose you forget the day,” and he looked at him with meaning.

“Are you suggesting that I﻿—?”

“I am suggesting nothing,” said Collins, sternly. “I am citing facts.”

“If you think I had any hand in the murder, you had better arrest me,” said the other wildly.

“I am not a policeman, and do not go about arresting people. The police know their business. I am merely giving you a friendly warning against temper. And now I think I will go to bed. I am sure someone has been listening to our conversation. And if you don’t mind, I think I will take this.” And he picked up the revolver. Sanders watched him go without a word.

===Chinese===
“你把名片从门底下塞进去，还附了张条子说必须立刻见他——那一天，我想你总不至于忘记吧。”说着，他意味深长地看着他。

“你是在暗示我——？”

“我什么也不暗示，”科林斯厉声说，“我是在摆事实。”

“要是你认为我同这桩凶案有什么干系，你最好逮捕我。”对方狂乱地嚷道。

“我不是警察，不干四处抓人的营生。警察自会办他们的事。我不过是友情提醒你，收敛收敛脾气。现在我要去睡了。我敢肯定，方才咱们的话有人一直在偷听。你要是不介意，这个我就带走了。”说着，他拾起那把左轮手枪。桑德斯一言不发，望着他离去。

## XIII. The Car in the Dark / 第十三章 黑暗中的汽车

===Original===
In spite of the adventures of the night, Collins was the first down in the morning. Old John was in the dining-room.

“Oh, sir,” he said, “Mr. Eric has gone off early this morning. He has left a note for Miss Mabel, and he left something for me as he always does, with a note to say he had to go back to London, and would have breakfast on the train.”

“Oh, yes,” said Collins. “Miss Watson told me he was going today, but I did not think he was going so early.”

“May I say a word to you, sir? You have been very good to me, and did not laugh at me the other day. You know, sir, about﻿—what I saw.”

“What you thought you saw,” said Collins, with a smile.

“Yes, sir, well I was awake last night and I can swear I heard people talking. I should have come down, but after what happened, I was afraid to.”

===Chinese===
尽管夜里经历了那番风波，早晨科林斯还是第一个下了楼。老约翰正在餐厅里。

“哦，先生，”他说，“埃里克先生今儿一早就走了。他给梅布尔小姐留了封信，也照老规矩给我留了点东西，还附了张条子，说他必须回伦敦去，早饭就在火车上吃。”

“哦，是吗，”科林斯说，“沃森小姐跟我说过他今天走，可没想到他动身这么早。”

“先生，我能跟您说句话吗？您一向待我很好，那天也没有笑话我。您知道的，先生，就是——我看见的那件东西。”

“是你自以为看见的东西。”科林斯微笑着说。

“是，先生。不过呢，昨天夜里我醒着，我可以起誓，我确实听见有人说话。我本该下楼去看看的，可自打出了那桩事，我不敢。”

===Original===
“That’s quite all right, I was about myself, and so was Mr. Sanders. I expect it was the storm which kept us all awake.”

“But what were you doing in the empty room in the attics, sir, if I may be so bold as to ask?”

“In the attics?” said Collins; then, quickly, “Oh, yes, of course, we heard a window banging, and went to see.”

John looked sceptical. “But forgive me, sir, it sounded as though there was a woman with you, or a man disguising his voice.”

===Chinese===
“没关系的。当时我也没睡着，桑德斯先生也没睡。我看是那场暴风雨，闹得大家都没合眼。”

“不过，恕我斗胆问一句，先生，您在阁楼那间空屋子里做什么呢？”

“阁楼？”科林斯说；随即赶忙接道：“哦，对了，当然，我们听见一扇窗子哐当哐当地响，就上去瞧了瞧。”

约翰满脸狐疑。“不过恕我冒昧，先生，听那动静，仿佛还有位太太跟您在一处，再不然就是个捏着嗓音的男人。”

===Original===
Collins dared not ask further.

“You must have been mistaken, surely,” he said.

“Perhaps so, sir,” he said, doubtfully.

The room with the light in it, thought Collins. This is getting interesting.

===Chinese===
科林斯不敢再多问了。

“你准是听岔了，真的。”他说。

“也许吧，先生。”他半信半疑地说。

那间亮着灯的房间，科林斯心想。事情越来越有意思了。

===Original===
Allery came in. “Hullo, Collins,” he said. “Down already. What a night of storm, but it has cleared up now.”

“Yes, it has cleared up now,” said Collins, with such meaning in his voice that the other man looked at him with surprise.

“Sanders has gone,” said Collins.

“Really, I knew he was going today, but he is very early. You are off, too?”

“Yes, I must get back. It is very jolly down here, but not business. There are a lot of things I must see to in London.”

===Chinese===
艾勒里走了进来。“喂，科林斯，”他说，“已经下楼啦。好一场暴风雨之夜，好在如今放晴了。”

“是啊，如今放晴了。”科林斯说。这话音里意味深长，引得对方惊异地望了他一眼。

“桑德斯走了。”科林斯说。

“当真？我知道他今天要走，可也实在太早了。你也要动身？”

“是啊，我必须回去。这乡下是挺快活，可惜不是来办公的。伦敦有一大摊子事，等着我去料理。”

===Original===
The others drifted in slowly, but a message came from Mabel that she was not feeling well, and would not be down till later.

There was a general feeling of uneasiness in the air.

Collins had ordered the car for ten o’clock, and thought he would not see his hostess, but she came in as he was getting ready to go.

“I am most awfully sorry I could not be down this morning. Pray forgive me.”

===Chinese===
其余的人慢悠悠地陆续到了，梅布尔却捎下话来，说她身子不舒服，要过一阵才下来。

人人心中都笼着一层不安。

科林斯订的是十点的车，本以为见不着女主人了，不料他正收拾行装要走时，她走了进来。

“今天早上没能下楼，实在抱歉得很。请您千万原谅。”

===Original===
“I hope you are feeling better,” he said. She was obviously ill, and lines of black showed round her eyes.

“I am afraid this visit has been very dull for you,” she said.

“On the contrary, I have enjoyed it immensely,” he hesitated. “Miss Watson,” he said, “I would not like to worry you, but if it should happen that certain suspicions gather round someone who is perhaps dear to you﻿—”

===Chinese===
“但愿你觉着好些了。”他说。她显然病着，眼圈四周隐隐透出黑晕。

“只怕这一趟做客，实在让你闷得慌了吧。”她说。

“恰恰相反，我过得快活极了。”他迟疑了一下。“沃森小姐，”他说，“我本不想让你烦心，可万一有些疑点，偏偏聚到某个或许与你至亲至近的人身上——”

===Original===
He got no further. Her face turned ashen, and she reeled as though about to faint. Collins caught her, and held her, but she repulsed him.

“What do you mean?” she said in a hoarse whisper.

“I want to warn you. If you hear anything﻿—any rumour about Sanders﻿—”

“Eric?” she said, in unmistakable astonishment. “Whatever should there be against him?”

There was more relief than indignation in her tone, which Collins was quick to notice. He took a quick resolve.

“I will tell you. On the day that your father was murdered, after he had posted a letter, Mr. Sanders called to see him.”

===Chinese===
他没能再说下去。她的脸刷地一片死灰，身子摇晃，眼看就要晕倒。科林斯一把扶住她，将她搀着，她却推开了他。

“你这是什么意思？”她嗓音沙哑，低声问道。

“我想提醒你。你要是听到什么——任何有关桑德斯的传闻——”

“埃里克？”她说，那惊愕再明白不过。“他还能有什么把柄落人指摘？”

她话音里宽慰多过愤慨，科林斯立刻听出来了。他当机立断，拿定了主意。

“那我就告诉你。你父亲遇害那天，他寄出一封信之后，桑德斯先生上门来求见。”

===Original===
Now her eyes were wide open with horror.

“He handed a note under the door, begging your father to see him.”

“But how do you know this?” she said breathlessly.

He pulled out his pocket book without a word, and handed her the card. She looked at it, turning it over in her hand, and gave it back to him.

===Chinese===
这一下，她的双眼惊恐地睁得老大。

“他从门底下塞进一张条子，恳求你父亲见他。”

“可这些你是怎么知道的？”她气都喘不匀了。

他一言不发地掏出记事本，把那张名片递给她。她看了看，在手里翻来覆去，又递还给他。

===Original===
“I found this in the room at the first examination, and Mrs. Simmons afterwards told me the truth.”

“Did he see my father?” she asked.

“No; he would not see him.”

“And﻿—have you told this to the police?”

“No,” replied he; “I work independently. I have not told a soul.”

===Chinese===
“初次勘验房间时我发现了它，后来西蒙斯太太把实情告诉了我。”

“他见到我父亲了吗？”她问。

“没有；你父亲不肯见他。”

“还有——这些你告诉警察了吗？”

“没有，”他答道，“我独力行事，对谁也没吐露过一个字。”

===Original===
Tears came into her eyes.

“How good of you, and he has treated you so badly all the time, and you never said a word. Of course you don’t really suspect him, but you might have given him an awful time.”

“I am afraid you give me credit for too much generosity. I never move till I am certain. In any case I would not have let his behaviour affect my attitude.”

“Well, I think you have acted like a sportsman, and I am very grateful,” and she gave him her hand.

“You haven’t got to go at once, have you? Do stay a little longer.”

===Chinese===
她眼里涌起了泪水。

“你心眼儿真好。他一路上那样刻薄地待你，你却一个字也没提。当然，你并非真的疑心他，可你原本满可以叫他吃一番大苦头。”

“恐怕你把我抬举得太宽厚了。没有十分把握，我绝不轻举妄动。再说无论如何，我也不会让他待人的行径，影响我办事的态度。”

“不管怎么说，我认为你行事大有君子风度，我非常感激。”说着，她把手伸给了他。

“你不是非马上就走不可的，对吧？再多待一阵子吧。”

===Original===
“This is very good of you,” he said, “but I must get off by this train.”

“I am sorry,” she said, and went out.

Collins stood in thought.

“Why was she so scared, and then so relieved when I mentioned Eric’s name?” This was getting interesting.

He went outside and ordered the car, telling the man he would want it immediately.

===Chinese===
“你太客气了，”他说，“可我必须赶这班火车。”

“真遗憾。”她说着走了出去。

科林斯伫立原地，陷入沉思。

“为什么她先头那样惊恐，我一提埃里克的名字，她又那样如释重负？”事情越来越有意思了。

他走到外面吩咐备车，告诉司机他马上就要用。

===Original===
When he returned Allery met him in the hall. He came forward at once.

“Collins, Mabel has told me what you have done about Sanders. Let me tell you it is a generous action. Although I know the boy is as straight as a die, you could have made havoc of his life, and something would have clung to him, even when he had cleared himself.”

“Don’t mention it. I knew he was not the murderer.”

===Chinese===
他回到屋里时，艾勒里在门厅里迎上他，立刻走上前来。

“科林斯，梅布尔把桑德斯那件事告诉我了。我得说一句，这是桩宽厚仗义的举动。尽管我知道那孩子正直得无可挑剔，可你原本能把他的一生搅得一塌糊涂——就算日后洗清了自己，有些东西也会一直粘在他身上，甩都甩不掉。”

“不值一提。我早知道他不是凶手。”

===Original===
Allery looked at him.

“You have done a very clever thing,” he said, “by accident. Oh, I have been keeping my eyes open. If you had used this against Sanders you would have made all Mabel’s sympathy turn to him, and against you. It was like that with her father. She would have turned on you with loathing. As it is she is struck with your generous conduct towards Sanders, and angry with him for his treatment of you.”

“Nonsense, my dear fellow,” said Collins. “You are entirely wrong about﻿—what shall we say﻿—the situation.”

Allery gave a keen glance at him. “Humph,” he said, “I wonder.”

“The car is waiting, sir,” said John.

Collins took his leave, and was driven to Wilton-on-Sea. At the station he dismissed the chauffeur with a liberal tip, and watched him drive off. He then went to the parcels office and despatched his bag to his flat in London. Having done this he set out for a long walk, with nothing but a stout stick, and a rucksack with a few necessaries for the night. He had a long tour in front of him.

===Chinese===
艾勒里端详着他。

“你可办成了一件绝顶聪明的事，”他说，“尽管是出于无心。哦，我这双眼睛可一直睁着呢。你若是拿这件事去对付桑德斯，只会把梅布尔的满腔怜惜都推向他那一边，掉过头来对付你。她对父亲便是如此。她会满怀憎恶地跟你反目。如今这样呢，你待桑德斯宽宏大量，她深受触动，反倒怨他不该那样待你。”

“胡扯，我亲爱的伙计，”科林斯说，“你把——该怎么说呢——把局面全看岔了。”

艾勒里目光锐利地瞥了他一眼。“哼，”他说，“难说。”

“车已经备好了，先生。”约翰说。

科林斯告辞登车，被送到滨海威尔顿。到了车站，他赏了司机一笔丰厚的小费，打发他把车开回去，目送汽车驶远。随后他到行李房，把提包托运回伦敦的公寓。办完这些，他便动身长途步行，随身只有一根结实的手杖，和一只装着几样过夜必需之物的背包。前头等他的，是一段漫长的跋涉。

---

===Original===
A steady rain was falling through the thick night, but the wind had dropped. The Vale was wrapped in shadow, not a light was showing. In the shrubbery Collins watched, getting what shelter he could. Unless all his calculations were at fault, here was the crisis of the situation. He was in front of the main door, and here it was that something would take place.

The time passed slowly, and he was thankful for the flask he had brought. Away in the distance a clock was striking. It was only nine o’clock.

Presently a familiar sound broke on his ear, the purring of a car. Along the drive came a gaunt, formless mass showing no light. He parted the bushes and looked keenly into the body of the car. It was empty. There was no one but the driver, who was heavily wrapped up. The car drew up at the door, and the driver got down. It was too dark to see further, and Collins came stealthily from the bushes, and turned down the drive. He was stiff from his waiting, but broke into a run, and only paused when he came to the entrance into the main road. The gate was open, but he carefully shut it.

===Chinese===
浓黑的夜色中下着绵绵的雨，风却已经息了。谷园笼罩在一片黑暗里，不见一丝灯火。科林斯守在灌木丛中张望，能寻多少遮蔽就寻多少。除非他全盘推算都错了，此刻正是整个局面的紧要关头。他埋伏在正门前——要有事，就该出在这里。

时间过得很慢，他暗自庆幸带来了那只小酒壶。远处一处钟声正在敲响。才不过九点。

不多时，一阵熟悉的声音传进他耳中——汽车低沉的嗡鸣。车道上驶来一个黑沉沉、不成形状的庞大影子，没有一点灯光。他拨开灌木，凝神细看车身里面。车是空的，除了司机别无一人，那司机裹得严严实实。汽车在门前停住，司机下了车。天黑得再往后就什么也看不清了，科林斯便悄悄钻出灌木丛，转身顺着车道往下走。久候之下浑身都僵了，他还是撒开腿跑了起来，直到大路的入口才停步。院门开着，他却细心地把它关上了。

===Original===
It would be necessary to open it and to light up. He concealed himself.

It was not long before he heard the car coming down the drive.

Silently he drew from his pocket an automatic pistol and stood ready.

At the gate the car came to a stop. He heard a woman’s voice say, “Bother, the gate must have shut. Just open it, will you?”

===Chinese===
待会儿少不了要开门、开灯。他把自己隐蔽好。

没过多久，他就听见汽车顺着车道驶了下来。

他悄没声儿地从口袋里掏出一支自动手枪，站定候着。

汽车在院门前停住。他听见一个女人的声音说：“讨厌，这门准是自己关上了。劳驾去开一下，好吗？”

===Original===
A man’s voice replied, “Hush, not so loud,” and a figure got out of the car, and went forward.

The gate swung back, and at the same moment the driver turned on the headlights. They were dimmed and did not give much light, but a flicker struck the man, though he tried to dodge out of the way.

In that brief moment Collins recognised him. The next moment the car had swung out into the road.

===Chinese===
一个男人的声音答道：“嘘，别这么大声。”一条人影下了车，朝前走去。

大门向后荡开，同一瞬间，司机拧亮了车头灯。灯光压得暗暗的，照不出多远，却有一星微光扫中了那个男人，任他竭力闪躲也没能躲开。

就在那短短一瞬里，科林斯认出了他。下一刻，汽车已经一拐，驶上了大路。

===Original===
“Allery, by all that’s holy,” said Collins, “and the other is Mabel, of course.” Then he laughed.

“So that’s it, is it?”

He set out on his long tramp to the nearest town.

===Chinese===
“老天在上，是艾勒里！”科林斯说，“另一个嘛，自然是梅布尔。”说完他笑了。

“哦，原来是这么回事，是吗？”

他动身踏上漫长的徒步，朝最近的镇子走去。

## XIV. Back in London / 第十四章 重返伦敦

===Original===
Boyce was smoking an excellent cigar, and was generally pleased with himself. He had just received a short note from the Prime Minister, thanking him for his good work in running the murderer of the Home Secretary to earth, and hinting that when the time came for the retirement of that fine old soldier, Sir Thomas Hawley, as Chief Commissioner for London, the new Home Secretary could not do better than appoint so efficient an officer as he had proved himself to be.

This was good reading. He had feared some strong words about his allowing lunatics to be at large, but the truth was that Sir James had never been popular with his colleagues, as he was considered reserved, and had not lent himself to giving soft berths to the nephews and friends of his fellow Cabinet Ministers.

His death had enabled the Premier to reshuffle the Ministry, and bring in an impecunious nephew of his own to a minor post.

So everyone was happy.

Boyce rang the bell and sent for Sinclair.

===Chinese===
博伊斯正抽着一支上等雪茄，通身上下志得意满。他刚收到首相的一封短笺，感谢他将杀害内政大臣的凶手缉拿归案，并暗示说，待到那位优秀的老军人、伦敦警察总监托马斯·霍利爵士（Sir Thomas Hawley）荣休之时，新任内政大臣若能委任一位像他这般久经考验的干练官员，实为上策。

这封信读来令人舒坦。他原本还担心，为了他放任疯子在外游荡一事，会挨几句重话；但事实是，詹姆斯爵士在同僚中向来不得人缘——人家嫌他落落寡合，又不肯通融，把闲差美缺安插给内阁同僚们的子侄亲友。

他的死倒让首相得以改组内阁，把自己一个囊中羞涩的侄子安插进一个次要职位。

于是人人皆大欢喜。

博伊斯按铃叫人，把辛克莱唤了来。

===Original===
The latter was not in the same genial mood. None of the reflected glory of Boyce’s triumph had come his way, and he was perfectly convinced that whoever was guilty of the murder, Jackson was not.

“Take a seat, Sinclair,” said Boyce. “You might care to see this letter from the Premier,” and he handed it over with an air of indifference which did not deceive the other.

“Very good, sir, I congratulate you,” he said, simply.

“And now, Sinclair, I want to read you the indictment which Giles, of the Public Prosecutor’s office, has drawn up. I think it is very well done. Of course it is only in the form of notes.

===Chinese===
后者却没这份好兴致。博伊斯凯旋的荣光一丝也没照到他身上，而且他深信不疑：无论真凶是谁，反正不是杰克逊。

“请坐，辛克莱。”博伊斯说，“首相这封来信，你不妨过过目。”他把信递过去，摆出一副满不在乎的神气，可并没有骗过对方。

“很好，长官，恭喜您。”他淡淡地说。

“好了，辛克莱，我想给你念念公诉官署（Public Prosecutor’s office）的吉尔斯（Giles）拟好的起诉书。我认为拟得相当周到。当然，眼下还只是要点的形式。

===Original===
Case against John Jackson, for the murder of Sir James Watson, Bt.

- 
						

Jackson confesses in three separate statements that he has done the murder. But this without corroboration is of little value, since he has been declared insane by Medical Experts.

- 
						

We have, therefore, to seek corroboration. Jackson states that he was several times hanging about the house in Leveson Square waiting for his chance.

This is confirmed by P.C. Jenkins and P.C. Whiting, both of whom have identified the man as having been seen in the vicinity of the Square.

===Chinese===
对约翰·杰克逊的指控：谋杀詹姆斯·沃森从男爵。

- 

杰克逊在三份各自独立的口供中都供认自己行凶。但孤证无佐，此类供认价值寥寥，因为医学专家已宣告他精神失常。

- 

因此，我们必须另寻佐证。杰克逊称，他曾多次在勒维森广场那座宅子附近徘徊，等候下手的机会。

这一点已由警员詹金斯（P.C. Jenkins）与警员怀廷（P.C. Whiting）证实，两人都辨认出此人确曾在广场一带出没。

===Original===
- 
						

Jackson states that he called on Superintendent Sinclair three days previous to the murder, and while in his office, stole writing paper, and a letter signed by the latter, and on this paper wrote to the Central News Agency.

Superintendent Sinclair confirms that such a call was made, but cannot trace any missing letter, though there might have been one.

“Is that so?” said Boyce, glancing at the other.

Sinclair made a face.

“Well, I certainly said I remembered the man calling, but I told them there was no missing letter. When the lawyer asked whether it would have been possible for such a letter to have been taken, I said of course it was possible, but highly improbable. That’s the way the lawyers twist evidence, but go on, sir.”

===Chinese===
- 

杰克逊称，案发前三天他曾拜访辛克莱警司，趁在其办公室之机，窃得信纸若干及一封由后者署名的信函，并在这种信纸上致函中央新闻社。

辛克莱警司确认确有此番来访，但查不出有信函遗失，尽管未必没有这种可能。

“是这么回事吗？”博伊斯说着，瞟了对方一眼。

辛克莱做了个苦脸。

“唉，我确实说过我记得此人来访，但我告诉过他们没有信函遗失。律师问我，有没有可能丢失了这样一封信，我自然说有可能，只是极不可能。律师们就是这样歪曲证词的。您接着念吧，长官。”

===Original===
Boyce looked as though he was about to rebuke him for this heretical sentiment, but continued.

- 
						

Jackson states that he called Sinclair and Collins on the phone, and corroboration can be obtained﻿—he had the opportunity to do so﻿—and there is a doubtful identification by a paper seller, who says he saw him leave the Public Telephone Call Office at Piccadilly Circus, at the time named. This man is not a reliable character, but the evidence can be used if necessary.

- 
						

*Revolver*. Jackson makes a rambling statement as to how he got the revolver which cannot be relied upon, but as he is mad this does not count for much.

===Chinese===
博伊斯神色一凛，像是要为这句离经叛道的话呵斥他，却还是接着念了下去。

- 

杰克逊称，他曾打电话给辛克莱和科林斯，此节可获得佐证——他有这样的机会——另有一名卖报人的指认存疑，此人声称在所指明的时刻看见他从皮卡迪利广场（Piccadilly Circus）的公用电话亭走出来。此人并非可靠的证人，但如有必要，这项证据也可一用。

- 

*左轮手枪*。关于那把左轮手枪的来历，杰克逊的供述颠三倒四，无从凭信；不过他既是疯子，这点也就无关紧要。

===Original===
Sinclair lifted his eyebrows. “That’s rather amusing,” he said. “Where they find corroboration, they accept his statements; where they don’t, they say he is mad.”

Boyce looked at him severely. “This is only a confidential memo,” he said, “for the information of the office only.”

“I see,” said Sinclair, with contempt.

Boyce went on reading.

- 
						

*Motive*. Although motive is not essential in the case of a lunatic, it is helpful with a certain type of criminal’s mental derangement. There is abundant evidence that Jackson had a fancied grievance against the late Home Secretary, who had turned down all his petitions for release from the asylum at Broadmoor. He had also sent threatening letters to Sir James.

===Chinese===
辛克莱扬了扬眉毛。“这倒有意思，”他说，“找得到佐证的地方，他们就采信他的供述；找不到佐证的地方，就说他是疯子。”

博伊斯严厉地瞪着他。“这只是一份机密备忘录，”他说，“仅供本部参考。”

“明白了。”辛克莱轻蔑地说。

博伊斯继续往下念。

- 

*动机*。就疯子而言，动机虽非要件，却有助于剖析某一类罪犯的精神错乱。现有充分证据表明，杰克逊对已故内政大臣怀有臆想的仇怨——正是这位大臣驳回了他申请从布罗德莫尔出院的一切呈请。他还曾向詹姆斯爵士寄出恐吓信。

===Original===
- 
						

*The Actual Crime*. Jackson states that he followed Sir James in, after he had been to the Pillar Box in the Square to post a letter. Mrs. Simmons declared on oath that he did not, but on further examination, when asked to swear that no one came in after that, said she could not do so, and showed great signs of confusion.

Sinclair pricked up his ears. “I wonder,” he said.

“What’s that?” said Boyce.

“Well, sir, Collins always said that she was not telling the whole truth to us, and that she was hiding something.”

===Chinese===
- 

*作案实况*。杰克逊称，詹姆斯爵士先到广场上的邮筒投寄了一封信，他随后尾随进门。西蒙斯太太曾宣誓作证说他没有进去，但再经盘问，被要求立誓担保其后无人进屋时，她却说办不到，且流露出极大的慌乱。

辛克莱竖起了耳朵。“有意思。”他说。

“什么有意思？”博伊斯说。

“是这样，长官，科林斯一直说，她没对我们讲出全部实情，她隐瞒着什么。”

“这不就成了。”博伊斯说，“当然，若真到了庭审、生死攸关的地步，我们就该把那老太婆翻个底朝天，她多半就会招认；至于她为何要设法包庇他，我就琢磨不透了；反正无论如何，我们有办法撬开她的嘴。嗯，案情就是这样。你觉得如何？”

===Original===
“There you are,” said Boyce. “Of course if it was a matter of a trial, and of life and death, we should turn the old woman inside out, and she would probably confess; though why she should try and screen him, is more than I can tell; we may have it out of her in any case. Well, that’s the case. What do you think of it?”

“I don’t think any jury would convict,” said Sinclair doggedly.

“Really, Sinclair, you are very obstinate; I suppose because you had no hand in catching the man.”

“I see in all the accounts,” said Sinclair, “it is made out that the police caught the man, and nothing is said of him giving himself up.” Boyce looked uncomfortable.

“It doesn’t do any harm, and does the police good,” he said; “and in any case I am sure they would have got him,” he ended, lamely.

Sinclair remained silent.

===Chinese===
“我看任何陪审团都不会定罪。”辛克莱固执地说。

“真是的，辛克莱，你倔得很；我琢磨着，是因为抓这家伙你没出上力吧。”

“我看所有的报道，”辛克莱说，“都写成是警方抓到了这个人，对他主动投案一事只字不提。”博伊斯神色不自在起来。

“这没什么害处，对警方却有好处，”他说，“再说，反正我确信他们迟早也会抓到他。”末了这句，他说得底气不足。

辛克莱不作声。

博伊斯恼了。

===Original===
Boyce was annoyed.

“Here’s my idea of what occurred,” said he, rather peevishly.

“Jackson comes out from the asylum, we will assume, partially cured. He has nothing to do, and gradually the old madness comes over him. He nurses his grievance against Sir James until it becomes an obsession. He comes to you about it. Then he sees the official paper on your desk, and with a madman’s cunning he takes some pieces.

“Perhaps he thinks he will write to Sir James on it, who can say? He hangs about waiting for a chance, possibly only to speak to him. He had obtained a revolver, goodness knows where, and then the plan matures. With the cunning and vanity of insanity, he writes to the Central News﻿—which by the way no one but a madman would do, and calls you and Collins up for the same reason. Perhaps he was watching you all the time when you were at the house.

===Chinese===
“依我看，事情经过是这样的。”他颇为烦躁地说。

“我们不妨这样假定：杰克逊从精神病院出来时已部分痊愈。他无所事事，旧日的疯狂便渐渐重新袭来。他对詹姆斯爵士的积怨越养越深，终成痼念。他为这事来找过你。接着他看见你桌上的公文纸，便怀着疯子的狡黠顺走了几张。

“他也许盘算着拿这纸给詹姆斯爵士写信——谁说得准呢？他在那一带游荡，守候时机，或许只求同爵士说句话。左轮手枪他从哪儿弄到的，天晓得；随后整个计划便酝酿成形。他怀着疯狂所特有的狡诈与虚荣，给中央新闻社写信——顺带一句，这种事除了疯子没人干得出——又出于同样的缘由打电话找你和科林斯。你们在那座宅子里的时候，说不定他一直在暗中盯着。

===Original===
“He sees Sir James come out with a letter, and as he states, he nearly killed him then, but thinks he will do so inside.

“He follows him in, and shoots him and escapes.

“He is watching you, and when you go to Collins’ flat, he follows and leaves that stupid message which also is the work of a madman which you told me about. How’s that for a case?”

Sinclair remained silent for a minute.

===Chinese===
“他看见詹姆斯爵士拿着一封信出来，据他自己供述，当时就差点动了手，但转念决定还是到屋里再干。

“他尾随进门，开枪杀人，然后脱身而去。

“他一直盯着你，等你去了科林斯的公寓，便跟上去留下那条愚蠢的口信——你跟我讲过那事，那同样出自疯子的手笔。这样一桩案子，够圆满吧？”

辛克莱沉默了片刻。

===Original===
“A good counsel could smash it to pieces. I am certain that he never took anything from my room, but of course it is one of those things one cannot swear to. If he followed Sir James in, why did not Mrs. Simmons see him; and if she did, why should she screen him? Why did she hear no shot? And if she did, why did she not raise the alarm? How did he get out again, and close and lock the door behind him? Then who was the man who called on Sir James in the afternoon? Jackson makes no claim to be that man, probably because no mention was made of it in the papers?

“What I think happened is as Collins suggested. This man read all the accounts and so got them into his head that he is quite certain he did the murder. It is not an uncommon phase.”

Boyce interrupted. “I have no patience with all this. Of course there are difficulties. Whoever heard of a case where there were not, but the evidence in my opinion is overwhelming. Anyway, I am satisfied.”

“Very good, sir, if you are convinced, that is sufficient. What does the Public Prosecutor think of it?”

===Chinese===
“出色的辩护律师能把它砸个粉碎。我敢说他绝没从我房里拿走过任何东西，不过这当然是那种谁也无法起誓担保的事。倘若他是尾随詹姆斯爵士进门的，西蒙斯太太怎么会没看见他？就算看见了，她又为何要包庇他？她怎么会没听见枪声？就算听见了，为何不报警呼救？他又是怎么出来的，还能随手把门带上锁好？再者，那天下午拜访詹姆斯爵士的人又是谁？杰克逊可从没自称是那个人，多半因为报上对此只字未提吧？

“照我看，实情正如科林斯所推测的。这人读遍了所有报道，印象深植脑海，便笃信自己就是凶手。这种心理状态并不罕见。”

博伊斯打断了他。“这一套我可没耐心听。案子当然有疑点。谁听说过没有疑点的案子？但依我看，证据是压倒性的。总之，我满意了。”

“很好，长官，您若信服，那就够了。公诉长官（Public Prosecutor）又怎么看？”

===Original===
“My dear Sinclair, have you been so long in the service as not to know that the Public Prosecutor is not concerned with opinions, but to make out a case on the evidence.”

“And so you think that the case is ended?”

“I think,” said Boyce unctuously, “that this poor fellow will go back to Broadmoor, from which he ought never to have been released, and that our Department will have scored a triumph.”

“By the way,” he said, as if anxious to change the conversation. “What has happened to our friend Collins, he seems to have disappeared?”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的辛克莱，你在这行当里干了这么久，难道还不知道公诉长官是不问见解、只凭证据立案的么。”

“这么说，您认为这案子已经了结了？”

“我认为，”博伊斯拿腔拿调地说，“这个可怜虫会被送回布罗德莫尔——他压根就不该放出来——而我们部里也将大功告成一次。”

“对了，”他说，像是急于岔开话头，“我们的朋友科林斯出什么事了？他人好像消失了。”

===Original===
“Oh, he’s gone down to Devonshire to Sir James’ place.”

“What, is he still on some wild goose chase?”

Sinclair smiled. “I rather fancy it’s a different sort of chase from what I saw in London. Eric Sanders will have to look to his laurels.”

“Ho-ho, is that it? Fancy our friend Collins. He doesn’t fit in with marriage bells, somehow. I expect if there’s anything in it, he will give up amateur detective work.”

===Chinese===
“哦，他到德文郡詹姆斯爵士的宅子去了。”

“什么，他还在搞那种徒劳无功的追查？”

辛克莱微微一笑。“我倒觉得，这趟追猎跟我在伦敦见的那趟不是一回事。埃里克·桑德斯可得看好自己的桂冠了。”

“嗬嗬，是这么回事？真想不到我们的朋友科林斯。他这个人怎么也跟婚礼的钟声不搭调。我想要是真有其事，他就会金盆洗手，不干业余侦探这营生了。”

===Original===
“Mr. Sylvester Collins to see you, sir,” said the messenger.

“Show him in,” said Boyce. Then in a whisper﻿—“Not a word about this, he will only start arguing.”

Collins entered. He was neatly dressed as always, but he had a gaunt look and the lines on his face suggested sleepless nights.

“Where have you sprung from?” said Boyce, with affected geniality of manner. He was not anxious to go over the whole case with this man whose keen intellect he feared.

“Oh, I have been first in Devonshire and for the last three days on a walking tour.”

===Chinese===
“西尔维斯特·科林斯先生求见，长官。”信差说。

“请他进来。”博伊斯说。随即又压低嗓门——“这桩事一个字也别提，他一准要跟咱们抬杠。”

科林斯走了进来。他衣着一如既往地整洁，人却形容枯槁，脸上的皱纹透着彻夜无眠的痕迹。

“你这是打哪儿冒出来的？”博伊斯说，摆出一副故作亲热的样子。他可不想跟这个人把全案重捋一遍——此人头脑何等锐利，他是忌惮的。

“哦，我先是在德文郡，最近三天徒步旅行来着。”

===Original===
“You look it,” said Boyce.

“I really came to see Sinclair, but heard he was with you, so came on.”

Boyce looked uncomfortable. “Would you two rather be together? I have finished with him.”

“I suppose you have just settled the case of Sir James to your satisfaction, eh?” he said with a laugh.

“Oh, I know you do not agree with our conclusions, but I would much rather not go into the whole matter.”

===Chinese===
“瞧你这副样子就知道了。”博伊斯说。

“我本是来找辛克莱的，听说他在你这儿，就顺路进来了。”

博伊斯显得不大自在。“你们俩想单独谈谈吗？我跟他没事了。”

“我猜，詹姆斯爵士的案子你们刚办得称心如意了吧？”他笑着问。

“哦，我知道你不赞成我们的结论，可这案子我实在不想再从头掰扯了。”

===Original===
“I don’t wish to discuss it. I think you have come to the wisest decision you could under the circumstances.”

“Now you are trying to be sarcastic.”

“Not at all. I had an idea of my own, but I don’t think it is worth following up. I have finished with the case, and am quite satisfied with the way things have turned out.”

The other two looked at him in astonishment.

“Well, you have changed your opinion. I am very glad,” said Boyce, with genuine satisfaction.

===Chinese===
“我也不想讨论。我认为，在此情形之下，你们已作出了再明智不过的决定。”

“你这可是在说反话了。”

“哪里的话。我自己原有个想法，但我想不值得再追下去。这案子我已经撒手不管，如今这结局，我相当满意。”

另外两人愕然望着他。

“哎呀，你改了看法。我很高兴。”博伊斯说，语气里是真正的满足。

===Original===
Sinclair looked bewildered.

“So your clue proved a fraud, did it?” he said.

“It did not lead where I expected,” he answered.

“This is all Greek to me,” said Boyce; “won’t you tell us?”

“No; it would only introduce the name of a man who has nothing to do with the matter.”

“You wanted to see me?” said Sinclair, still puzzled.

===Chinese===
辛克莱一脸茫然。

“这么说，你那条线索到头来是场空？”他说。

“它没有引向我所预期的地方。”他答道。

“我简直如闻天书，”博伊斯说，“就不能给我们讲讲？”

“不能；那只会牵出一个与本案毫无干系的人的名字。”

“你要见我？”辛克莱问，仍是一头雾水。

===Original===
“Any time will do. By the way, Boyce, how long do you think it will be before your case is finished? I mean, all settled?”

“I can’t say, you know the course of the law is not swift.”

“Shall we say a month?”

“I should think that will easily cover it.”

“Why are you so anxious to know?”

===Chinese===
“什么时候都行。对了，博伊斯，依你看，你这案子还要多久才能了结？我是说，全部办妥？”

“说不好，你知道，法律程序的脚步是快不了的。”

“咱们不妨估作一个月？”

“我想一个月绰绰有余了。”

“你干嘛这么急着知道？”

===Original===
Collins flicked the ash off his cigarette into an ash tray.

“Nothing much, only I know who the murderer was, and I wanted to know how long it would be before your man was convicted.”

The other two gazed at him in utter astonishment.

“Do you mean to say that you think you know who murdered Sir James and you are not going to tell who it was?” said Boyce.

“I never had any doubt in my own mind at all. But to give him up﻿—no, I am afraid that would be impossible. You see, he doesn’t exist.”

===Chinese===
科林斯把香烟的灰弹进烟灰缸里。

“没什么大不了，只不过我知道真凶是谁，想弄清楚你们那位还要多久才会被定罪。”

另外两人目瞪口呆地凝视着他。

“你是说，你知道是谁谋杀了詹姆斯爵士，却又不打算说出那人是谁？”博伊斯说。

“我心里可从来没有过半点疑问。可要我把这个人交出去——不，恐怕那是不可能的。要知道，他这个人并不存在。”

===Original===
“Doesn’t exist? What nonsense. Are you trying one of your jokes on us?” said Boyce, crossly; he hated mysteries.

“He’s gone, disappeared, vamoosed.”

“Do you mean he’s dead?” said Boyce.

“The question is, did he ever exist?”

“Oh, I’ve no patience with this sort of talk,” said Boyce. “If you know anything, for goodness sake say what it is; if not, don’t talk in riddles.”

===Chinese===
“不存在？一派胡言。你这是又拿我们打趣？”博伊斯愠怒地说；他最恨故弄玄虚。

“他走了，失踪了，跑没影了。”

“你是说他死了？”博伊斯问。

“问题是，他到底存在过没有？”

“哎呀，这种话我听着就没耐性，”博伊斯说，“你要是知道点什么，千万看在老天爷份上说出来；不知道，就别出谜语让人猜。”

===Original===
Sinclair had been watching keenly. His face was grave.

“Yes, I think I know what you mean,” he said.

“Oh, you, too. What on earth are you getting at?”

“I shall be in a position to say in a few days’ time, to tell you more,” said Sinclair.

“I wish you two would not be so confoundedly mysterious,” said Boyce.

===Chinese===
辛克莱一直在敏锐地注视着。他面色凝重。

“是，我想我明白你的意思。”他说。

“哦，连你也来了。你们葫芦里到底卖的什么药？”

“再过几天，我就有条件多说一些了。”辛克莱说。

“你们俩别这么神神叨叨的行不行。”博伊斯说。

===Original===
“If you’ve got anything to tell me, do so. As for you, Sinclair, I expect loyalty from you at any rate.”

Sinclair replied with some stiffness.

“I shall not take any action without consulting you, sir, and I may be quite wrong.”

“Very well,” said Boyce, with a gesture of dismissal.

Alone together Collins and Sinclair went to the latter’s room.

===Chinese===
“你们有什么要告诉我的，尽管讲。至于你，辛克莱，我至少还指望你尽一份忠心。”

辛克莱的回答带着几分生硬。

“未经向您请示，我不会擅自采取行动，长官；何况我也可能大错特错。”

“很好。”博伊斯说着，摆手示意送客。

只剩两人后，科林斯和辛克莱一同去了辛克莱的房间。

===Original===
“What an ass that man is,” said Collins. “He hasn’t the brains of a rabbit.”

“I wonder,” said the other, “whether he is quite the fool you think him.”

“What are your plans?”

“Do you know a place called Wilton-on-Sea?” said Sinclair. Collins looked keenly at him.

“Yes, I know it,” he said.

===Chinese===
“那家伙真是头蠢驴，”科林斯说，“连兔子的脑量都没有。”

“我倒有些怀疑，”另一位说，“他是不是真像你想的那么蠢。”

“你有什么打算？”

“你可知道一个叫滨海威尔顿的地方？”辛克莱说。科林斯锐利地打量着他。

“知道，我熟悉那地方。”他说。

===Original===
“I am thinking of running down there,” said Sinclair.

“What on earth are you going there for?”

“I have been told that it is very good air, and as I have a few days’ leave, I thought I would try it.”

Collins thought for a moment.

“Well, you probably don’t know, but it is quite close to Sir James Watson’s place﻿—in fact, it is the station for it.”

===Chinese===
“我打算到那儿去一趟。”辛克莱说。

“你到底去那儿干什么？”

“人家跟我说那边空气很好，我手头有几天的假期，就想过去试试。”

科林斯沉吟片刻。

“唔，你多半不知道，那地方离詹姆斯·沃森爵士的宅子很近——其实就是那宅子通火车的车站。”

===Original===
“Really?” said Sinclair. “Then of course you know it well?”

“If you are really going there I will run you down in my car. You might like to see Sir James’ place.”

The men looked at each other.

“I would like to see inside your head, and find out what there is there,” said Sinclair. “You’ve something concealed.”

Collins laughed. “That’s just what I was thinking. What are you after? Well, we will each keep his own counsel.”

===Chinese===
“是吗？”辛克莱说，“那你想必对那地方了如指掌了？”

“你若真要去，我可以用车送你去。詹姆斯爵士的宅子，你说不定愿意参观参观。”

两人对视了一眼。

“我真想钻进你脑袋里看一看，弄清楚里头到底装着什么，”辛克莱说，“你有事藏着掖着。”

科林斯笑了。“我心里想的正是同一件事。你打的是什么主意？好吧，咱们各守各的心事。”

## XV. The Crisis / 第十五章 危局

===Original===
Beyond Wilton-on-Sea, there is a bare hill standing gaunt above the surrounding country.

On the seaward side the cliff is sheer, and to the West a sudden drop into a quarry pit makes for danger. On the East a very steep path leads to a semi-ruined church, surrounded by a church yard, and some little distance away is a tower where once stood an ancient castle.

The church forms a landmark for miles.

From a distance it appears to be an imposing edifice. On near approach there is a tiny chancel which still retains a roof, and a nave with no roof. It is all very small, like the chapel of a stronghold in days gone by. At the base of the hill is a public-house of mean appearance, and also some straggling houses.

===Chinese===
滨海威尔顿郊外，有一座光秃秃的山丘，嶙峋峭拔，俯临四周的乡野。

临海的一面悬崖笔直，西边骤然陷落成一个采石坑，颇为凶险。东面一条极陡的小径通往一座半颓的教堂，教堂四周围着一方墓园，再隔开些许，立着一座塔楼，那里曾雄踞着一座古堡。

这座教堂数英里外便可望见，是一处地标。

远看它俨然一座气派的建筑。走近了才见，只有小小的圣坛间还留着屋顶，中殿却是露天的。整个教堂小得很，宛如往昔要塞里的礼拜堂。山脚下有一家面目寒酸的小酒馆，还零落地缀着几户人家。

===Original===
It was here that Sinclair and Collins had taken up their residence. For three days they had been glued to the spot. A fretful distrust of each other was growing up, which they tried their best to hide.

There had been no talk of going to Sir James’ house. Collins would sit in the little sitting room upstairs, reading, with one eye on the window. Sinclair was more restless; he wandered outside, prowling round the base of the hill but never going up.

He appeared to be drinking more than was good for him, and evidently suffering from the strain of waiting. Each was sure that the other was keeping something to himself, but whatever it was it had drawn them to this spot. Evening was coming on after a grey autumn afternoon, and a thin drizzle was falling. It was a time when a wise man hugs his fire, and is glad to draw the curtains and light a cheery lamp.

There was no light in the small upper room where Collins sat like a sphinx. Sinclair was glowering in the armchair, his face slightly grey, and a worried look in his eyes.

===Chinese===
辛克莱和科林斯就在这儿落了脚。一连三天，他们死守在这地方，寸步不离。两人之间渐渐生出烦躁的相互猜忌，只是都竭力遮掩。

谁也没提过要去詹姆斯爵士的宅子。科林斯总是坐在楼上的小客厅里看书，一只眼睛却瞟着窗外。辛克莱更坐不住；他在外头转悠，绕着山脚逡巡，却从不上山。

他看去酒喝得过了度，分明正被等待的煎熬磨着。两人都拿准对方心里藏着事，但不管藏的是什么，那桩事把他们引到了此地。灰暗的秋日下午过尽，暮色四合，天上飘着蒙蒙细雨。这样的时辰，聪明人只该偎着炉火，乐得拉上窗帘，点起一盏明亮的灯。

楼上那间小屋里没有点灯，科林斯端坐其中，活像一尊斯芬克斯。辛克莱阴郁地瞪着眼，窝在扶手椅里，面色微微发灰，眼里含着忧色。

===Original===
The hillside was getting dark, and the church on the top stood out black against the western sky. A straggling group of people were coming down the steep path. There had been a service in the tiny chancel, and curiosity had drawn visitors to attend.

Perhaps a dozen or so were descending the steep pathway.

Collins gave a slight movement, and drew in his breath quickly.

“At last!” he said, almost involuntarily.

===Chinese===
山坡渐渐暗了，山顶的教堂衬着西天显出一团黑影。稀稀落落的一小群人正顺着陡径走下来。方才小圣坛里做了一场礼拜，好奇心招来了看客。

走下这条陡径的，大约有十来个人。

科林斯微微一动，急促地吸了口气。

“终于来了！”他说，几乎是不由自主。

===Original===
He sprang to his feet, and took his mackintosh from a chair.

Sinclair got up, too. “Well?” he said.

Collins laughed. “Come on then. I see you want to be in at the death.”

Without a word Sinclair put on his coat, and followed.

===Chinese===
他一跃而起，从椅背上拿起雨衣。

辛克莱也站了起来。“怎么？”他说。

科林斯笑了。“那就走吧。我看你是不见到收场不甘心。”

辛克莱一言不发，穿上外套，跟着去了。

===Original===
At the point where the steep path wound upwards there was a lychgate. Here in the shadow they waited while the rain dripped off the tiled roof. The people had passed, and a solitary figure was approaching in the gathering gloom.

He was scarcely a yard off, when Sinclair made an exclamation, and sprang forward. He laid his hand on the other’s shoulder and looked straight in his face. “Ah,” he said, “Lewis at last! I arrest you for the murder of Sir James Watson, and I warn you﻿—but of course you know all about that.” The other made no movement of protest or resentment. Collins came forward smiling blandly.

===Chinese===
陡径蜿蜒上山的去处有一座墓园门廊。两人在廊影里候着，雨水顺着瓦檐滴答滴答地淌。人群过去了，渐浓的暮色里，一个孤零零的身影正走近前来。

那人离他们不过一码远近，辛克莱低呼一声，纵身扑上前去。他一手按住来人的肩头，直盯着他的脸。“啊，”他说，“总算逮着你了，刘易斯！你因谋杀詹姆斯·沃森爵士被捕了。我警告你——不过这些你自然都清楚。”对方没有半点抗辩或愠怒的举动。科林斯走上前来，一脸温煦的笑。

===Original===
“Steady, Sinclair, don’t let your professional zeal run away with you. You haven’t a warrant to start with, and you are mistaking your man.”

“What do you mean?” said Sinclair, turning to him.

“You are mistaking your man, that is all. Let me introduce you. This is Sir Ronald Watson, Baronet, Superintendent Sinclair.”

A look of blank astonishment was on Sinclair’s face, and he looked from one to the other in bewilderment.

===Chinese===
“稳着点，辛克莱，别让你的职业热情冲昏了头。第一，你手里连逮捕令都没有；第二，你还认错了人。”

“你什么意思？”辛克莱转身朝他问道。

“你认错了人，就这么回事。我来给你引见。辛克莱警司，这位是罗纳德·沃森爵士，从男爵。”

辛克莱满脸茫然错愕，困惑地望望这个，又望望那个。

===Original===
“What on earth do you mean?” he said.

The other man turned to Collins, “Hast thou found me, oh mine enemy?” he said, with a smile, which belied his words.

“Come on,” said Collins; “let’s get indoors, it’s beastly out here.”

“So be it,” said the other.

===Chinese===
“你这到底是什么意思？”他说。

那人转向科林斯。“我仇敌啊，你寻着我了么？”他说着，唇边带笑，那笑意却与话里的敌意全不相称。

“走吧，”科林斯说，“进屋去，外头糟透了。”

“也好。”那人应道。

===Original===
They crossed the road where the mud was splashing, and entered the house. Once in the room, the stranger turned to Sinclair.

“Yes; I am Ronald Watson, though how Mr. Collins has run me to earth is a mystery. And what you are doing in the matter, unless you knew my identity, is more than I can say.”

“I don’t understand it at all,” said Sinclair, who was inclined to be sulky at the turn of affairs. “It seems to me there will have to be a lot of explanation.”

“You shall have it. The time has gone by for this concealment. It was a mistake. Only let me tell you I had nothing to do with the murder of my poor father.”

===Chinese===
他们横过泥浆飞溅的马路，进了屋。一进房间，那陌生人便转身对着辛克莱。

“不错，我就是罗纳德·沃森。只是科林斯先生究竟怎么把我追查到的，对我来说还是个谜。至于您在这桩事里做些什么，若不是您早知道我的身份，我就更无从猜起了。”

“我全然摸不着头脑，”辛克莱说。事态如此转折，他不禁有些恼。“依我看，你们是非好好解释一番不可了。”

“你们会得到解释的。遮遮掩掩的日子已经过去了。那本是个错。只是请容我讲一句：我可怜的父亲遇害，与我毫无干系。”

===Original===
“I can answer for that,” said Collins.

“Look here,” continued Collins, “I have my car here, and I suggest that we run over to The Vale, and have a full explanation there.”

“Yes; that will be the best thing,” said Watson, or Lewis. A sudden thought crossed Sinclair’s mind. “Oh, of course. That will do,” he said. Collins was watching him keenly.

“Will you settle up here then, while Watson and I get the car ready?”

===Chinese===
“这一点我可以担保。”科林斯说。

“是这样，”科林斯接着说，“我的车就在这儿，我建议我们开车去谷园，到那里把事情原原本本讲清楚。”

“好，那样最好。”沃森——或者说刘易斯——应道。辛克莱脑中忽然掠过一个念头。“哦，自然，就这么办。”他说。科林斯正锐利地注视着他。

“那么请你在这儿把账结了，我和沃森去把车备好，如何？”

===Original===
Sinclair was about to demur, but after all this was Collins’ capture, and he could not very well insist on keeping the man with him, and as Collins had said he had no warrant.

Without waiting for an answer, the other two went out.

In a few minutes the humming of the car was heard, and Sinclair, having paid their bill, went to the front door.

The small luggage they had brought was always ready packed, for each had felt they might have to move in a hurry.

At the door Collins and Watson were already in the car, and Sinclair got in behind with none too good a grace.

They set off into the night at a rapid pace.

===Chinese===
辛克莱本想表示异议，可人到底是科林斯擒来的，他不好硬留在自己身边，何况科林斯说了，他并没有逮捕令。

那两位没等他答话便出去了。

不多几分钟，汽车的马达嗡嗡响起，辛克莱结清了账，走到前门口。

他们随身带来的那点行李始终收拾得停停当当，因为两人都觉着，说不定就得仓促动身。

门口，科林斯和沃森已在车里坐定，辛克莱上了后座，老大不情愿。

汽车风驰电掣，一头扎进夜色里。

---

===Original===
Old John came to the door in answer to their ring, and Collins got down, leaving the others in the car.

In a matter-of-fact voice he asked whether he could see Miss Watson﻿—he would not come in, he had some friends in the car.

John said he would ask her to come. Whatever astonishment he felt he did not show. Warned by some premonition she could not master, Mabel came at once. She had just been going up to dress.

“How do you do, Mr. Collins?” she said. “John tells me you won’t come in.”

===Chinese===
老约翰应铃出来开门，科林斯下了车，让其余两人留在车里。

他用平平常常的口气问，能否见一见沃森小姐——他就不进屋了，车上有几位朋友。

约翰说他去请她来。无论心里多么惊讶，他脸上分毫未露。梅布尔被一种按捺不住的预感攫住，立刻出来了。她本刚要上楼更衣。

“您好，科林斯先生，”她说，“约翰告诉我，您不肯进来。”

===Original===
“I wanted to speak to you first,” he said. “Don’t be alarmed, I have your brother here with me. I brought him over from Wilton.”

She turned white and caught hold of the post of the door.

“My brother?” she stammered.

“Yes; he feels, and I agree with him, that the time has come for a full explanation.”

===Chinese===
“我想先跟您说句话，”他说，“别慌，您哥哥在我这儿。我把他从滨海威尔顿接来了。”

她脸色煞白，一把抓住门柱。

“我哥哥？”她结结巴巴地说。

“是的。他觉着——我也同意——把一切和盘托出的时机到了。”

===Original===
She lifted her head proudly. “There is nothing shameful or underhand.”

“I know that,” said Collins quietly, “but I have Superintendent Sinclair here, an old friend of mine from Scotland Yard, and he would like to hear the whole thing.”

“Scotland Yard?” she said. “He has not﻿—?”

“Arrested him. Oh, no, there’s nothing of that sort.”

===Chinese===
她高傲地扬起头来。“这里头没有见不得人的事，也没有鬼鬼祟祟的勾当。”

“这个我知道，”科林斯平静地说，“不过我身边这位是辛克莱警司，我在苏格兰场的老朋友，他想听听事情的原委。”

“苏格兰场？”她说，“他没有——？”

“逮捕他？哦，没有，没那回事。”

===Original===
A figure came from the car.

“It’s all right, Mabel, don’t be frightened. We’ve made fools of ourselves with all this secrecy, and we’ll have all the cards on the table now. Let’s get out of this wet.”

Sinclair advanced and shook hands with Mabel. He had met her, of course, in London, at the time of the Inquest, but could hardly claim acquaintance.

===Chinese===
一个身影从汽车里走了出来。

“没事，梅布尔，别怕。咱们这么遮遮掩掩，实在是自讨苦吃，如今索性把牌全摊到桌面上。先进屋，别淋着。”

辛克莱上前同梅布尔握手。验尸审讯那阵子，他自然在伦敦见过她，但谈不上相识。

===Original===
In the hall they were met by Allery and Eric Sanders.

Collins opened his eyes in surprise.

“Eric,” said Mabel. “This is my brother.” The two men shook hands with a mutual look of interest.

Allery laughed outright.

“You young dog,” he said. “How did you come to be caught after all our trouble. I was afraid Collins would be one too much for us.﻿ ﻿… I congratulate you on your cleverness,” said he, turning to Collins.

===Chinese===
门厅里，艾勒里和埃里克·桑德斯迎了上来。

科林斯惊讶地睁大了眼睛。

“埃里克，”梅布尔说，“这是我哥哥。”两人握手，彼此都带着饶有兴味的神色打量对方。

艾勒里放声大笑。

“你这小滑头，”他说，“我们费了这么大周折，你怎么到头来还是被逮住了。我还担心科林斯这个人咱们斗不过呢。……”说着他转向科林斯，“我佩服你的手段。”

===Original===
“I am afraid this is all Greek to me,” said Sinclair stiffly. He had expected Tragedy, and found Comic Opera.

“You shall know everything,” said Allery; “but come to dinner first. Can you manage it?” he said to Mabel.

“I’ll go and see about it at once,” said she.

“As these three poor men have no dress clothes with them, we will have a scratch meal, and then a round talk.” Her spirits seemed to have risen now that the affair was over.

===Chinese===
“恐怕这对我全然是天书，”辛克莱生硬地说。他满以为会见到一出悲剧，撞见的却是一幕喜歌剧。

“你们自会知道一切，”艾勒里说，“不过先用晚饭。你张罗得开吗？”他对梅布尔说。

“我这就去打点。”她说。

“这三位可怜的先生没带礼服，我们就吃顿便饭，然后痛痛快快长谈一场。”事情既然收了场，她的兴致似乎也高涨起来。

===Original===
Dinner was a merry meal, even if the gaiety was somewhat forced.

Old John moved round with a look of blank astonishment on his face. His eyes were round and wide, and he could not keep them off Watson. The aunt had sent a message that she would not come to dinner. The news had proved too much for her nerves.

When the port was on the table, and John had retired, Mabel said, “This room is very comfy, and I think we might go into matters here. You can smoke.”

“Not till after this excellent port,” said Collins. “Our ancestors would turn in their graves if we smoked with the port. Isn’t that so, Watson?”

===Chinese===
晚饭吃得很欢，尽管那欢畅有几分是强打出来的。

老约翰在旁边张罗，一脸茫然错愕。他两眼瞪得溜圆，目光怎么也离不开沃森。姑母捎下话来，说不下来用晚饭了。这个消息对她的神经实在刺激太甚。

波特酒端上桌、约翰退下去之后，梅布尔说：“这间屋子很舒服，我想咱们就在这儿谈吧。你们可以抽烟。”

“得先喝完这上好的波特酒，”科林斯说，“喝波特酒时抽烟，我们的祖先在坟墓里也要翻身的。是不是，沃森？”

===Original===
“That was the custom,” said the other with a smile. There was no trace of the Scotland Yard “hack” now in this man who presided at the table as one born to it.

“Well, before we have your story,” said Collins, “I would like to know how many were in the plot. Miss Watson and you, Allery, I know. Anyone else?” and he glanced sharply at Sanders.

“You are a wizard,” said Allery. “No, there were only us two. Sanders knew nothing about it.”

Sinclair moved uneasily. Was he in a madhouse? “Plot” and “secret”! These people were talking as though they were playing a game, and he had come on the track of a murderer.

===Chinese===
“从前确有这个规矩。”那人微笑着说。此刻他主掌席面，一派天生熟惯的气度，身上再无半点苏格兰场“雇佣文员”的影子。

“那么，在听你的故事之前，”科林斯说，“我想知道这桩共谋里共有几个人。沃森小姐和你，艾勒里，这两个我知道了。还有谁没有？”说着他利落地扫了桑德斯一眼。

“你真是个活神仙，”艾勒里说，“没有，总共只有我们两个。桑德斯毫不知情。”

辛克莱不自在地挪了挪。他莫不是进了疯人院？“共谋”、“秘密”！这伙人谈笑自若，仿佛在玩一场游戏，而他一路追来，追的是个杀人凶手。

===Original===
“Can we have the explanation of all this?” he said, testily.

“Certainly, Sinclair,” said Watson, “and you are entitled to one from me, at any rate. Here goes.”

The main lights were turned off, and only the electric bulbs in shades threw a soft light on the table. There was no sound in the room while Watson spoke.

===Chinese===
“这一切总可以给我们解释解释了吧？”他焦躁地说。

“当然，辛克莱，”沃森说，“至少我欠你一个交代。这就讲起。”

大灯关了，只剩几盏罩着灯罩的电灯泡，往桌面上洒下一片柔光。沃森讲话时，屋里声息全无。

===Original===
“I will not be more tedious than possible, but I want to make the narrative clear, so I must go back.”
