“I need not go into the reasons for my leaving England. Allery here, I know, thought I had done something criminal﻿—in fact, forged my father’s name. But I think I have convinced him that it was only a youthful outbreak, which I sincerely regret.” Allery nodded, gravely.

===Chinese===
“我尽量拣要紧的讲，不叫大家腻烦，但要把事说清楚，就必须从头讲起。”

“我离开英国的缘由，就不必细说了。我知道，这里的艾勒里当时以为我干了什么犯法的事——说穿了，就是以为我伪造了父亲的签名。不过我想我已经使他相信，那不过是少年人的一时胡闹，至今我由衷追悔。”艾勒里神色凝重地点了点头。

===Original===
“I wandered about in the South American States. I found I could not settle down to any definite occupation, and after a time I got mixed up with a pretty little revolution. Partly through pride, and partly because I was not carrying out the conditions my father imposed, I stopped communicating with the lawyer at Monte Video, and then I was in prison, and nearly executed for my part in the revolution which failed. When I got out, I had had enough of plots, and was only released on my agreeing to leave the country. I knew a man who was a merchant, and he gave me a job to come to England in charge of some freight for his firm which required someone to travel with it, largely bullion. It was a responsible job, though an easy one, and with a strong letter of recommendation from the firm I got a position in the London office, where my knowledge of the other end was useful. I had intended to go to the old man, and tell him the whole thing, but it savoured too much of the prodigal son, and I delayed doing so. I soon got sick of the office work, and as I had always had a taste for detective stories, I got the idea that I would try and get into Scotland Yard. My father was not then Home Secretary, or I would not have risked a chance meeting. As Sinclair here knows I got in as a clerk on the recommendation of my good merchants. Only the head of the firm knew where I had come from, and he died soon after, so my past was hidden.

===Chinese===
“我在南美诸国间流浪。我发觉自己定不下心做一样正经营生，过了一阵，卷进了一场小小的革命。一半出于自尊，一半因为我没有遵行父亲开出的条件，我断了同蒙得维的亚那位律师的联系，随后便下了狱，还因参与那场失败的革命险些被处决。出来之后，我对密谋造反这类事已经受够了，而且只有答应离开那个国家，才得了释放。我认识一位经商的先生，他给了我一件差事：护送他商行的一批货来英国，这批货得有人随行押运，大半是金银锭。差事虽轻省，责任却不小；凭着商行一封有力的荐书，我在伦敦分号谋到一个职位，我对货物那一头的底细正好用得上。我原打算去见老人家，把一切原原本本告诉他，可那太有浪子回头的味道，我便一拖再拖。不久我就腻烦了案牍生涯，而我素来喜好侦探故事，便起了个念头，想设法进苏格兰场。那时我父亲还不是内政大臣，否则我可不敢去冒狭路相逢的风险。这里的辛克莱知道，我是靠我那几位好心的商人荐举，进去当了一名文书。只有商行老板一人知道我的来历，他不久便去世了，我的过去就此无人知晓。

===Original===
“I believe I discharged my duties satisfactorily, and was promoted to be right-hand man to Superintendent Sinclair. I might even have become an Inspector in time.

“All this time I had held no communication with my father or sister, though I couldn’t resist the temptation of coming down here and looking at the old place, and saw them both without being seen. This was only three weeks before the death of my father.” He paused and steadied his voice. “It got on my nerves and I had almost made up my mind to come and tell him the whole story. On the very day of the terrible occurrence, I had made up my mind to go to him, and as you know now, I had spoken aloud on the subject. I went out with the intention of doing so, but wandered round in a state of uncertainty, and then returned. Would to God I had gone!”

===Chinese===
“我相信自己的差事当得还算不坏，后来升作辛克莱警司的左膀右臂。假以时日，说不定还能当上巡官。

“这些年里，我同父亲和妹妹一直音信不通，只是忍不住偷偷下来一趟，看看老宅子，还望见了他们父女俩，自己却没露面。那不过是父亲遇害前三星期的事。”他顿了顿，稳住嗓音。“这事搅得我心神不宁，我几乎已拿定主意，要把全部实情讲给他听。就在那桩可怕的事发生的当天，我下了决心要去见他，而且你们如今已经知道，我还把这话大声说了出来。我出了门，本意就是去那里，却又心神不定地绕了一圈，转了回来。上帝啊，要是那天去了该有多好！”

===Original===
“Wait a moment,” said Sinclair. “Then you were not the man who called on Sir James on that afternoon?”

“No; I never saw him again,” he answered sadly.

===Chinese===
“且慢，”辛克莱说，“那么案发那天下午登门拜访詹姆斯爵士的，并不是你？”

“不是。我从此再没见过他。”他黯然答道。

===Original===
“You can imagine the awful shock I had when you sent for me and told me of the telephone message, although I hoped it was, as you thought, a hoax. When you asked me to go into the house, I was so upset I felt I could not do so, and made an excuse. I wandered out in a terrible state of anxiety till the evening papers came out with an account of the affair. I didn’t know what to do, but I felt I must get in touch with Mabel and declare myself. I was really half off my head, and so I settled up at my old lodgings and left London. I did not go back to the office, as already I had the idea of disappearing as Lewis and coming back as myself. I suppose it was pride. I thought somehow it would not look very well that I had been a clerk in Scotland Yard. Then next morning the papers came out, and I saw to my horror that I was ‘wanted’ in connection with the crime. You can imagine my feelings. I could see the frightful construction that would be placed on my actions﻿—the most awful accusation that can be brought against any man.

===Chinese===
“你们可以想见，后来你打发人叫我去，把那通电话的事告诉我时，我受了多大震动——尽管我当时还指望，那正如你所想，不过是场恶作剧。你要我进那宅子去，我心乱如麻，实在迈不进那道门，便找了个托辞。我怀着极度的焦灼在外面游荡，直到晚报把案情的报道登出来。我六神无主，只觉得必须同梅布尔联络上，表明身份。我真是半疯了，于是退掉老住处的账目，离开了伦敦。我没有回办公室，因为那时我已打定主意：以刘易斯的身份销声匿迹，再以本来面目重新露面。我想那是自尊心作祟。我总觉得，自己当过苏格兰场的文书，这情形传出去不大好看。第二天早晨，报纸出来了，我惊骇地发现自己竟因这桩案子遭到通缉。你们想得到我是什么滋味。我眼看人们会把何等可怕的罪名扣到我的一举一动上——那是天下加诸一人之身最可怕的指控。

===Original===
“It was dreadful. Of course I knew I could clear myself, but there would be that nasty suspicion which always hangs round a man who has been accused and that damnable saying ‘there is no smoke without a fire.’ I had arrived the night before, and not wishing to go to the house at once, I had spent the night in the old tower near the chapel, where I used to go birds-nesting when I was a youngster. I came on here in the early hours of the morning, and roused Mabel up. She came down and let me in, and we had a very long talk. We could not decide what to do. It seemed difficult for me to get out of the country, and equally difficult to stay.

“Mabel had to go up to the inquest, and there was no one to advise us. So we decided to take Allery into our confidence. He came down here and we told him the whole story.”

===Chinese===
“那阵子真是可怕。我当然知道自己能洗刷干净，可是那种黏在人身上甩不脱的恶名，会一辈子缠着受过指控的人，还有那句可恶的鬼话：‘无风不起浪’。我头天夜里到的，不想立刻进宅子，便在小礼拜堂旁的古塔里过了一夜——小时候我常到那里掏鸟窝。凌晨时分我才到宅子来，唤醒了梅布尔。她下楼放我进去，我们长谈了许久，却议不出个所以然。我要逃出国去似乎难，留下来同样难。

“梅布尔得上伦敦去出席验尸审讯，我们又没有个可以讨主意的人。于是我们决定把艾勒里拉进来，对他推心置腹。他到这儿来，我们把事情原原本本讲给他听。”

===Original===
All eyes were turned on the old lawyer.

“Yes,” he said. “They dragged me into it, and I had to give advice. I was quite convinced that our young friend had nothing to do with the crime. There was nothing criminal in his remaining in hiding. If he could have got away to Monte Video, he could have come back from there in the ordinary course and established his identity. But, in the meantime, he had better wait till the police had got hold of the real man, and then there would be no case against him. It was hard to decide what to do, but the publicity was what we all wanted to avoid.”

===Chinese===
众人的目光一齐转向老律师。

“是的，”他说，“是他们把我拽进来的，我只得出主意。我完全相信，我们这位年轻朋友与罪案毫无干系。他藏匿不出，本身算不得犯法。倘若他能脱身去蒙得维的亚，日后循着正常途径从那边回来，再确立自己的身份就是了。但在那之前，他最好等警方拿到真凶，到那时便再没有官司可打。怎么办才妥当，实在难断，不过我们大家想避开的无非是满城风雨。”

===Original===
“We arranged,” continued Watson, “for me to stay in the old attic over the hall door; there are no secret chambers or anything of that sort in the house. I just lived as best I could while Mabel was in London. Then you all came down here to stay. That made matters easier in a way, as Mabel and Allery could both come and see me. We hit on the rather absurd scheme of my dressing up in my father’s court dress and with a wig and false moustache. We thought, if by any chance I were seen, it would be thought to be the ghost of Sir James. We thought that would be the lesser evil.”

===Chinese===
“我们商量妥当，”沃森接着说，“让我住进大门顶上那间老阁楼；这宅子里没有什么密室暗道之类的构造。梅布尔在伦敦的日子，我就那么将就着过。后来你们诸位下来小住。这倒多少方便了些，梅布尔和艾勒里都能来看我。我们想出了那个颇为荒唐的计策：我穿上父亲的宫廷礼服，戴上假发和假髭须。我们盘算着，万一撞见我，人家只当是詹姆斯爵士的亡灵。两害相权，这总还是轻些的那个。”

===Original===
“So then it was you that old John saw in this room?” said Sanders.

“Yes, and I would have taken him into our confidence, but he fled like a madman. I don’t think he knew where he was. After that we thought it was too risky for me to remain in the house, and I was in Mabel’s room all ready to go, when we heard noises downstairs, and sounds of talking, so I had to take cover again.”

“So it was you who were talking to Mabel that night?” said Sanders before he realised what he had said.

===Chinese===
“那么老约翰在这间屋里瞧见的就是你了？”桑德斯说。

“是的。我本想连他也拉进圈内，可他疯子似的逃了出去，我看他都认不清自己身在何处。打那以后，我们觉得我再留在宅子里未免太险。那天我已在梅布尔房里收拾停当，正要动身，忽听得楼下响动，还有人语声，只好重新躲起来。”

“那么那天夜里在房里同梅布尔说话的就是你了？”桑德斯脱口而出，话出了口才醒悟过来。

===Original===
“What do you mean?” said Mabel, sharply.

He was crimson with shame and could hardly meet her eyes.

“Only that I heard someone talking in your room with you, and came down here and﻿—met Collins.”

===Chinese===
“你什么意思？”梅布尔厉声说。

他羞得满脸通红，几乎不敢接触她的目光。

“我只是说，我听见有人在你房里同你说话，便下楼到这儿来——撞见了科林斯。”

===Original===
Mabel’s eyes glittered ominously. “I think you had better explain yourself,” she said.

“Oh, please, don’t ask me. Well, if you must know, I accused Mr. Collins of having been﻿—with you, and we had heated words over it. There, that’s the truth, and you must think what you will of me,” and he moved his hands in a gesture of despair.

They all looked at Collins.

===Chinese===
梅布尔的眼睛闪着不祥的光。“我看你最好把话讲明白。”她说。

“哦，求你，别问了。好吧，你一定要知道的话——我指摘科林斯先生那晚跟——跟你在一起，为这个我们还恶言相向了一场。就是这么回事，这就是实话，你怎么看我都可以。”说着他做了个绝望的手势。

众人都望着科林斯。

===Original===
“I would rather not say anything about the matter, but since we are all giving explanations, I found Sanders in here with a loaded revolver waiting to shoot me. I was able to prove to him that it was not I that he had heard, and that his suspicions were groundless and unworthy. That is all.”

“How horrible,” said Mabel, clenching her teeth.

“Oh, I know,” said Sanders, “I was a brute and Collins here acted like a gentleman. I had better go.”

===Chinese===
“这件事我本不愿多讲。不过既然大家都在摆事实，那晚我在这间屋里撞见桑德斯，他握着一支上了膛的左轮枪等着打死我。我向他证明了，他听见的人并非我，他的猜疑既无根据，又不光彩。如此而已。”

“真是骇人。”梅布尔咬紧牙关说。

“哦，我知道，”桑德斯说，“我是个畜生，科林斯倒表现得像个正人君子。我最好还是走吧。”

===Original===
“Please stay where you are﻿—for the present,” said Mabel. “Since you have acted like this I will also say something. Mr. Collins knew that you had called on Sir James on the very afternoon of the crime, and he said nothing about it, because he was certain that you were not the criminal, or connected with the crime. He could have ruined you if he had liked to tell what he knew. And you repay him with vile suspicions. Now you will wait to hear the end.”

===Chinese===
“请待在原地别动——暂时先别动，”梅布尔说，“既然你做出了这种事，我也说一句。科林斯先生早知道案发当天下午去拜访詹姆斯爵士的正是你，他却只字不提，因为他确信你不是凶手，同这桩罪案也毫无牵连。他只要肯把知道的说出去，就能毁了你。而你就这样报答他——用卑劣的猜疑。现在，你得留下来听完结尾。”

===Original===
There was an awkward silence for a few minutes. Sinclair looked unasked questions at Collins.

“I am sorry Miss Watson has mentioned this, but since it is out I rely on you, gentlemen, to keep it to yourselves. I picked up a card on the floor of the library which Sanders had dropped. Mrs. Simmons knew all about this visit.”

“Oh, so that’s it, is it?” said Sinclair. “I begin to see. That’s what she was keeping back in her evidence. That’s what you had up your sleeve.”

===Chinese===
一时间众人尴尬地沉默着。辛克莱望着科林斯，眼里净是没问出口的疑问。

“沃森小姐把这件事挑明了，我很遗憾。不过既然已经出口，就仰仗诸位先生把它烂在各自肚里。我在书房地板上捡到过一张名片，是桑德斯失落下的。那次来访，西蒙斯太太知道得一清二楚。”

“哦，原来是这么回事，对吧？”辛克莱说，“我开始明白了。她作证时瞒住的就是这个。这就是你袖中藏着的牌。”

===Original===
“I thought it would not be fair to tell you this at the time, as you would be bound to use it officially. If it had led to anything, of course I would have told you; when I was satisfied in my own mind that Sanders had nothing to do with the crime.” Sanders squirmed with shame. “I decided to tell no one.”

“That’s why you told Miss Watson, I suppose,” Sanders blurted out.

“You wrong Mr. Collins, he only told me when we had got at cross-purposes. When he told me there might be suspicion on someone dear to me I thought he meant Ronald. He treated you most generously all through,” she said, coldly.

===Chinese===
“我当时觉得，这件事若告诉你并不公平，因为你身为官员，势必把它用在公务上。倘若它真牵出什么来，我当然会告诉你——总之，等到我自己确信桑德斯与罪案毫无干系。”桑德斯羞得坐立不安。“我便决定谁也不告诉。”

“所以我猜，你就是因为这个才告诉了沃森小姐。”桑德斯脱口而出。

“你冤枉科林斯先生了。他是等到我们彼此闹了误会，才把这事告诉我。他对我说，我亲近的人可能受到怀疑，我当时还以为他指的是罗纳德。他自始至终待你都宽厚之至。”她冷冷地说。

===Original===
“We are rather wandering from the main story,” said Allery, to pour oil on the water. “Let’s hear the rest, Watson.”

“There is little more to tell. We decided that I must get out of the house, and I thought of the old tower. Allery here had arranged for a motor boat to take me to Bristol, and I was going on board a tramp bound for Rio. I was going to answer the advertisement from there and return. Tomorrow I should have been off, but our friend here,” he pointed to Collins, “seems to have been one too much for us, and came in the nick of time.”

===Chinese===
“我们未免离题太远了，”艾勒里说着打圆场，“往下讲吧，沃森。”

“要讲的不多了。我们议定我必须搬出宅子，我想到了那座古塔。这里的艾勒里安排了一条汽艇送我去布里斯托尔，我打算再搭上一条开往里约的不定期货轮，从那边应那则广告的征，然后回来。明天我就该动身了，可是我们这位朋友——”他指向科林斯，“看来终究棋高一着，恰恰赶在这节骨眼上到了。”

===Original===
Collins gave a laugh. “I see you all want to know my part in the matter. I am afraid there is no mystery about it, and no great skill. I will confess that when I found the visiting card on the floor I thought it was a possible clue, and worth following up. But when I became acquainted with Sanders I realised that it was a mere coincidence.” He bowed slightly to Sanders with a politeness which was almost a mockery, and Sanders looked hatred at him. Every word was putting him in a position of humiliation and Collins as the hero of the piece, and it was gall to him.

Collins continued.

===Chinese===
科林斯笑了一声。“我看你们都想知道我在这件事里的份量。恐怕其中并无奥秘，也谈不上什么高明手段。我承认，在地板上发现那张名片时，我当它是一条可能的线索，值得追一追。可等结识了桑德斯，我便明白那纯属巧合。”他朝桑德斯微微一躬，那份客气几乎近于揶揄；桑德斯向他投去憎恨的目光。这番话字字都把他钉在羞辱里，反把科林斯衬成这一场里的英雄，他心里如吞苦胆。

科林斯接着说了下去。

===Original===
“The first thing which put me on the track was the fact that no news had been heard of Lewis. I was certain that he had nothing to do with the crime, and yet he had disappeared. His confusion when asked to go to Leveson Square was taken to be a sign of guilt, but if there was no guilt there must be another explanation. He had not fled instantly. He had gone back to his lodgings, and paid his bill, and then gone. There was, therefore, I argued, another reason for going. It must be connected in some way with Sir James, especially when he had said that he was going there on that afternoon. There was a hue and cry out for him, and he had not come forward to clear himself. Then he must have a reason beyond the mere shrinking from publicity.

“Then it is difficult for a man without friends, as he appeared to be, to disappear entirely, as he seemed to have done.

“If it was anything to do with Sir James, there might conceivably be a clue at this house. On the first night I was here as the sun was setting a ray shone on that portrait of Sir James, and I saw Lewis looking at me.”

===Chinese===
“头一件把我引上这条线索的，是刘易斯杳无音讯这件事。我确信他与罪案无关，人却不见了。他被叫去勒维森广场时的慌乱失态，被众人当作了心虚的凭据；可他若问心无愧，就必定另有解释。他并没有即刻出逃。他回过住处，付清账目，这才走人。因此我断言，他出走另有缘故，而且必定与詹姆斯爵士有某种牵连——何况他自己说过，那天下午正要去那里。通缉令已经出了，他却不露面洗刷自己，那么他的缘由，必不止于不敢抛头露面这一层。

“再说，一个看似举目无亲的人，要像他这样消失得无影无踪，谈何容易。

“倘若事情同詹姆斯爵士相干，这座宅子里说不定就藏着线索。我到此地的头一晚，夕阳西沉，一道余晖正照在詹姆斯爵士那幅画像上，我看见刘易斯在望着我。”

===Original===
“What do you mean?” said Mabel, startled.

“I mean the likeness was unmistakable. The light was peculiar, and the eyes, nose and forehead were all that showed up, and there was the striking family likeness.”

===Chinese===
“你这是什么意思？”梅布尔吃了一惊。

“我是说，那份相似一望而知。当时光线奇特，画面上凸显出来的只有眼睛、鼻子和额头，而那种惊人的家族相似就摆在眼前。”

===Original===
“I saw the expression on your face,” said Allery, “and it made me uneasy.”

“And I saw the look that passed between you and Miss Mabel,” said Collins with a laugh, “and it confirmed my suspicions.”

===Chinese===
“我看见了你当时的神情，”艾勒里说，“看得我心里直发毛。”

“我也看见了您和梅布尔小姐之间交换的那个眼色，”科林斯笑着说，“那更坐实了我的怀疑。”

===Original===
“Then the ghost, of course, was as clear as daylight to me. The dress was a good idea, if I may say so. I found the marks of real feet on the hearthrug. When I went from here I did not go back to London, but kept watch. I thought I had got you,” he said turning to Watson, “when the car left here secretly at night; but you were too cunning, there was only Allery and Miss Mabel. I suppose that was your doing, Allery?”

===Chinese===
“至于那个幽灵，在我眼里当然一清二楚。恕我直言，那身礼服是个好主意。我在炉前地毯上找到了真人的脚印。我离开此地后没有回伦敦，而是一直守望。夜里那辆汽车悄悄驶出宅门时，我以为已经拿住你了，”他转向沃森说，“可你们太狡猾，车上只有艾勒里和梅布尔小姐。那是你安排的吧，艾勒里？”

===Original===
Allery burst out laughing. “We did you there. We had no idea that anyone was watching, of course, but we had to take precautions, so Mabel fetched the car from the garage, and Watson was waiting there. He was on the floor of the car, covered with a rug, and I came out of the front door and joined them. It was in case any servants were about. We didn’t want another ghost episode. But how did you pick up the clue?”

===Chinese===
艾勒里放声大笑。“那一手确实把你骗过了。我们自然不知道有人盯梢，但不得不加小心，于是梅布尔去车房把车取出来，沃森在那里候着。他趴在车厢底板上，身上盖一条毯子，我从前门出来同他们会合。那是防着撞上仆人。我们可不想再闹一回幽灵。不过，你是怎么摸到这条线索的？”

===Original===
“I hung around. The night was too wet to follow the tracks of the car, but twice I saw you going on the road to Wilton in the evening, and each time I was further along the road, and on the second occasion was able to follow up, and saw the car at the bottom of the hill. I was certain Lewis, or Sir Ronald, as I now thought it was, was hiding in the neighbourhood, and knew a search would simply give the game away.

“So I came down here and watched, and finally saw you coming down the hill. I had found out about this strange service, and calculated that if a lonely man wanted to come down he would take the chance when a lot of others were coming.”

===Chinese===
“我就在这一带盘桓。那晚雨太大了，没法跟车辙，可我两次在傍晚望见你们行驶在去滨海威尔顿的路上，每次我都已守在前头更远的路段，第二回便跟了上去，看见汽车停在山脚下。我确信刘易斯——或者说，我此时认定的罗纳德爵士——就藏在左近，也明白一搜山，反倒会泄露天机。

“于是我下山来守候，终于看见你从山上下来。我打听到了这场古怪的礼拜，盘算着：一个怕孤单露面的人要下山，多半会趁大队人马一齐下来的时候。”

===Original===
“Very clever,” said Allery.

“I think that explains all there is to explain. But how did you get on the track, Sinclair?”

“I had my suspicions, but pardon me, I am not a freelance like you are, but an official, and you must allow me to keep my secrets.”

===Chinese===
“真是高明。”艾勒里说。

“我想，该解释的就是这些了。不过你自己是怎么追上这条线索的，辛克莱？”

“我自有我的疑心。只是恕我直言，我不像你是个无拘无束的自由人，我是吃官饭的，你得容我保有自己的秘密。”

===Original===
“Of course,” said Collins, carelessly.

“What I don’t understand,” said Allery, “is, why you were so keen on finding out the whole thing?”

“Blackmail, I should think,” said Sanders.

===Chinese===
“那是自然。”科林斯随口说道。

“我不明白的是，”艾勒里说，“你为什么这样起劲，非要把事情查个水落石出？”

“我猜是为了敲诈吧。”桑德斯说。

===Original===
Mabel gave him a look of contempt, and the rest ignored him.

“I really couldn’t tell you. I suppose a problem always fascinated me, and then I thought I might be of some use, perhaps,” and he glanced at Mabel.

“You have certainly cleared up the mystery,” said Allery.

===Chinese===
梅布尔鄙夷地瞪了他一眼，其余的人只当他不存在。

“我实在说不上来。大概谜题天生使我着迷；再说，我又想，自己或许还能派上点用场。”说着他瞟了梅布尔一眼。

“你确实把谜团解开了。”艾勒里说。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Sinclair, “but we are no nearer to the question of the murder.”

“I thought they had got the man,” said Mabel in surprise.

“They think they have,” said Sinclair.

===Chinese===
“是啊，”辛克莱说，“可是对凶案本身，我们并没有更接近一步。”

“我还以为他们抓到凶手了呢。”梅布尔惊讶地说。

“他们自以为抓到了。”辛克莱说。

===Original===
“I suppose it will remain one of those unsolved mysteries.”

“By the way, Sir Ronald,” said Sinclair, starting up, “can I have a word with you, I shan’t keep you a minute?”

“Oh, really, Mr. Sinclair, I think he has had enough for this evening,” said Mabel.

===Chinese===
“看来这案子只能成为又一桩悬案了。”

“对了，罗纳德爵士，”辛克莱猝然开口，“我能同你说句话吗？耽搁不了你一分钟。”

“哎呀，说真的，辛克莱先生，我想他今晚已经够受了。”梅布尔说。

===Original===
“I am afraid the matter is very urgent,” insisted the other.

Something in his manner irritated Allery. “Mr. Sinclair,” he said, “you must remember, please, you are a guest here, and not on official duty. Are you not satisfied with the explanation you have heard?”

Collins interposed. “Don’t you think you ought to tell old John and the servants that you have come home? You know how servants gossip.”

===Chinese===
“恐怕这件事非常紧急。”对方执意说道。

他那副神气里有种东西惹恼了艾勒里。“辛克莱先生，”他说，“请您务必记住，您在这里是客人，不是在办官差。方才听到的这番解释，您还不满意吗？”

科林斯插进来解围。“你不想想，是不是该告诉老约翰和仆人们，说你回家了？你是知道的，仆人最会嚼舌头。”

===Original===
“Of course,” said Watson, “I must do that at once. Don’t disturb yourselves, we don’t want any solemn announcement. I will just go and tell him, in an offhand way. The old fellow will be very pleased.”

He got up and went out. Sinclair bit his lip.

There was a silence when he had gone out.

===Chinese===
“当然，”沃森说，“我这就去办。各位都别起身，咱们不必搞得郑重其事。我就这么随随便便地去告诉他一声。老人家准会高兴得很。”

他起身出去了。辛克莱咬了咬嘴唇。

他出去后，屋里静了片刻。

===Original===
Allery cleared his throat.

“Mr. Sinclair,” he said, “this brings about an awkward situation. We are rather in your hands. Your department is searching for Lewis. You have﻿—if I may say so﻿—unofficially found him. The question is, if you are satisfied that he has nothing to do with the crime, will you consent for us to carry out our plan and let him go, as we had arranged.”

“That places me in a curious position. I can’t give an answer offhand. I must think it over.”

===Chinese===
艾勒里清了清嗓子。

“辛克莱先生，”他说，“这就造成了一个为难的处境。我们多少攥在您手里了。您的部门正在通缉刘易斯，而您——恕我直言——已经以非官方的身份找到了他。问题是：倘若您确信他与罪案无涉，可否成全我们的计划，让我们按原先的安排放他走？”

“这可把我摆在一个古怪的处境里了。我没法脱口作答，得琢磨琢磨。”

===Original===
“Take your time,” said Collins pleasantly. “Have a whiskey and soda?” He rose from the table and went to the sideboard.

“Bother, there’s none here. Miss Mabel, may I act as butler and fetch some, we don’t want to send for old John just now?”

“Certainly,” she said with a smile.

===Chinese===
“不急，慢慢想，”科林斯和颜悦色地说，“来杯威士忌苏打？”他从桌边起身，走向餐具柜。

“嗐，这儿没有。梅布尔小姐，让我权且充一回管家去取些来，可好？这会儿就不必叫老约翰了吧？”

“当然可以。”她微笑着说。

===Original===
He went out.

Sanders spoke for the first time since his gross insinuation.

“I will go. After what has happened you will not want me to remain.”

“That is a matter for you to decide,” said Mabel stiffly.

===Chinese===
他出去了。

自那句露骨的恶语之后，桑德斯头一回开口。

“我走吧。出了这样的事，你们不会想让我留下的。”

“那是你自己决定的事。”梅布尔冷冷地说。

===Original===
“Oh, that’s all nonsense,” said Allery, “you two are not going to let this business upset you. It only needs an apology, and I am sure Sanders will give that. He was upset, and perhaps a little jealous,” he added.

Sanders coloured.

“Your remark is in doubtful taste,” he said. “I don’t think any apology would serve in a case like this.” He rose.

===Chinese===
“哦，那全是胡说，”艾勒里说，“你们两个总不至于为这点事闹掰。只消一句道歉就完了，我担保桑德斯赔得起这个不是。他当时心绪不佳，兴许还有几分醋意。”他补充道。

桑德斯涨红了脸。

“您这话未免有失分寸，”他说，“这样的情形，我看什么道歉都无济于事。”他站起身来。

===Original===
Sinclair put up his hand. “Stop,” he said firmly, “I may want you.”

Sanders turned from red to white. “What do you mean?” he said.

“I have no warrant, and cannot make any arrest. But it would be advisable if you did not leave this house.”

“This is about the last straw,” said Sanders, and sank into his chair.

===Chinese===
辛克莱抬起手。“站住，”他语气坚定地说，“我也许用得着你。”

桑德斯的脸由红转白。“你什么意思？”他说。

“我没有逮捕令，不能逮捕任何人。不过，你最好不要离开这座宅子。”

“这真是忍无可忍了。”桑德斯说着，颓然跌坐回椅子里。

## XVI. The Criminal Found / 第十六章 真凶寻获

===Original===
“He’s a long time getting that whiskey,” said Allery.
“I expect he’s having a talk with John and my brother,” said Mabel. Sinclair sprang forward in his chair.
“Good God,” he shouted, and without waiting for leave, he rang the bell, keeping his hand on the button in his excitement.
A maid answered the door.

===Chinese===
“他去拿威士忌，怎么这么半天还不回来。”艾勒里说。
“我猜他在跟约翰和我哥哥聊天呢。”梅布尔说。辛克莱猛地从椅子上直起身来。
“天哪！”他失声叫道，也不等人许可便伸手按铃，激动得手一直抵在按钮上不放。
一个女仆前来应门。

===Original===
“Where is Mr. Collins?” said he at once.
The maid looked her surprise. She was not used to being spoken to like that.
“Mr. Collins and Sir Ronald have gone out for a run in his car, sir,” she replied stiffly. “Sir Ronald left a message that they might not be back for some time.”
“Why didn’t you come and tell us, Mary?” said Mabel quietly. She also resented Sinclair’s interference.

===Chinese===
“科林斯先生在哪儿？”他劈头就问。
女仆一脸惊讶。她可不习惯被人这样呼来喝去。
“科林斯先生和罗纳德爵士坐车出去兜风了，先生。”她生硬地答道，“罗纳德爵士留了话，说他们一时半会儿怕是回不来。”
“那你怎么不来告诉我们一声，玛丽？”梅布尔轻声说。她对辛克莱的多事也同样心存不满。

===Original===
“He said it would do later on as you were busy, Miss,” and she looked surprised.
Allery was quick to notice it, and said:﻿—
“All right, Mary, thank you. That’s quite all right.”
The maid went out. Sinclair’s face was ashy. He buried his face in his hands.

===Chinese===
“他说诸位正忙着，过后再告诉也一样，小姐。”她显出吃惊的样子。
艾勒里立刻察觉了，便说道——
“好了，玛丽，谢谢你。没你的事了。”
女仆退了出去。辛克莱面如死灰，双手掩住了脸。

===Original===
“What’s the matter, man?” said Allery sternly.
“Matter, he’s gone to his death,” said Sinclair.
For a moment there was a silence tense and vital. It seemed as though the horror of these weeks had come into the room in a living form. Powers of evil gathered round.
The shadows deepened at the word. There was utter and crushing conviction in the tones.

===Chinese===
“怎么回事，老兄？”艾勒里厉声问。
“怎么回事——他是去送死啊。”辛克莱说。
一时间，屋里静得令人窒息，静得生死攸关。仿佛这几个星期以来的恐怖已化作活物，进了这间屋子。邪恶的势力正四下围拢。
这句话一出口，屋里的阴影仿佛更深了几分。那语气里透着彻头彻尾、压倒一切的笃定。

===Original===
Mabel was the first to grasp the meaning, and a flush mounted to her face. “Do you suggest that my brother, after all you have heard, is a murderer?” she said in a passionate voice.
“No, not for a moment,” said Sinclair, “but before the morning comes he will be murdered, as his father was murdered, and by the same hand.”
“You mean?” said Allery, leaning forward.
“Exactly. Collins, the cleverest scoundrel we have had for a generation.”

===Chinese===
梅布尔第一个会过意来，一股热血涌上她的脸。“你是说，听过了方才那些话，我哥哥竟然还是个杀人凶手？”她激愤地说。
“不，一刻也没有，”辛克莱说，“可是不等天亮，他就会遇害，同他父亲一样遇害，而且死在同一只手上。”
“你是说——？”艾勒里俯身向前。
“正是。科林斯——我们这一代里最狡猾的恶棍。”

===Original===
“Mr. Collins,” said Mabel, and there was pain in the voice. “I will never believe that. You must be mad.”
Allery rose with a grim look on his face. “You have said too little or too much. We must know what you mean.”
The temporary numbness passed from Sinclair and he was the man of action.
“Yes, yes, of course, everything, but not now. We must act at once. You must trust to my word. I shall want all your help. First, have you a car, and a driver?”

===Chinese===
“科林斯先生，”梅布尔的声音里透着痛楚，“我永远不会相信这种话。你一定是疯了。”
艾勒里脸上带着冷峻的神色，站起身来。“你这话不是说得太少，就是说得太多。我们必须弄清楚你的意思。”
辛克莱那一阵木然过去了，他又成了雷厉风行的人。
“是，是，当然，一切都会说明白，但不是现在。我们必须立刻行动。你们得相信我的话。我少不了要请大家相助。首先——你们有汽车吗？有司机吗？”

===Original===
His energy infected the others.
“There’s our car,” said Mabel.
“Then get it ready at once,” said Sinclair abruptly. “Ask questions afterwards.” Sanders, who had not said a word after his last rebuff, sprang to his feet. “I’ll go and see to it.”
“Good. Can you drive? No, the chauffeur must do that. I will go, no, no, that won’t do, I must telephone.”

===Chinese===
他的劲头感染了众人。
“我们有一辆。”梅布尔说。
“那就马上去备车。”辛克莱急促地说，“要问的回头再问。”桑德斯自上次碰了钉子后一直一言未发，此刻一跃而起：“我去办。”
“好。你会开车吗？不行，还得司机来开。我去——不，不，那也不行，我得打电话。”

===Original===
“I’ll go,” said Sanders quietly. “If you’ll tell me where to go.”
Sinclair looked doubtful. “It’s dangerous your dealing with a man who will stick at nothing. Have you a revolver?”
“Yes,” said Sanders blushing at the recollection which it conjured up, of the night he had unworthy suspicions of Mabel.
“Can you shoot? You are dealing with a crack shot.”

===Chinese===
“我去。”桑德斯平静地说，“只要您告诉我去哪儿。”
辛克莱面有疑色。“你要对付的是个无所忌惮的人，这很危险。你有左轮手枪吗？”
“有。”桑德斯答道，脸不由红了——这话勾起了那段回忆：那天夜里，他曾对梅布尔起过不光彩的疑心。
“枪法怎么样？你要打交道的可是个神枪手。”

===Original===
“I’m pretty useful,” said the other.
“Then go.” Sanders went without a word.
“The telephone, in the hall isn’t it?” he rushed out and seized the instrument. “Hullo, Trunks. Scotland Yard priority call. Superintendent Sinclair,” he said, and hung up the receiver.
Sanders came in breathless. “We can’t get the car to start,” he said.

===Chinese===
“还过得去。”对方说。
“那就去吧。”桑德斯二话没说就走了。
“电话在门厅里，是吧？”他冲了出去，抓起话筒。“喂，总机。苏格兰场优先通话，辛克莱警司。”说完他便挂上了听筒。
桑德斯气喘吁吁地跑了进来。“汽车发动不起来。”他说。

===Original===
“Ah, I feared as much, he’s tampered with it,” said Sinclair.
He seized the telephone, “Hullo that call coming through?﻿—”
“All right; give me the police station at Wilton in the meantime﻿—urgent.”
He waited a moment with the receiver in his hand. “Hullo, Superintendent Sinclair here. That you, Miles?﻿—good. Get the best car at once, and come here with two men. Armed, you understand. I take full responsibility. Must ring off, I’m waiting for the Yard.”

===Chinese===
“啊，我就怕这一手——他做了手脚。”辛克莱说。
他抓起电话：“喂，接通了吗？——”
“好吧；先给我接滨海威尔顿的警察局——急事。”
他拿着听筒等了片刻。“喂，我是辛克莱警司。是迈尔斯吗？——好。马上用你们最好的车，带两个人赶到这里来。带上枪，明白吗。一切责任我担。得挂了，我还在等苏格兰场的电话。”

===Original===
The bell tinkled, and he got through.
He issued rapid orders.
When he put up the receiver, he turned to the others.
“There’s one point in our favour. He doesn’t know he’s suspected. He has always regarded everyone as a fool. But he’s a clever rogue if ever there was one, and he’ll take some catching.”

===Chinese===
电话铃叮铃一响，他要的电话接通了。
他快速下达了一连串命令。
放下听筒后，他转向众人。
“有一点对我们有利：他不知道自己已被怀疑。他一向把所有人都当成傻瓜。不过，他若是算得上狡猾，那真是狡猾到了家，要抓住他谈何容易。”

===Original===
“But my brother,” said Mabel, “you said he was in danger.”
“I am so sorry, Miss Watson. I was so upset for the moment. There is no immediate danger. In fact, his one chance lies with keeping your brother safe, for the moment, but his life is in danger. I am bound to tell you that I have given orders for the house in Leveson Square to be watched, but it may not be there.”
“What may not be there?” said Allery; “we are all in the dark.”
“I know. I will explain, but there’s no time now.”

===Chinese===
“可是我哥哥，”梅布尔说，“你方才说他有危险。”
“实在抱歉，沃森小姐。我方才一时心绪不宁。眼下还没有迫在眉睫的危险。事实上，那人唯一的机会，就在于暂时保全你哥哥；可你哥哥的性命，终究悬在险境。我必须告诉您，我已下令监视勒维森广场的宅子，但事情未必出在那里。”
“什么未必在那里？”艾勒里说，“我们全都蒙在鼓里呢。”
“我知道。我会解释的，可现在没有时间。”

===Original===
“As far as I know he’s gone to London and he will not spare the car. He may not go direct in case of some such action as I have taken.”
Sanders came in.
“The car will go now,” he said. “Luckily the chauffeur had spare parts, otherwise it would have been hopeless, and there was evidently not much time to tamper with it. I’ll get off, if you tell me where to go.”
“Oh, don’t go,” said Mabel with a revulsion of feeling. “Surely the police can do all that’s necessary?”

===Chinese===
“据我所知，他到伦敦去了，而且会一路狂飙，全不惜车。他未必走直路，以防有人采取我这样的行动。”
桑德斯走了进来。
“车现在能开了。”他说，“幸亏司机那儿有备件，不然就彻底没指望了。看来他下手脚的时间不多。告诉我去哪儿，我这就动身。”
“哦，别去了。”梅布尔忽然一阵心绪翻腾，“该办的事，警察肯定都能办妥，不是吗？”

===Original===
“I’m going,” said Sanders, and his jaw set square. There was a long account to settle with this man.
“Go direct to London, as hard as you can. Don’t stop to ask questions on the way. Go to Leveson Square; here, take my card with you, and explain to the men on duty there. If you don’t catch them on the road, and I’m afraid you have a poor chance, he will have got there first. They have full instructions to act, but you must get hold of Watson, and see to his safety if you can. Good luck to you. Telephone if you can. Use my name, and they will give you priority.”
Without even looking at Mabel or saying goodbye, he went out.

===Chinese===
“我一定要去。”桑德斯说，牙关咬得紧紧的。同这个人，他还有一笔长长的旧账要清算。
“直接去伦敦，越快越好。路上别停车打听。到勒维森广场去；来，带上我的名片，拿它向那边值勤的人说明情况。如果你在路上追不上他们——恐怕希望不大——他就会抢先赶到。那边的人都接到了放手行动的完整指示，但你务必设法控制住沃森，尽力保他安全。祝你好运。有可能就打电话来。报我的名字，他们会给你优先接通。”
他连看都没看梅布尔一眼，也没道声别，径自出去了。

===Original===
“Oh, my brother and Mr. Collins! This is awful; surely there must be some ghastly mistake!”
“You must be brave,” said Allery, “and wait for Mr. Sinclair’s explanation.”
A sound was heard outside as the car shot out into the night.
“Too long a start,” said Sinclair, looking at his watch.

===Chinese===
“哦，我哥哥，还有科林斯先生！这太可怕了；这里面一定出了什么骇人的差错！”
“你要沉住气，”艾勒里说，“等着听辛克莱先生解释。”
外面传来一阵声响，汽车冲进了夜色之中。
“落后太久啦。”辛克莱看看表说。

===Original===
John had been hovering at the door, and now came forward.
“Can I be of any service, Miss?” he said.
“No, thank you, John,” said she, “except you can tell the servants to keep absolutely silent about anything that is happening. They will all know in time. Meanwhile I depend on their loyalty.”
The old man bowed. “I am sure you may depend on them, Miss; but can I get you anything?”

===Chinese===
约翰先前一直在门口转悠，这时走上前来。
“小姐，有什么要效劳的吗？”他说。
“不用了，谢谢你，约翰，”她答道，“只消嘱咐仆人们，对眼下发生的一切绝对守口如瓶。事情迟早大家都会知道。眼下我全指望他们的忠心了。”
老人躬了躬身。“小姐，您尽管放心他们；要不要给您弄点什么来？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Allery, “some strong black coffee would do us all good. I can see we are going to have a night of it.”
“Very good, sir,” said John.
The sound of a car at the door was heard. Sinclair opened it, and a policeman in plain clothes entered and saluted.
“That’s right, Miles. Our man has gone to London, at least I think so. A car has just gone in pursuit, and I have informed the Yard. The whole route will be watched and patrolled. They are also watching the house in Leveson Square. He has taken Sir Ronald Watson with him.”

===Chinese===
“要的，”艾勒里说，“给大家来些浓烈的黑咖啡，对我们都有好处。我看今晚咱们要通宵了。”
“好的，先生。”约翰说。
门口传来汽车声。辛克莱开了门，一个穿便衣的警察走了进来，举手敬礼。
“来得正好，迈尔斯。我们的人去伦敦了，至少我认为如此。刚有一辆车追下去了，我已经通知了苏格兰场。整条路线都会有人监视巡逻。勒维森广场的宅子也有人盯着。他把罗纳德·沃森爵士带在了身边。”

===Original===
The Inspector opened his eyes wide.
“Yes,” said Sinclair, “we have found Sir Ronald Watson only today.”
He issued further instructions, with a description of the car, and its occupants. He gave them the number, “but,” he said, “he’s altered that, if I know him.”
“Very good, sir,” said the Inspector, “we’ll catch him,” he added cheerfully. It was not often such a chance came in his way, and visions of promotion floated before him.

===Chinese===
督察睁大了眼睛。
“是的，”辛克莱说，“我们就是今天才找到罗纳德·沃森爵士的。”
他又下达了几道指示，说明了那辆车的特征和车上的人员。他把车牌号给了他们，“不过，”他说，“要是我没算错他的为人，这号牌他已经换过了。”
“遵命，先生，”督察说，“我们准能抓住他。”他又乐呵呵地补了一句。这样的机会可不是常落到他头上的，晋升的前景已在他眼前浮动。

===Original===
When he had sent them off, Sinclair came back to the others.
“Shall we come into the dining-room?” he said. “I must remain here where I can be found, though I would have dearly loved the chase,” he added sadly. “I don’t expect in the least they will catch him. But I must try everything.”
He sank wearily into a seat. “What a day,” he said.
The others waited.

===Chinese===
把他们打发走后，辛克莱回到了众人那里。
“咱们进餐厅去坐坐好吗？”他说，“我必须留在这儿，好让人随时找得到我，虽然我多想亲自去追啊，”他黯然补了一句，“我丝毫不指望他们能抓住他。但每一步我都必须试到。”
他疲惫地陷进座椅里。“这一天过的。”他说。
众人等待着。

===Original===
Presently he sat up.
“Of course you will want an explanation, and you are entitled to one. It will help pass the time.”
“If you are too tired we can wait,” said Mabel, but she was on the rack. Her brother, the man who had been her lover, and he who had lately filled so much of her life and whom she had grown to respect even if there was no deeper feeling, were all involved.
Sinclair started like a tired man, but as he went on he warmed to his tale.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，他坐直了身子。
“你们当然想要个解释，也理应得到一个。正好也好打发时间。”
“要是你太累，我们可以等。”梅布尔说，可她心里正受着煎熬。她的哥哥，那个曾做过她恋人的男人，还有近来充塞了她生活那么多、纵无更深的情愫也已赢得她敬重的另一个男人，全都卷了进去。
辛克莱开口时像个疲惫已极的人，可讲着讲着，他便渐入佳境，讲得有了精神。

===Original===
“Collins was a deep scoundrel, but like all such he had two weaknesses. He was so vain that he could not leave things alone, he must try daring experiments, and he regarded all mankind as fools. That was where he made his great mistake. I don’t expect we shall ever know the truth, for I am certain he will never be taken alive.
“A great amount is still obscure, in fact, it was only this evening that I was sure I was right. Otherwise, of course, I should have acted before. The first indication I had was a very slight slip, so slight that it was almost instinct that made me notice it. He was recounting Mrs. Simmons’ evidence to Boyce and myself, and he said that she had stated that Sir James had complained of feeling sleepy. Now I was sure that she had said nothing of the sort, and I took the trouble to ask her after, and she was certain he had never said so. In a curious way this kept on recurring to my mind, but I dismissed it. Mind you, I hadn’t the remotest suspicion at the time. I merely thought it curious.
“Then, of course, I had a letter from Sir James the day after the murder.”

===Chinese===
“科林斯是个城府极深的恶棍，但这一类人无一例外都有两个软肋。他虚荣心重得手痒难耐，凡事不肯罢休，非要试验些胆大包天的手法不可；他又把普天之下的人都当成傻瓜。他最大的失策正在这里。我想我们永远无法知道全部真相了，因为我确信，他绝不会让人活着抓住。
“还有许许多多环节至今不明。事实上，直到今天晚上，我才确信自己想对了。不然的话，我当然早就动手了。我得到的第一个苗头，是一次极细微的失口，细微得几乎全凭本能我才留意到。当时他正向博伊斯和我复述西蒙斯太太的证词，他说她曾交代，詹姆斯爵士抱怨过犯困。可我分明记得，她根本没说过这样的话；事后我又特意去问了她，她也肯定他从来没有这样说过。不知怎的，这件事老在我脑子里打转，可我还是把它放下了。要知道，我当时连一丝一毫的怀疑都没有，只觉得事情有点蹊跷。
“接着，命案发生的第二天，我又收到了詹姆斯爵士的一封信。”

===Original===
The other two looked at him in surprise.
“Oh, of course, you don’t know about that.” He felt in his pocket and produced the letter, which he read to them.
“Again I was struck with the fact that he had been anxious not to have Collins told. It stuck in my mind, until recalled, in a vivid manner. I was in his flat, and we were discussing the evidence of Mrs. Simmons, when he suddenly said he heard a noise, and went to the door, dramatically taking his pistol with him.
“Now, my hearing is very good, and I was almost certain that there was no one there. Outside the door he found a piece of paper with a scribbled message on it. You know what that was because it has been in the papers. We searched the flat, but there was no one there. When we came back he picked up the evidence from the floor, and was reading it. He handed it back to me, and I saw that the letter from Sir James was with the other document. I could see then that he had got hold of this by a clever ruse, but I did not think it was anything more than a trick, though I did not like it.

===Chinese===
另外两人诧异地看着他。
“哦，对了，你们不知道这件事。”他在口袋里摸了摸，取出那封信，念给他们听。
“再次让我震动的是，他生前急切叮嘱不可让科林斯知道。这件事一直横在我心里，直到后来被一幕场景鲜明地勾了起来。那天我在他的公寓里，我们正讨论西蒙斯太太的证词，他忽然说听见了动静，还煞有介事地握着手枪走到门口去。
“要知道，我的听觉极好，当时几乎可以断定外面根本没有人。他在门外发现了一张纸，上面潦草地写着字。那上面是什么，你们都知道，报纸登过。我们搜遍了整套公寓，一个人影也没有。等我们回来，他从地板上拾起那份证词读着，随后递还给我，我看见詹姆斯爵士那封信竟和其他文件夹在了一起。那时我已看出，他是耍了个巧妙的花招把信弄到手的；不过我当时只当那是他搞侦探的一记伎俩，尽管心里不痛快，倒没有多想。

===Original===
“That night I stayed at his flat, and had very little sleep.
“I went over the scene at Leveson Square as I had done many times, and then I recalled that Collins had asked me to telephone for a doctor, and remained at the door while I called the old woman. When I came back he had spread mats on the floor in order not to obscure footmarks. He examined the floor himself, and said there were three sets of footmarks, Sir James’ and the unknown man’s, and then he said with a laugh, ‘and these, I think, are mine when I stepped to lay the rug,’ and he took off his shoes, and they fitted.
“I was so astounded that I had to help myself to a whiskey and soda, in order not to show my feelings, for the marks he found were under where the rug had been. Since he had spread it from the doorway, it must have been over the marks. It may seem surprising, but in the events which followed, this had entirely gone out of my mind, but now came back with added force. It is always said in my profession that we must never eliminate any possibility, however seemingly improbable.

===Chinese===
“那天夜里我宿在他的公寓里，几乎没有合眼。
“我把勒维森广场那一幕在脑中又推演了一遍，像从前推演过的许多次一样。接着我回想起来：科林斯当时让我去打电话请医生，自己守在门口，由我上楼去叫那位老太太。等我回来时，他已在地板上铺了垫子，免得踩坏脚印。他亲自查验地板，说现场有三组脚印，一组是詹姆斯爵士的，一组是那个身份不明的人的，随后他笑着说：‘我想这一组是我的，是我进去铺地毯时留下的。’说着他脱下鞋子比对——正相吻合。
“我惊得不能不给自己倒上一杯威士忌苏打，好掩饰心绪——因为他发现的那些脚印，是在地毯原先铺着的位置底下。既然地毯是从门口一路铺进去的，那就必然早已盖住了这些脚印。说来奇怪，在后来的一连串事件中，这一点竟被我忘了个干净，此刻却加倍有力地涌了回来。我们这一行有句老话：无论一个可能性看起来多么不合情理，也永远不能把它排除。

===Original===
“More or less for idle speculation, I began to think, as I could not sleep. Facts came crowding in. Mrs. Simmons had suppressed the fact that Sanders had come in the afternoon, that I did not know at the time, but I was sure that she would not hide a murderer. If that were so, then, as we found there was no possible means of leaving the room, and she had seen him go to his study and lock the door, the murder could not have taken place then. What remained? It must have taken place after the door was opened by us. But the only person who was for a moment alone with Sir James was Collins. But the thing was absurd. We should have heard the shot. I dismissed the whole thing from my mind. But it would keep coming back. Collins had turned up at my office at the very moment, with a plausible story of having been called up on the phone. He would, of course, have been able to get official paper, and knew my signature perfectly well. Motive﻿—well, I have been too long in the service to look for motive till I have a case.
“The next day we went to Leveson Square, and the Home Office doctor mentioned that the bullet had only penetrated a little way into the skull.
“Forgive me for going into all this,” he said to Mabel.
“Go on,” she said, bravely. “I must know the truth.”

===Chinese===
“睡不着，我便由着心思漫无边际地推想。种种事实纷至沓来。西蒙斯太太隐瞒了桑德斯下午来过这件事——这我当时并不知道——可我确信，她绝不会窝藏一个凶手。倘若如此，那么，既然我们查明那房间没有任何脱身的可能，而她又亲眼看见他进了书房、锁上了门，凶案就不可能发生在那个时候。还剩下什么？只能发生在房门被我们打开之后。而那时唯一同詹姆斯爵士单独相处过片刻的，只有科林斯。可这事太荒唐了。枪响我们不可能听不见。我把这整套念头从心里赶了出去。可它偏要一次次卷土重来。科林斯偏偏就在那一刻出现在我的办公室，还带着一套言之凿凿的说辞，说是接了电话被叫去的。公函纸他自然弄得到，我的签名他更是烂熟于心。至于动机——唉，我干这一行太久了，案子没有坐实之前，从不忙着去找动机。
“第二天我们去了勒维森广场，内政部的医生提到，子弹只稍稍钻进了颅骨。
“原谅我讲得这样细。”他对梅布尔说。
“讲下去，”她勇敢地说，“我必须知道真相。”

===Original===
“I was on the alert, then, and I noticed that Collins started talking about his grievance against Boyce for letting the Press have details. As a rule, he would have picked up a point like that at once. The doctor had suggested a half charge as the explanation, but another came to my mind. An air pistol would make comparatively little noise, especially if one shouted out something at the same time. The whole appearance of Sir James was as though he was in a deep sleep. Here, again, my experience came in. I had known a similar case in India.
“The man who did the murder would have to be certain that he was asleep, or the plot would fail. There was only one way. Drugging! You might say why not poison, but here was no need, and always the risk of poison being found. A sleeping draught was all that was necessary, and then I remembered that Collins had slipped out the remark about Sir James feeling sleepy. There were two glasses which had contained whiskey, into which a sleeping draught could easily have been put.
“Things were beginning to take shape. I must confess I had suspicions of Lewis at first, but I soon dismissed that, and kept it up on purpose.”

===Chinese===
“从那时起我便留了心。我注意到，科林斯开始念叨他对博伊斯的怨气，抱怨博伊斯让报界得到了案情细节。按他素来的敏锐，这样一个要点他本该立刻抓住。医生提出用装药减半来解释，可我脑中浮起了另一种解释。气枪的声响相比之下要小得多，尤其当旁边有人同时高声喊叫的时候。而詹姆斯爵士那副模样，整个人就像沉在酣睡之中。这里又用得上我的经验了：我在印度经手过一桩相似的案子。
“行凶的人必须先确信他已睡熟，否则全盘计谋都会落空。办法只有一个：下药！你们也许要问，为什么不用毒药？可这里用不着毒药，何况毒药总有被验出来的风险。一味安眠药水就足够了。这时我便想起，科林斯曾失口提过詹姆斯爵士犯困。现场有两只装过威士忌的杯子，往里下一点安眠药水，再容易不过。
“事情渐渐显出了眉目。我坦白说，起初我疑心的是刘易斯，但很快便排除了他，只是有意还把这份疑心挂在明面上装样子。”

===Original===
He paused and helped himself to coffee.
“You must understand,” he continued, “that I had not a shred of evidence. If I had told my suspicions I should have been laughed at, and probably lost my job. Then there came the complication of Boyce and Jackson. I saw he was dead set on getting this man proved guilty, as he was sure we should never get the real man.”
“Do you mean to say he was going to get an innocent man convicted?” said Mabel, in horror.
“Not convicted. He would merely be put back in an asylum, and I can assure you it happens far oftener than the public know, that an innocent man has to suffer. The police argue that they have got hold of a man with a terrible past, and that even if he is not the actual culprit he deserves to be put away. You will find, in these cases where murder is the crime, he is always sent to penal servitude for life.”

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，给自己倒了杯咖啡。
“你们必须明白，”他接着说，“我当时手里没有一丝一毫的证据。若把怀疑说出去，只会招人耻笑，多半还会丢了差事。接着又添上了博伊斯和杰克逊这重纠葛。我看得出，他一门心思要给这人定罪，因为他笃信真凶是永远抓不到了。”
“你是说，他要让一个无辜的人被判罪？”梅布尔惊骇地说。
“倒不是判罪。那人不过是被重新送回疯人院。而且我敢向你们担保，无辜者蒙冤受难的事，远比公众所知道的要多得多。警察的逻辑是：手里抓住的是个劣迹斑斑的人，纵然不是真凶，也活该关起来。你们会看到，凡涉杀人重案的这类人，总归被判终身苦役。”

===Original===
“How dreadful,” said Mabel, “I thought our justice was so good in England.”
“I am afraid what Mr. Sinclair says is true,” said Allery. “I have never been mixed up with criminal cases, but I have heard a good deal. Please go on.”
“I saw,” said Sinclair, “that the real clue lay in the letter I had received, and it was disquieting that Collins had read it according to my supposition. The key to the situation lay in finding Sir Ronald Watson.
“I kept in touch with Collins’ movements, and soon learnt of his visits here. I thought he was here for another reason,” and he glanced awkwardly at Mabel, “but the mystery he made of it, led me to think he might have a double motive. I did not in the least suspect the truth, but thought he was trying to find the missing man from this end, if you understand me?”

===Chinese===
“太可怕了，”梅布尔说，“我还一直以为，英格兰的司法是那样公正。”
“恐怕辛克莱先生说的是实情。”艾勒里说，“我从没沾手过刑事案件，可耳闻的也不算少。请讲下去。”
“我当时就明白了，”辛克莱说，“真正的线索就在我收到的那封信里；而科林斯果然如我推测的那样看过那封信，这一点令人心惊。解开整个局面的关键，在于找到罗纳德·沃森爵士。
“我一直留意着科林斯的行踪，很快就摸清了他往这里跑。我原以为他是为另一件事来的，”他难为情地瞥了梅布尔一眼，“可他把这事弄得神神秘秘，倒让我想到他或许一石二鸟。我丝毫没有疑到真相上头，只当他想从这一头去找那个失踪的人——你们明白我的意思吗？”

===Original===
Allery nodded. “That’s all quite clear,” he said.
“He turned up at my office the other day. I could see he had something in the wind. Even with his iron nerve the delay must have been fretting him, for there was always the possibility that we should get the man, and then the secret, whatever it was, would be out.
“I tried a bluff. I asked him where Wilton-on-Sea was, and said I wanted to go there. All I knew was that it was close to this place. He would not let me go alone, for he did not know how much I knew, and so we came down, and watched for those wretched days which have put years on to my life.

===Chinese===
艾勒里点点头。“都听明白了。”他说。
“前些天他到我办公室来了。我看出他心里藏着事。纵然有钢铁般的神经，这番拖延也必定让他焦灼——因为我们随时可能找到那个人，到那时，无论那秘密是什么，都将真相大白。
“我虚晃了一枪。我问他滨海威尔顿在哪儿，说我想去一趟。我只知道那地方离这里不远。他不肯让我独自去，因为他摸不清我知道多少，于是我们一同下来，苦守了那些该死的日子——那些日子让我平白老了好几岁。

===Original===
“I had to pretend to take to drink to throw dust in his eyes.
“When he found Sir Ronald in such a dramatic fashion, I realised that things had come to a crisis. There was danger, real and personal, in the air. With supreme bluff he introduced me, and I must confess he took me completely by surprise. When I saw Lewis coming, for a moment all my ideas crumpled like a pack of cards, and I thought I had made a colossal fool of myself; but when I realised who it was, I saw the whole game. He would not let him out of his sight, nor allow me a word with him.
“He saw his one chance was to get the document out of him before I had a chance.

===Chinese===
“为了迷他的眼，我只得装出一副贪杯的样子。
“当他以那样戏剧性的方式找到罗纳德爵士时，我明白局面已到了摊牌的关口。空气里弥漫着危险，真实而切身的危险。他使出登峰造极的障眼法把我引见入场——我必须承认，他着实打了我一个措手不及。待我看见刘易斯走来，一时间我整套推断像一副纸牌似的轰然坍塌，我自以为铸成了天大的笑柄；可等我认出来者是谁，我便看穿了整个棋局。他不让他离开自己的视线半步，也不容我同他说上一句话。
“他看清了自己唯一的机会：抢在我有机会之前，把那份文件从他手里弄到手。

===Original===
“But he was one too good for me at the end. When he slipped out I never thought he would make a bolt for it like that.”
He finished his story, and looked at the others.
Allery was scrutinising the end of his cigar. Mabel was deeply distressed.

===Chinese===
“只是到头来，他到底还比我高出一着。他悄悄溜出去的时候，我万万没想到，他竟会那样夺路而逃。”
他讲完了这段经历，看了看其余的人。
艾勒里正细细端详着手里那截雪茄。梅布尔深陷在痛楚之中。

===Original===
“You’ve been working pretty hard lately, I suppose,” said the former.
Sinclair started: “I am always pretty hard at work,” he said.
“You’ve been letting this thing prey on your mind, I expect, a good deal?”
Sinclair stiffened. “Do you mean that I have been imagining things?” he said.

===Chinese===
“你这阵子干得够辛苦的，我想。”艾勒里说。
辛克莱一怔：“我一向干得挺辛苦。”他说。
“我猜，你让这件事在心里纠缠得不轻吧？”
辛克莱绷起了脸。“你是说，这一切全是我的臆想？”他说。

===Original===
“It is a most entertaining exhibition of deduction,” said Allery.
“Of course, I have not had time to tell you all,” said Sinclair, “and it may never come out.”
“It is a wicked lie,” said Mabel. “I will never believe it.” Her eyes were blazing.
“I am sorry, Miss Watson,” stammered Sinclair.
“I am going to lie down, Mr. Allery,” said she, and walked from the room ignoring Sinclair.


===Chinese===
“这可是一场精彩纷呈的推理表演。”艾勒里说。
“当然，我还没来得及把一切都告诉你们，”辛克莱说，“何况真相也许永远不会水落石出。”
“这是恶毒的谎言，”梅布尔说，“我永远不会相信。”她的双眼冒着火。
“对不起，沃森小姐。”辛克莱嗫嚅道。
“我要去躺一躺，艾勒里先生。”她说着走出房间，对辛克莱看也不看一眼。

## XVII. The Wrong Letter / 第十七章 送错的信

===Original===
In the stillness of the night the sound of a car was heard. Sinclair went to the door and waited.
Out of the blackness the car emerged, and came to rest at the door. From the inside issued Sanders. His face was set and grim. Without a word he walked into the house, and into the dining-room. Allery was sitting where he had sat immovable all the evening. Sanders took off his great coat, and took out his revolver and tossed it on the table.
“Let me have a drink,” he said. “I’m done up.”

===Chinese===
夜阑人静，传来一阵汽车的声响。辛克莱走到门口，等候着。
汽车从一片漆黑中钻出来，停在门前。桑德斯从车里跨出。他绷着脸，神色冷峻。他一言不发地走进宅子，走进餐厅。艾勒里还坐在他整晚纹丝不动坐着的那个位置上。桑德斯脱下厚呢大衣，掏出左轮手枪，往桌上一扔。
“给我倒杯酒，”他说，“我累垮了。”

===Original===
“Tell us all about it,” said Sinclair, pouring out a drink and handing it to him.
“Where’s Mabel?” said Sanders to Allery.
“She went to lie down, but said she wanted to be called directly there was any news. I sent for her when I heard the car.”

===Chinese===
“把事情原原本本讲给我们听吧。”辛克莱说着，斟了一杯酒递给他。
“梅布尔呢？”桑德斯问艾勒里。
“她去躺下了，不过说了，一有消息就立刻叫她。我听见汽车声，就打发人去请她了。”

===Original===
Mabel came in looking woefully drawn and pale. She was in negligee costume.
“Well?” she said in a dull voice.
“It’s all over. I chased the car, but we had no chance of catching it. By a sheer fluke at the little village of Paxton, we saw men standing about which seemed strange at this hour, and happened to ask whether anything had happened. It appeared that a car had smashed up at the bend. A bad smash.”

===Chinese===
梅布尔走了进来，面容愁苦憔悴，脸色苍白。她穿着一身家常晨衣。
“怎么样？”她声音发滞地问。
“全完了。我开车追那辆车，可我们压根没有指望追得上。纯属碰巧，在帕克斯顿（Paxton）小村，我们看见几个人影站在四下里——这个钟点显得蹊跷——便顺口问了一句出了什么事。原来是有辆汽车在弯道上撞了个稀烂。撞得惨极了。”

===Original===
“And Mr. Collins?” said the girl.
“I should have thought your first thought would have been for your brother,” said Sanders.
“Let’s have the news,” said Allery, crossly.

===Chinese===
“那科林斯先生呢？”姑娘问。
“我还以为，你头一个挂念的该是你哥哥呢。”桑德斯说。
“有话快讲。”艾勒里焦躁地说。

===Original===
“I am thankful to say that Watson is safe and hardly injured at all. I saw him, and he is coming on as soon as he has made his statement to the Police. Your Inspector came along, and he is bringing Watson back.”
“And the other,” said Allery, almost in a whisper.
“Dead!” said Sanders solemnly.

===Chinese===
“谢天谢地，沃森安然无恙，几乎一点伤都没受。我见到他了，他向警方录完口供就赶过来。你们那位督察也赶到了，正把沃森送回来。”
“那另一个呢。”艾勒里问，声音低得近乎耳语。
“死了！”桑德斯沉痛地说。

===Original===
The silence was broken by a woman’s sobs. Mabel had thrown herself down on the sofa, and was weeping bitterly. Sanders was going to her, but Allery motioned him back. “Let her alone,” he said. “She has had an awful time of it.”
“You will be better in bed,” he said gently to her. “Let me fetch your old nurse.”
The faithful old servant had been hovering about all the evening. She came in and put her arm about the weeping girl, and led her from the room.

===Chinese===
一阵女人的啜泣打破了寂静。梅布尔扑倒在沙发上，哭得撕心裂肺。桑德斯正要朝她走去，艾勒里却抬手把他拦住。“别管她，”他说，“这些日子她受的罪已经够多了。”
“你还是躺到床上去的好。”他柔声对她说，“我去把你的老乳母请来。”
这位忠实的老仆人整晚都在近旁守候徘徊。她走进来，搂住哭泣的姑娘，把她带出了房间。

===Original===
“Here he is,” said Allery, jumping to his feet, as the sound of a car was heard. Watson and the Inspector came in, the former looking very white and shaken.
“A bad smash, sir,” said the Inspector to Sinclair, “they must have been going at a cracking pace. I have a full statement from witnesses.”
“Thank you, Miles,” said Sinclair, handing him a drink. “I will send for you tomorrow, and there will be a report to draw up. The matter is more serious than you know. At present a discreet silence is best, you understand.”

===Chinese===
“他来了。”听见汽车声，艾勒里一跃而起。沃森和督察走了进来，前者面色惨白，惊魂未定。
“撞得很惨，先生。”督察对辛克莱说，“他们那车速肯定快得吓人。目击者的完整口供，我已经拿到手了。”
“辛苦了，迈尔斯。”辛克莱说着，递给他一杯酒，“明天我会派人去找你，有一份报告要起草。这事情比你所知的严重得多。眼下还是缄口不言最为妥当，你明白。”

===Original===
“Very good, sir,” said the other, almost giving a wink, and withdrew. There was an awkward silence in the room.
“Whatever is the meaning of the whole business,” said Watson. “I am all in the dark.”
“Did Collins tell you nothing on the way?” said Sinclair.

===Chinese===
“是，先生。”对方答道，几乎要递个眼色，便退了出去。屋里一时陷入尴尬的沉默。
“这整桩事究竟是怎么回事？”沃森说，“我完全是蒙在鼓里。”
“一路上科林斯什么也没跟你说？”辛克莱问。

===Original===
“No, he came and said that it was absolutely necessary for us to get to London at once. He said the honour of my father’s name was involved, and that the matter must be kept secret, especially from the police. He hinted that there was some secret connected with my father’s past life. He was so insistent that I went blindly with him.”
“You have had a lucky escape,” said Sinclair. “Collins, in my view, was the murderer of your father.”
“What!” said Watson, starting forward in his seat.

===Chinese===
“没有。他来找我说，我们务必即刻赶回伦敦。他说这事关系到我父亲名声的荣誉，必须保密，尤其不能让警察知道。他暗示说，这跟我父亲过去的某段隐秘有关。他催逼得那样紧，我便糊里糊涂地跟他走了。”
“你可真是侥幸脱了身。”辛克莱说，“依我看，科林斯就是杀害你父亲的凶手。”
“什么！”沃森叫了一声，猛地从座位上探身向前。

===Original===
“The others here are sceptical, they cannot believe it. It all hangs on a document which your father said he had hidden. He wrote and told me.” He took the letter from his pocket, and handed it to Watson, who read it with a puzzled air.
“But I don’t understand. He says he hid it where he once put his will in my presence.”
“Exactly.”

===Chinese===
“这里的其他人都将信将疑，他们没法相信。可一切都系于一份文件——你父亲写信告诉过我，说他把它藏了起来。”他从口袋里掏出那封信，递给沃森。沃森满腹狐疑地读了。
“可我不明白。他说他把那东西藏在了他当着我的面放过遗嘱的地方。”
“正是。”

===Original===
“Then why did Collins want to go to London?”
“To get it, I suppose,” said Sinclair.
“But it’s not there at all; it’s here,” said Watson.

===Chinese===
“那科林斯为什么还非要赶去伦敦？”
“想必是去取那东西。”辛克莱说。
“可它根本不在那儿；它在这儿。”沃森说。

===Original===
In breathless silence he rose and walked to the fireplace. Reaching up, he turned the portrait of Sir James from the wall. Behind was the oak panelling. Sliding a panel back he put his hand and drew out some papers, and brought them to the light.
One was a dusty envelope, tied and sealed. The other was new.
Picking up the first, Watson said with some emotion:

===Chinese===
一片屏息的寂静中，他站起身，走向壁炉，抬手把詹姆斯爵士的画像从墙上翻开。画像后面是橡木护壁板。他把一块壁板向旁滑开，伸手进去取出几份文件，拿到亮处。
一只信封蒙着灰尘，系着绳，封着口；另一只是新的。
沃森拿起头一只，颇为动情地说：

===Original===
“When we had our last interview in this room, before I went to South America, my father told me he had made a will cutting me completely out, and had left this with Mr. Allery. Isn’t that so?”
“That is quite correct,” said Allery. “I tried to persuade him not to do so, in spite of the opinion I then had of you, but he would not make any change.”
“Well, he told me here, he had made another dated after that. He said, ‘I am going to hide it here. No one knows of the existence of this place, and I am placing you on your honour. If you come back having redeemed your character, and with a clear conscience, I will bring out this will, if I am alive. If I am dead you can produce it, if you think you have made good.’ He was a strange man and had curious ideas, but he was absolutely just.”

===Chinese===
“我去南美之前，在这间屋子里同父亲最后一次面谈时，他告诉我，他立了一份把我完全排除在外的遗嘱，并已把它交给了艾勒里先生。是这么回事吧？”
“一点不错。”艾勒里说，“尽管我当时对你的为人另有看法，我还是力劝他别这么做，可他执意不肯改动分毫。”
“可他在这儿告诉我，在那之后他又立了一份新的。他说：‘我要把它藏在这里。没有人知道这地方的存在，我就凭你的名誉信托于你。倘若你回来时已经挽回了自己的名声，问心无愧，而我尚在人世，我会把这份遗嘱公之于世。倘若我死了，你若认为自己已经改过自新，便可把它出示于人。’他是个奇特的人，有些古怪的念头，但他绝对公正。”

===Original===
“That accounts for the Will he made some little time ago, bringing you back,” said Allery. “I see it all now.”
“But the other document?” said Sinclair impatiently. “Of course we supposed it was in the London house. I thought he would have it with him, and so did Collins, evidently.”
Watson picked up the envelope, and broke the seal.

===Chinese===
“这就解释了他不久前立的那份遗嘱——把你召回家的那一份。”艾勒里说，“如今我全都明白了。”
“可另一份文件呢？”辛克莱不耐烦地说，“我们当然都以为它在伦敦的宅子里。我以为他会随身带着，科林斯显然也这么以为。”
沃森拿起那只信封，拆开封印。

===Original===
Within was a roll of paper, which he unfolded, and laid on the table.
The feeling in the room was intense.
The light shone full on the writing, and the men leant forward to scan the words. It seemed almost as though the dead man was with them speaking from the tomb his awful accusation.

===Chinese===
里面是一卷纸。他把它展开，平放在桌上。
屋里的气氛紧张到了极点。
灯光正照在那字迹上，几个人都俯身细读。那一刻，几乎仿佛死者就在他们中间，正从坟墓里道出他那可怕的控诉。

===Original===
Watson read in a firm voice.
“To my son.”
My dearest boy,

===Chinese===
沃森用沉稳的声音念了起来。
“致吾儿。”
我最亲爱的孩子：

===Original===
If you ever read this I will have passed forever from your sight. I have longed for you for years, and have bitterly repented the hasty action which drove you from my side, though at the time I thought I was acting for your ultimate good. I pray to God that you may be still alive, and may return to take your place here.
I can write no more as to this as the time is short. A great menace hangs over me, and I feel that my life may be taken at any time. I will be brief.
In my capacity of Home Secretary it was necessary for me to be acquainted with the most dreadful secrets of crime, and criminals.

===Chinese===
你若读到这封信，我便已永远从你的眼前逝去。多年来我无时不在思念你，并为那桩将你从我身边赶走的草率之举痛悔不已——尽管当时，我以为自己是为了你终久的益处。我向上帝祈祷，愿你仍然活在人世，能够回来，承继你在这里的位置。
关于这层，时间紧迫，我不能再多写了。一场大祸正悬在我头顶，我感到自己的性命随时可能不保。长话短说。
身为内政大臣，我必须知悉世间最骇人听闻的罪行与罪犯的秘密。

===Original===
Among the most baffling problems of modern times has been the personality of a master criminal, a blackmailer, forger and rogue, whose diabolical cunning had eluded the police completely. Even his name and residence were entirely unknown, though he had gone under several aliases for blackmailing purposes. He seemed to know every shady secret in Society.
“Webb or Atkins,” said Sinclair excitedly. “The man we have been trying to find for years.”
“Go on,” said Allery.

===Chinese===
当今时代最令人束手无策的难题之一，便是一名犯罪大师的真身——此人是个勒索犯、伪造犯兼无赖，其魔鬼般的狡诈令警方完全无从追缉。连他的姓名和住址都无人知晓，尽管为了行勒索之事，他曾用过好几个化名。上流社会的每一桩阴暗隐私，他似乎都了如指掌。
“韦布（Webb），或者阿特金斯（Atkins）。”辛克莱激动地说。“我们找了他好些年都没找着的人。”
“往下念。”艾勒里说。

===Original===
Some few days ago I received a letter from Sylvester Collins, the Investigator, who was actually trying to find this criminal. Several of the highest in the land had been asking me to locate this man, as their lives were being made a misery by him. Collins promised to send a report on the matter. When I opened the letter he sent me, the whole world seemed to go round, and for minutes I could not collect my thoughts. Instead of the letter I had expected, there was a typewritten document to the Duke of —﻿—﻿— demanding instant payment of a very large sum, and in the case of refusal a threat that incriminating documents of the worst character would be sent to the Duchess.
It was on perfectly plain paper, with no address, but giving careful instructions as to how the money was to be paid in Paris.
Even then I did not grasp the full meaning of what I read. The note had come by hand, straight from Collins but within a few moments I received a letter by hand from the Duke, containing the note I was expecting from Collins, which, he said, had evidently been sent to him in error. There could be only one explanation, however improbable it appeared. The two letters had come from the same person.

===Chinese===
数日之前，我收到侦探西尔维斯特·科林斯的一封来信，他当时正着手缉查这名罪犯。国内几位身份最尊贵的人物一直在催我查出此人的下落，因为他把他们的日子搅成了苦海。科林斯允诺就此案呈我一份报告。可等我拆开他寄来的信，只觉天旋地转，好几分钟都收不拢心神。我等着的那封信并不在里面，取而代之的，是一份打字机打出的文件，写给某公爵，勒令即刻支付一笔巨款，并威胁说若敢拒付，便将性质最恶劣的罪证文件寄给公爵夫人。
信纸素净无饰，不留地址，却把款项该当如何在巴黎交付，交代得细致入微。
即便在那时，我也尚未领悟所读内容的全部含义。那封短简是专人送来的，径直出自科林斯之手；可没过几分钟，我又收到公爵专人送来的一封信，里面装的正是我盼着科林斯给我的那封短简——公爵说，那显然是投递错了，寄到了他那里。解释只可能有一个，无论它显得多么不可能：两封信出自同一个人之手。

===Original===
I went at once to the Duke, and in strict confidence told him of what had happened. He told me the whole dreadful story.
This man Webb﻿—Julian Webb﻿—had been draining him like a cask.
Of course, I could have called in the police, but such terrible issues were at stake, and the scandal would have been so profound, that I chose the other course.

===Chinese===
我即刻去见公爵，把发生的一切绝密地告诉了他。他也把那件可怕的事和盘托出，讲给了我。
这个韦布——朱利安·韦布（Julian Webb）——把他当酒桶似的榨了又榨。
当然，我本可以把警察召来，可牵涉的干系太过可怕，丑闻又会深不见底，于是我选择了另一条路。

===Original===
I knew the risk and counted the cost. I sent for Collins, and faced him with the letter, and the situation. He tried to bluff, and of course denied the whole thing. I told him quite plainly that if I put the matter into the hands of the police, as it was my duty to do, it would mean disgrace and a long term of penal servitude for him; but that I was willing to save a scandal, to let him leave the country after he had handed over the incriminating documents. I informed him that when he had gone I should place the whole thing in the hands of Scotland Yard. He asked me for a week to consider the matter, and make his arrangements.
He is coming to see me tomorrow in Town, and has agreed to bring the documents with him.
I feel that, to such a man, the disgrace and flight will not appeal, and my life is now in grave danger. I have promised, perhaps somewhat rashly, not to reveal anything till I have seen him, so I can take no steps, but I am writing this and hiding it where you only will know, and if you come back, you will know what has happened to your father.

===Chinese===
我明知风险，也掂量过代价。我派人召来科林斯，把那封信和整个局面摊在他面前。他先想虚张声势，自然把事情一概否认。我明白无误地告诉他，倘若我按职责所在将此事交付警察，等着他的便是身败名裂和长年的苦役；但我愿意压下这场丑闻，等他交出那些罪证文件，便任他离境出走。我还知会他，等他一走，我便把整件事呈交苏格兰场。他向我求了一周期限，好考虑此事，料理后路。
他明天就要到伦敦来见我，并且已经答应把文件随身带来。
我料想，对这样一个人，身败名裂与亡命出逃是劝不动的，我的性命眼下危在旦夕。我已许下诺言——或许有些轻率——在见到他之前不吐露一字，故而我无法采取任何行动。但我写下这封信，把它藏在只有你会知道的地方；你若回来，便会知道你的父亲遭遇了什么。

===Original===
If all is well, I shall myself destroy this when this danger is past.
I can write no more. If you should ever read this you will know how to act.
Goodbye my boy, and think kindly of your father, who treated you harshly, but has always loved you.

===Chinese===
倘若一切平安，待这场危险过去，我会亲手把此信毁去。
我不能再写下去了。你若果真读到这封信，自会知道该如何行事。
别了，我的孩子。愿你宽厚地记挂你的父亲——他待你严苛，却始终爱着你。

===Original===
Look after Mabel. If I am dead it is my wish that she marry Eric, who is a good boy. I have been very selfish in this matter.
James Watson.
Well, here it was at last.

===Chinese===
照看好梅布尔。我若死了，愿她嫁给埃里克——他是个好孩子。在这件事上，我一直太自私了。
詹姆斯·沃森。
好了，它终于就在眼前了。

===Original===
Every one of the listeners remained lost in his own thoughts.
Ronald buried his face in his hands.
Allery broke the silence. Leaning across to Sinclair, he took his hand, and said, “I apologise. You were right and we were wrong, but who would have suspected it?”

===Chinese===
在场的听者个个沉浸在各自的思绪之中。
罗纳德把脸埋进了双手。
艾勒里打破了沉默。他俯身过去，握住辛克莱的手，说：“我向你道歉。你是对的，我们错了。可谁能疑心到这一步呢？”

===Original===
“Please don’t apologise, Mr. Allery,” said Sinclair. “It was a terrible shock to me, and as I told you, it was only last evening that I could make up my mind.
“I think everything is clear now. Collins had no intention of going abroad. He got round Sir James to give him that week to mature his plans for as cunning and clever a murder as was ever planned.
“The visit in the afternoon, with all its precautions, was absolutely necessary, so that he could drug him, though how he managed to get him to take a whiskey and soda I cannot think. Probably he promised to agree with everything, otherwise Sir James would have immediately sent to Scotland Yard. Sir James’ promise then held good, and Collins would say he would bring all the necessary documents. After he had gone, some misgiving must have come to Sir James. Perhaps he was feeling drowsy, and suspicion of evil was growing in his mind.

===Chinese===
“请别道歉，艾勒里先生。”辛克莱说，“这对我也是个可怕的打击。而且正如我告诉过你的，直到昨天晚上，我才总算下定了决心。
“我想如今一切都清楚了。科林斯压根没打算出国。他哄得詹姆斯爵士给了他那一周期限，好从容酝酿一桩谋杀计划——其狡诈周密，堪称旷世仅见。
“下午那次造访，连同那一番小心谨慎，都是万万省不得的——为的是便于下药。至于他怎么设法让詹姆斯爵士喝下那杯威士忌苏打，我实在想不明白。多半他满口应承事事依从，否则詹姆斯爵士会立刻派人去苏格兰场。这样一来，詹姆斯爵士的诺言仍然作数，科林斯便说他会把一切必要的文件带来。他走了之后，詹姆斯爵士心里必定生出了某种不安。也许他正觉昏昏欲睡，对祸事的疑惧已在他心底渐渐滋长。

===Original===
“Anyway he wrote that letter, which did not break his promise, as you see.”
“There is a possibility, which is like the man’s daring,” said Allery.
“Collins may have gambled on Sir James taking a whiskey after he had gone. There are tasteless sleeping drugs which could have been put in the glass. You remember the room was in partial darkness.”

===Chinese===
“不管怎样，他写了那封信，而如你们所见，那封信并未违背他的诺言。”
“有一种可能，倒颇合此人的胆大包天。”艾勒里说。
“科林斯也许就赌着詹姆斯爵士在他走后会喝上一杯威士忌。有些安眠药无色无味，可以预先放进杯子里。你们记得，那房间当时半明半暗。”

===Original===
“I will go and break it to Mabel. She must know the truth. It is better that I should tell her,” and he glanced at Sanders.
Without another word he went out.
“What are you going to do about the whole thing?” said Ronald, turning to Sinclair.

===Chinese===
“我去向梅布尔挑明这件事。她必须知道真相。由我来告诉她更好。”说着，他朝桑德斯望了一眼。
他再没多说一个字，走了出去。
“这整桩事情，你打算怎么处置？”罗纳德转向辛克莱问道。

===Original===
“I shall have to lay the matter before Boyce, and of course Jackson must be exonerated, but I hope that nothing will be done. It would rake up the whole scandal which Sir James gave his life to hide, and the man is beyond the reach of the Law. I see no point in a sensational disclosure.”
“I am glad,” said Watson. “I would rather have it so.”
Mabel came into the room with Allery. She held her head proudly, and was dry-eyed, though very white. She walked straight to Sanders.
“Eric,” she said. “I have had a terrible lesson, and I do not suppose I shall ever forget it. I have misjudged you, and you were right in your opinion. If you wish our engagement to be announced, I am willing. And please forgive me for my temporary madness. I give you my word it was nothing more. I can never forgive myself.”
Allery exchanged glances with the others, who quietly rose, and slipped from the room.

===Chinese===
“我将不得不把此事呈报博伊斯，杰克逊当然必须洗清罪名，但我希望此事就到此为止。否则，便会把詹姆斯爵士以性命换取掩盖的整桩丑闻重新翻出来，而那个人早已非人间法律所能制裁。我看不出将此事耸人听闻地公之于众有什么意义。”
“我很高兴如此。”沃森说，“我宁愿就这样了结。”
梅布尔随艾勒里走进房间。她高昂着头，眼里已没有泪水，尽管脸色仍旧惨白。她径直走到桑德斯面前。
“埃里克，”她说，“我得到了一个惨痛的教训，我想我一辈子也不会忘记。我看错了你，而你的看法是对的。如果你希望宣布我们的婚约，我愿意。也请你原谅我一时的心神迷乱——我向你担保，仅止于此。我永远不能原谅我自己。”
艾勒里与旁人交换了个眼色，众人悄然起身，悄悄退出了房间。

## Preface / 序言


===Original===
I can say with all sincerity, nay with all solemn responsibility, that this detective mystery deceived me. And as I have been looking out for a long time for a detective mystery that should be at least deceptive, whatever its other merits or demerits in being detective, I very willingly write a word to serve as a preface to it, though such books ought not to need such prefaces. The detective story is in this way a paradox (if I may use a word that has very painful memories for me) because the true reader and critic not only desires to be gulled, but even desires to be gullible. I wish when reading such a story to become as simple as Dr. Watson; to be in the happy, cheerful, childlike, radiant condition of Dr. Watson and not in the much more dark and disillusioned and satiated and sceptical condition of Sherlock Holmes. I generally am in that childlike condition. But in every case it is my ardent and aspiring ambition to be stupider than the man who wrote the story. And in the case of this story I actually succeeded.

This desire to be deceived is really peculiar to detective romance. It is in another sense that we say the same thing of other types of romance. It is sometimes said that when we go to the theatre we pay to be deceived. But we are not really deceived; we do not think that the dramatist intends something that he does not intend; we do not think the actor is doing something that he is not doing. We only forget, or half forget, for a moment, in the continuity and consistency of certain events, the fact that they come from a dramatist and an actor. But if we happen to remember it, we do not remember it with surprise. We are not astonished to discover that there is an actor on the stage, as we are (or ought to be) astonished to discover that there is a corpse in the summerhouse. We do not feel a momentary incredulity when we are told that the play was written by a playwright, as we do feel (or ought to feel) when we are told that the crime was committed by a curate. We watch a great actor performing Hamlet so well that (if we have luck) we lose for an instant the sense that he is a great actor; we feel for the moment that he is young Hamlet trying to avenge the death of old Hamlet upon Claudius. But we do not, either in forgetting or remembering, feel any shock of fact or the change of fact. We do not feel as we should feel if the play took a new and sudden turn, and we found that Hamlet had killed his own father and that his uncle was a perfectly blameless character. That would be the Detective Drama of Hamlet, Prince of Denmark, and now that so many peculiar experiments are being tried with that tragedy, I respectfully suggest it to the managers of the London Theatres.

If it is the first rule of the writer of mystery stories to conceal the secret from the reader, it is the first duty of the critic to conceal it from the public. I will therefore put my hand upon my mouth; and tortures shall not reveal the precise point in this story at which a person whom I had really regarded as figuring in one legitimate capacity suddenly began to figure in another, which was far from legitimate. I must not breathe a word about what the writer of this dramatic mystery does. I will confine myself strictly to saying what he does not do. And merely out of the things which he does not do, I could construct an enthusiastic eulogy. On the firm foundation of the things he does not do, I could erect an eternal tower of brass. For the things he does not do are the things being done everywhere today, to the destruction of true detective fiction and the loss of this legitimate and delightful form of art. He does not introduce into the story a vast but invisible secret society with branches in every part of the world, with ruffians who can be brought in to do anything or underground cellars that can be used to hide anybody. He does not mar the pure and lovely outlines of a classical murder or burglary by wreathing it round and round with the dirty and dingy red tape of international diplomacy; he does not lower our lofty ideals of crime to the level of foreign politics. He does not introduce suddenly at the end somebody’s brother from New Zealand, who is exactly like him. He does not trace the crime hurriedly in the last page or two to some totally insignificant character, whom we never suspected because we never remembered. He does not get over the difficulty of choosing between the hero and the villain by falling back on the hero’s cabman or the villain’s valet. He does not introduce a professional criminal to take the blame of a private crime; a thoroughly unsportsmanlike course of action and another proof of how professionalism is ruining our national sense of sport. He does not introduce about six people in succession to do little bits of the same small murder; one man to bring the dagger and another to point it and another to stick it in properly. He does not say it was all a mistake, and that nobody ever meant to murder anybody at all, to the serious disappointment of all humane and sympathetic readers. He does not make the general mistake of thinking that the more complicated the story is the better. His story is complicated enough, and on many points open to criticism; but the secret of it is found in the centre; and that is the central matter in any work of art.

G. K. Chesterton.

March, 1926.

===Chinese===
我可以万分诚恳地——不，以全部郑重的责任——说：这部侦探疑案把我骗过了。多年来我一直在寻觅一部至少骗得过人的侦探疑案——且不管它作为侦探小说在别的方面有多少优点或缺点——所以我十分乐意写几句话为它作序，尽管这类书本不该需要这样的序言。侦探小说正是在这个意义上构成一个悖论（请原谅我用了这个给我带来惨痛回忆的字眼）：因为真正的读者和批评家不但渴望被骗，甚至渴望自己好骗。读这样的故事时，我希望自己变得同华生医生（Dr. Watson）一样单纯；处于华生医生那种快活、开朗、天真、明朗的境地，而不是歇洛克·福尔摩斯（Sherlock Holmes）那阴暗得多、幻灭、餍足而多疑的境地。我通常就处于那种天真的境地。但无论读哪一部，我热烈而恳切的雄心，都是要比写出这个故事的人更笨。而在这一部故事上，我当真做到了。

这种受骗的愿望，实在是侦探传奇独有的。我们说别类传奇也有同样情形时，用的是另一个意思。人们有时说，我们进戏院是花钱买骗。可我们并没有真受骗：我们不会以为剧作家另怀着他并未怀有的意图，也不会以为演员在做着他并没有做的事。我们只是在某些事件环环相扣、前后一致之际，一时忘记、或半忘记了它们出自一位剧作家和一位演员之手。而即便碰巧想起来，我们也不带惊讶地想起。我们发现台上有个演员并不会大吃一惊；而发现凉亭里有具尸体，我们（或理应）会大吃一惊。听人说这出戏出自某位剧作家之手，我们不会有一时的难以置信；而听人说这桩罪案是某位副牧师犯下的，我们却会（或理应）一时难以置信。我们看一位伟大的演员演哈姆雷特（Hamlet）演得那样好，（运气好的话）会有一瞬忘了他是个伟大的演员，一时觉得他就是年轻的哈姆雷特，正要找克劳狄斯（Claudius）报老哈姆雷特被害之仇。但无论忘记还是记起，我们都感不到任何事实的冲击、事实的改换。倘若这出戏陡然生出一个新的转折，我们发现哈姆雷特杀的竟是自己的父亲、而他的叔父是个全然无辜的角色，我们本该会有的那种感受，此刻我们是没有的。那将是《丹麦王子哈姆雷特》的侦探剧；如今人们对那出悲剧正试做着种种奇特的实验，我谨将此议恭呈伦敦各家剧院的经理。

倘若神秘小说作家的头一条守则是向读者隐瞒秘密，那么批评家的头一样职责便是替公众隐瞒它。因此我唯有以手掩口；纵使严刑拷打，也休想叫我透露这个故事里那个确切之处：在那里，一个我原本真心当作以正当身份出场的人物，忽然开始以另一种远非正当的身份登场。这位戏剧性疑案的作者做了什么，我一个字也不能吐露。我要严格限于只说他没有做什么。而单凭他没有做的那些事，我就能写出一篇热情洋溢的颂词。在他所不为之事的坚实根基上，我能竖起一座永恒的青铜塔。因为他没有做的事，正是今日天下处处在做的事，而这些事正在毁灭真正的侦探小说，断送这门正当而可喜的艺术。他没有往故事里塞进一个庞大而无形的秘密社团——分支机构遍布世界各地，豢养着随时可以招来干任何勾当的暴徒，备有可以窝藏任何人的地下密室。他没有拿国际外交那又脏又暗的繁文缛节，一圈又一圈地缠绕一桩古典式谋杀或盗窃案的纯净可爱的轮廓，把它糟蹋；也没有把我们关于犯罪的崇高理想贬低到外国政治的水平。他没有在结尾突然冒出某人那位来自新西兰（New Zealand）、与他长得一模一样的兄弟。他没有在最后一两页里匆匆把罪行归到某个全然无足轻重的角色头上——我们之所以从未疑心过此人，是因为我们从未记得有过此人。他没有在主人公与恶棍之间难以取舍时，靠搬出主人公的马车夫或恶棍的贴身男仆来了局。他没有引进一名职业罪犯来替一桩私人罪案顶罪——那是一种彻头彻尾有失运动家风度的做法，也再一度证明职业化正在如何败坏我们民族的体育精神。他没有让大约六个人接连登场，来分做同一桩小小谋杀的零碎活计：一个递匕首，一个校准方向，再一个负责把它稳稳扎进去。他没有到最后宣布这一切全是一场误会、根本没有人存心谋杀任何人，以致一切仁厚而富于同情心的读者大失所望。他没有犯那种流行的通病，以为故事越复杂就越好。他的故事已经够复杂了，许多地方也尽可商榷；但谜底是在故事的正中心寻得的；而这正是一切艺术作品里居中的要义。

G. K. 切斯特顿。

1926年3月。
